# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## What this project is

meshio++ (Python package/import name `meshioplusplus`) is a Python library for reading and writing many mesh file formats used in scientific computing and FEM (Finite Element Method). It provides a unified `Mesh` data structure that all format readers/writers convert to and from.

This branch (`port-to-c++`) adds a **C++ core** (`meshioplusplus._core`, pybind11) built with **scikit-build-core + CMake**, replacing the old setuptools build. Most formats read/write through the C++ core with a pure-Python fallback.

## Keeping docs in sync (required)

**Every time a feature or format is added or changed, update all three of `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md`, and the `doc/` site in the same change.** At minimum: the format table and format-specific notes in [`doc/formats.md`](doc/formats.md) (plus any other relevant `doc/` page), the supported-formats list in `README.md`, and the architecture/"Adding a new format" guidance here. Changes to the shared format registry or the flat-binding surfaces must also be reflected in [`doc/wasm.md`](doc/wasm.md), [`doc/c_api.md`](doc/c_api.md), [`doc/fortran.md`](doc/fortran.md), [`doc/julia.md`](doc/julia.md), and [`doc/r.md`](doc/r.md). A feature is not "done" until CLAUDE.md, README.md, and the docs all reflect it.

**Keep the MCP server in sync with the Python API (required).** Every change to the Python public API surface — a new or changed operation, a changed signature or default, a new report field, a new format-affecting capability — must update the MCP server (`src/python/meshioplusplus/mcp/`: the pure tool in `_tools.py` **and** its typed FastMCP registration in `_server.py`), its tests (`tests/python/test_mcp.py`, including the `_NOT_TOOLS` parity table), and the tool table in [`doc/mcp.md`](doc/mcp.md) **in the same change**. This is enforced, not just requested: the parity-guard test (`test_every_operation_has_a_tool`) fails CI whenever a new `meshioplusplus.__all__` operation is claimed by no tool's `wraps` and has no explicit `_NOT_TOOLS` exemption — add the tool, not the exemption, unless the API is genuinely not path-expressible (the in-memory interop `to_*`/`from_*`, the GPU handoff, the interactive `view`). Like the docs rule above, a feature is not "done" until the MCP surface reflects it.

**Keep [`doc/roadmap.md`](doc/roadmap.md) in sync with what actually ships (required).** It lists what is *not* built, grouped by theme with an effort estimate; every time a change implements — fully or partially — an item listed there, update that item's entry in the same change: mark it done and remove it (or fold it into "Suggested sequencing" as complete) if it is now fully shipped, or narrow the bullet's wording to describe what remains if only part of it landed. Do not leave a shipped capability listed as a gap — the document's entire value is being an accurate "not built" list, and a stale entry actively misleads about where effort is still needed. Not every change touches the roadmap; most don't. This only applies when a change closes or measurably advances one of its listed gaps.

**Keep the example notebooks (`example/python/*.ipynb`) current with new features too.** When a user-facing capability is added (a new operation, a new format, a notable API), demonstrate it in the notebooks (a new/updated cell) and re-execute them with outputs (`PYVISTA_OFF_SCREEN=true jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute --inplace example/python/*.ipynb`) so the committed outputs stay in sync. **Notebook demos must be graphical, not just printed values** — render results with PyVista (off-screen: `pv.OFF_SCREEN=True` + a `pv.Plotter(off_screen=True).screenshot(return_img=True)` embedded via matplotlib, with a matplotlib fallback for head-less GL), coloring the mesh by the relevant data array (quality metric, per-point diff error, ...) so the effect is visually understandable. `example/python/03_mesh_operations.ipynb` is the reference pattern for the operations. Mirror notable additions into the C++ counterpart too (`example/cpp/03_mesh_operations.ipynb`) where practical — see the entry below.

**No hard-wrapped prose in `doc/` markdown (required).** Every `doc/**/*.md` file (this repo's VitePress site) is unwrapped: a newline appears only at an actual paragraph/block boundary — between paragraphs, before/after a heading, list item, table row, blockquote, or fenced code block — never as a mid-paragraph line break. A prose paragraph is exactly one line, however long. This applies only to `doc/`; `CLAUDE.md` itself already follows this (one line per bullet/paragraph), and ordinary prose elsewhere (`README.md`, code comments, commit messages) is unaffected unless asked. When editing or adding a `doc/` page, write new paragraphs as a single line and don't reflow existing ones at a column width — an editor or formatter set to hard-wrap at ~80/100 columns must be disabled for this directory. Structural elements stay one-per-line as normal: each list item, table row, and heading is still its own line; only wrapping *within* a paragraph or list item is disallowed. Fenced code blocks are exempt (their contents are literal, not prose).

## Version bumps (required)

**"Bump the version" always means every one of these **ten** files, together, to the same value — never just one or two. They are, in order: `pyproject.toml`, `CMakeLists.txt`, `conanfile.py`, `packages/vcpkg/meshioplusplus/vcpkg.json`, `src/wasm/package.json`, `src/viewer/package-lock.json`, `bindings/julia/MeshioPlusPlus/Project.toml`, `bindings/r/meshioplusplus/DESCRIPTION`, plus the two **compile-time version headers** added in v9.6.0 — `src/cpp/include/meshioplusplus/version.hpp` (`MESHIOPLUSPLUS_VERSION_MAJOR/MINOR/PATCH` **and** the `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_VERSION_STRING` literal) and `bindings/c/include/meshioplusplus/meshioplusplus.h` (`MIO_VERSION_MAJOR/MINOR/PATCH`). Those last two are the *only* entries on this list that gate themselves: `CMakeLists.txt` parses both and `FATAL_ERROR`s at configure time if either disagrees with `project(... VERSION ...)`, and a `static_assert` in `bindings/c/c_api.cpp` pins the C macros to the C++ ones — so forgetting them is a build failure rather than a wrong answer. They are hand-written rather than `configure_file`d for the same reason `abi_version.hpp` is: a consumer that never runs CMake (the single-header amalgamation, pkg-config, a hand-written makefile) must still be able to read them:**

- [`pyproject.toml`](pyproject.toml) — `version`
- [`CMakeLists.txt`](CMakeLists.txt) — `project(... VERSION ...)`
- [`conanfile.py`](conanfile.py) — `version`
- [`packages/vcpkg/meshioplusplus/vcpkg.json`](packages/vcpkg/meshioplusplus/vcpkg.json) — `version`
- [`src/wasm/package.json`](src/wasm/package.json) — `version` (npm publish uses this file verbatim; it silently lagged behind at 6.3.0 through two releases before this rule existed — it's the one that's easiest to forget since it lives outside the Python/CMake build)
- [`src/viewer/package-lock.json`](src/viewer/package-lock.json) — the `@meshioplusplus/wasm` `version`. The viewer consumes it as a `file:` dependency, so `npm ci` **hard-fails** the moment the lock disagrees with `src/wasm/package.json` — which breaks the `viewer-web` job in `ci.yml` and the Pages build in `docs.yml`. It was already stale at 7.10.0 against a 7.14.0 package when this entry was first written, so treat it as the sixth version file even though nothing else reads it. **Regenerate it with `npm install` run under npm ≥10** (`lockfileVersion: 3`) — do NOT hand-edit: npm 10 splits a `file:` dependency across *two* entries (a top-level `"../wasm"` entry carrying the real `name`/`version`/`license`/`engines`, and a lightweight `node_modules/@meshioplusplus/wasm` entry that is now just `{"resolved": "../wasm", "link": true}`, no version field at all), and a lockfile edited under the old single-entry shape (or under npm 9, which accepts and re-emits that older shape without complaint) silently passes `npm ci` on an npm 9 machine while still hard-failing on the npm ≥10 CI runners with `Missing: @meshioplusplus/wasm@X from lock file` — bit us exactly once, diagnosed only by reproducing with Node 20/22's bundled npm rather than a possibly-older local one. `npm install` (matching npm version) touches only the `@meshioplusplus/wasm`-related lines; nothing else in the lock shifts.
- [`bindings/julia/MeshioPlusPlus/Project.toml`](bindings/julia/MeshioPlusPlus/Project.toml) — `version` (the Julia package manifest, added in v8.3.0 — Pkg reads it verbatim, and like `src/wasm/package.json` it lives entirely outside the Python/CMake build with nothing in CI cross-checking it, so it is exactly the kind of manifest that silently drifts)
- [`bindings/r/meshioplusplus/DESCRIPTION`](bindings/r/meshioplusplus/DESCRIPTION) — `Version:` (the R package manifest, added in v8.3.0 — `R CMD build`/`install` stamp it into the installed package; same outside-the-build, nothing-cross-checks-it drift risk as the Julia manifest. Note the field is `Version:` with a capital V and a colon, DESCRIPTION's DCF syntax, not TOML)

The CMake `VERSION` feeds `mio_version()`, the C API `.so`'s VERSION/SOVERSION, and the installed package configs, so it isn't optional/cosmetic.

**Two docs also print the version verbatim and are CI-gated**: [`doc/cpp_api.md`](doc/cpp_api.md) and [`README.md`](README.md) each carry a `find_package(meshioplusplus X.Y.Z EXACT CONFIG REQUIRED COMPONENTS CXX)` line, and `ci.yml`'s `cpp-install` job `grep -qF`s both against `pkg-config --modversion` of the freshly installed prefix — so a bump that updates the eight files above but not those two lines turns that job red (deliberately; it is what keeps the documented pin from rotting back into a form that cannot work — see the `EXACT` note in the *Installable C++ API* entry below).

**`tests/python/test_io_baseline.py`'s four `BASELINE_HASHES` also move with every bump**, and are the one item on this list nothing gates: they are whole-file SHA-256s of VTU/VTP writes, and every writer emits a provenance comment carrying the release version (`Written by meshio++ v<version>`), so a bump changes all four. They were recorded at v10.19.0 and silently went stale for three releases (v10.20.0/.1/.2) before anyone noticed — the same drift `src/wasm/package.json` and `src/viewer/package-lock.json` are called out for above. Refresh them in the same change; before doing so, confirm the *only* difference is the version string (substitute the old version into the new bytes and re-hash), or a real regression is being papered over.

**A dated [`CHANGELOG.md`](CHANGELOG.md) entry is required in the same change as every version bump** — new formats, new operations, notable enhancements *and* breaking changes, not breaking changes only. Mark breaking items explicitly with a **Breaking:** prefix; everything else is assumed additive. (This rule replaced a breaking-changes-only policy under which the file silently fell six releases behind, from v6.6.0 to v7.1.0 — the entries were reconstructed from git history. Treat "bump the eight version files" and "add the CHANGELOG entry" as one indivisible step.) A pure patch/bugfix bump can be a single line.

## Commands

**Build/install for development** (native paths on). PEP 668 systems: use the in-repo uv venv (`uv venv --python 3.12 .venv`), then:
```bash
CMAKE_ARGS="-DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_WITH_HDF5=ON -DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_WITH_NETCDF=ON -DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_WITH_ZLIB=ON" \
  uv pip install --python .venv --no-build-isolation -e .
```
Plain environments: `pip install -e ".[all]"`. The optional deps (HDF5, netCDF, zlib) are auto-detected; when absent the C++ paths compile out and Python fallbacks (h5py/netCDF4/stdlib zlib) take over.

**Standalone C++ build**: `build/configure.sh` (Linux/macOS) and `build/configure.bat` (Windows) configure a CMake tree next to themselves (`build/cpp-<type>[-<mesh-backend>]`); flags: `--backend SEQ|STL|OPENMP|TBB`, `--mesh-backend MESHIO|NATIVE|KRATOS` (non-MESHIO implies `-DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_BUILD_PYTHON=OFF`), `--tests`, `--cli` (the native CLI binary, below), `--c-api`/`--fortran` (the C API entry below), `--install-cpp`/`--cpp-backends <LIST>` (the installable C++ API entry below; the list is comma-or-semicolon separated, and on Windows a multi-entry list must be quoted — cmd splits unquoted batch parameters on both `,` and `;`), `--build`, `--with/-out-hdf5/netcdf/zlib`, `--tbb-dir <path>`. They print the matching `CMAKE_ARGS … pip install` line (MESHIO only) and, when anything installable was enabled, the matching `cmake --install` line.

**Native CLI binary** (`src/cpp/cli/main.cpp` → `meshioplusplus_cli`, installed executable name `meshioplusplus`): a **Python-free** command-line tool over `meshioplusplus_core_obj` mirroring the Python console script's verbs (convert/info/ascii/binary/compress/decompress/quality/extract-surface/reorder/diff/merge/transform/clean/crop/slice/isosurface/voxelize/split/convert-cells/refine/partition/smooth/interpolate/stats/view/screenshot, plus the nested `data <verb>` group with the same nine *operation* sub-verbs and the same colon rules — `data export` is Python-only, see the gaps below). Supporting the repeated `--point`/`--cell`/`--field` flags the `data` verbs need required one extension to the hand-rolled parser: `cli_opt_spec` gained nothing, but `cli_parsed` gained a `multi` map recording every occurrence of each value-option, populated **alongside** the existing last-wins `values` map so all 16 pre-existing verbs are behaviourally unchanged; read it with `opt_values(p, name)` and test presence with `has_opt(p, name)`. `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_BUILD_CLI` (OFF by default; `build/configure.sh --cli --build`). Reuses the shared `registry.hpp` for format dispatch (with the `sniff_format` read fallback); ASCII/binary/compress variants call the per-format writer free functions directly (the registry bakes one default per format), and the operation verbs use `operations/*.hpp`. Works under any mesh/parallel backend and optional-dep set. **Documented gaps vs the Python CLI**: `convert -s/-d` never reaches the C++ core and errors (named **sets** no longer belong on this list — `info` prints point/cell/side sets and `diff` compares regions, since v8.1.0); formats whose C++ reader raises (e.g. gmsh `$Periodic`, which the Python path handles via fallback) are unreadable here — there is no Python fallback (gmsh 4.1 `$Entities` was the headline case until v9.7.0); **`data export`** has no counterpart at all (it writes Parquet through pyarrow, a Python library — see the interoperability entry below); and `view`/`screenshot` exist only in a **Polyscope-enabled** build (`-DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_WITH_POLYSCOPE=ON`, OFF by default and never in the release binaries — see the viewer entry below), otherwise they are listed but throw naming the flag. `--version`'s `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_CLI_VERSION` is fed by CMake `PROJECT_VERSION`. **Prebuilt, statically-linked release binaries** (Linux x86_64, Linux arm64, macOS universal, Windows x86_64) are attached to every `v*` tag's GitHub Release by `cli.yml` (see "CI" below) — built with `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_STATIC_RUNTIME=ON` (hoisted to a top-level option so it applies to both `_core` and `meshioplusplus_cli`: static `libgcc`/`libstdc++` on GNU, static MSVC CRT via `MSVC_RUNTIME_LIBRARY` on Windows) and `WITH_HDF5/NETCDF/ZLIB=OFF` + `PARALLEL_BACKEND=SEQ` for a single dependency-free executable; build from source with `--cli` (optionally without `--without-hdf5` etc.) for the full native format/threading feature set.

**WebAssembly build** (`@meshioplusplus/wasm` npm package): `build/configure-wasm.sh --build` (needs the [Emscripten SDK](https://emscripten.org/docs/getting_started/downloads.html) on `PATH`) builds **two artifacts** (v8.8.0) in separate trees, both `-DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_BUILD_PYTHON=OFF -DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_BUILD_WASM=ON -DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_MESH_BACKEND=NATIVE` (the fastest mesh backend; the JS API shape is unchanged and `meshBackend()` reports `"native"`) and HDF5/netCDF **on** (v8.0.0), differing only in the parallel backend: the **sequential** `meshioplusplus_wasm.{mjs,wasm}` (`build/wasm-<type>`, `PARALLEL_BACKEND=SEQ`) and the **threaded** `meshioplusplus_wasm_mt.{mjs,wasm}` (`build/wasm-<type>-mt`, `PARALLEL_BACKEND=OPENMP -DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_WASM_THREADS=ON`). `--seq-only` skips the threaded one; `--build` copies all four files into `src/wasm/dist/`. **Threaded = OpenMP over Wasm threads (pthreads/`SharedArrayBuffer`)**, so it is faster for the mesh operations but only loads where the page is **cross-origin isolated** (COOP `same-origin` + COEP `require-corp`) — it aborts on instantiation otherwise, with no in-artifact fallback, which is why both ship. The loader `loadMeshioPlusPlus(overrides, {variant})` (`src/wasm/src/index.mjs`) **auto-selects** via `globalThis.crossOriginIsolated` (undefined under Node ⇒ threaded, since `worker_threads` always work there; else true ⇒ threaded, false ⇒ sequential); `variant: 'mt'|'seq'` forces one. `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_WASM_THREADS` (CMake, EMSCRIPTEN-only) drives `-fopenmp -pthread` PUBLIC on `meshioplusplus_core_obj`, the `_mt` output name, and `-sPTHREAD_POOL_SIZE=globalThis.navigator?navigator.hardwareConcurrency:8` (`navigator` is Node ≥ 21 only, and the package's floor is Node 18 — the guard + `PTHREAD_POOL_SIZE_STRICT=0` on-demand growth is load-bearing; the whole expression is written without spaces/quotes so it survives CMake → Ninja → em++ as one arg). The `-pthread` flags never leak into the Python/gtest/C-API trees (those configure SEQ/native with the option OFF). A `parallelBackend()` JS/embind binding (`parallel_backend_js` in `js_bindings.cpp`, forwarded in `index.mjs`) reports `"openmp"`/`"seq"` so the smoke test can assert which artifact loaded. The **browser viewer** picks the threaded build wherever it can: the Pages demo vendors `src/viewer/public/coi-serviceworker.js` (a COOP/COEP service worker, injected into the **web** `index.html` only via a `vite.config.mjs` `transformIndexHtml` plugin — the embed single-file build uses no WASM and must never see it) to become cross-origin isolated, and `src/viewer/src/worker/worker.ts` imports both `.wasm` `?url`s and returns the matching one from `locateFile`. `src/wasm/package.json`'s `files` and the viewer's `vite.config.mjs` freshness check both cover both binaries. Builds `bindings/wasm/js_bindings.cpp` (embind) against the same `meshioplusplus_core_obj` as `_core`. Ships **all 43 formats** — 43 readable, 46 writable (`svg`/`tikz`/`gmsh22` write-only — the last since reading auto-detects a file's own `$MeshFormat` version and needs no separate key; `gid` was write-only too until its reader landed in v10.19.0), XDMF in all three data formats — so there is no longer a wasm-specific format gap; see `doc/wasm.md`. **Ragged cell blocks** (polygon/polyhedron) cross the JS boundary as flat CSR arrays (v8.7.0) — `{type, data, rowOffsets}` for 1-level (jagged polygon rows) and `{type, data, faceOffsets, cellOffsets}` for 2-level (polyhedron, cell → faces → node ids) — since embind has no efficient representation for a nested array of arrays; `mesh_to_val`/`val_to_mesh` build these from/into the uniform API's `RowSize`/`Row`/`NumFaces`/`Face` and `AddPolygonBlock`/`AddPolyhedronBlock` exactly as every C++ operation already does, so the boundary carries a polyhedron block correctly (round-trips through e.g. `clean`) even though no C++ format writer accepted one until v9.19.0 (MED/EnSight/VTU) and v9.20.0 (OpenFOAM) — the gap this sentence used to record the boundary work does not (and could not) close, distinct from and unblocked by it. Smoke test: `node tests/wasm/smoke.mjs`.
**wasm HDF5/netCDF/cgnslib** (v8.0.0; cgnslib v9.22.0): none is a system library on this target and CMake never downloads anything, so `build/build-wasm-deps.sh` source-builds **HDF5 1.14.6 + netcdf-c 4.9.3 + cgnslib 4.5.2** (pinned + SHA256-checked; `--without-cgnslib` drops the last, `--without-hdf5` drops all three) into `build/wasm-deps/<pins>/`, and `configure-wasm.sh` passes that prefix as **`CMAKE_FIND_ROOT_PATH`** — the required knob, since the Emscripten toolchain sets `CMAKE_FIND_ROOT_PATH_MODE_*=ONLY` (it `list(APPEND)`s its sysroot, so passing ours adds a second root rather than replacing it) and `CMAKE_PREFIX_PATH`/`HINTS` are therefore ignored. `configure-wasm.sh` builds the prefix automatically when missing; `--without-hdf5` (implies `--without-netcdf`) gives the old small artifact, `--deps-prefix` reuses one, and `--with-hdf5 --without-zlib` is a hard error (every HDF5 writer calls `H5Pset_deflate`). HDF5 **≥1.14 is required, not preferred** — 1.14.0 removed `H5detect`/`H5make_libsettings`, which is what made cross-compiling painful. Both are static, `-Oz`, against Emscripten's zlib port, with everything needing an absent OS service off (HDF5: threadsafe/MPI/plugins/ROS3/direct/mirror/subfiling VFDs; netCDF: DAP/DAP4/byterange/NCZarr/S3/libxml2→bundled ezxml/plugins-`dlopen`/mmap). **`HDF5_BUILD_HL_LIB=ON` is load-bearing** even though meshio++ uses only the core C API: netCDF-4's dimension-scale layer needs `H5DS*`. Three fix-ups, all in the script: `build/patches/hdf5-1.14.6-fe-invalid.patch` guards `feclearexcept(FE_INVALID)` (wasm's `<fenv.h>` defines `FE_ALL_EXCEPT` as 0 and no `FE_INVALID` at all), and the two installed CMake configs are rewritten to replace the imported-target names `ZLIB::ZLIB` / `HDF5::HDF5` / `hdf5::hdf5_hl` with archive paths — no single `find_package` in a consumer defines all of them, and CMake `try_compile` scratch projects (which netcdf-c's configure uses heavily) hard-fail on the dangling name. netCDF's interface is also reordered `hdf5_hl` before `hdf5`, since wasm-ld resolves archives in command-line order. **The rewritten `libz.a` path is not stable across CI runs** — it is Emscripten's zlib *port* archive under `em-config CACHE`, i.e. under `$EMSDK`, and `mymindstorm/setup-emsdk` installs into a fresh directory named after the current job's runner-assigned temp dir on every single run — while `build/wasm-deps/<pins>/` (the `$PREFIX`-rooted HDF5 archive paths) is `actions/cache`d *across* runs at a stable workspace path. A cache hit therefore restores CMake configs whose `libz.a` reference still names a prior run's now-nonexistent `$EMSDK`, and ninja fails with "missing and no known rule to make it" — bit us once a cache had gone a while without a rebuild-forcing change to `build-wasm-deps.sh`/`build/patches/*`, hence looking "fine" in every earlier PR. The fix (also in `build-wasm-deps.sh`) is a small unconditional loop, run after both fix-ups regardless of whether HDF5/netCDF were just built or skipped as "already installed": it re-points any `.../sysroot/lib/wasm32-emscripten/libz.a` suffix in either installed config at the *current* run's `$ZLIB_LIBRARY`, a no-op when the path is already current. **`-sSTACK_SIZE=4MB` + `-sSTACK_OVERFLOW_CHECK=1`** on the wasm target are part of this, not incidental: Emscripten's default 64 KiB stack is overrun by HDF5/netCDF-4 frames, the stack grows **down into the static data segment**, and the corruption is silent — one Exodus write clobbered libc++'s locale facets, after which every `istream >> number` in the module (i.e. every ASCII reader) trapped with "null function or function signature mismatch". `tests/wasm/smoke.mjs` pins this with an OBJ read placed *after* the exodus round-trip. Two documented consequences: writing `.xdmf` now emits an `.h5` companion (registry default follows the build — **two** files out of `FS`, not one), and MED writes **plain** `CHA` fields here but not the enhanced ones (units, multi-timestep metadata, `med:nom` component names, named profiles, ELNO/ELGA are Python-reference-only and there is no Python in this build, so they throw by name) — geometry/tags/families/regions and ordinary point/cell fields, multi-component included since v9.9.0, write normally. **Data-array component counts cross the boundary in sibling `*_components` objects** (v9.9.0): `point_data_components`/`cell_data_components`/`field_data_components`, `{name: k}`, emitted by `mesh_to_val` and honoured by `val_to_mesh`, with an absent name meaning one component so every pre-v9.9.0 caller is unaffected. A flat typed array carries no shape, which is exactly the rationale `xdmfSeriesWriteDataArrays`' own `components` argument already stated — the convention is deliberately the same one rather than a second invention. Before this, an `(n,3)` array re-entered C++ as `(3n,1)`: MED then refused to read its own output (`NCO=1`/`NBR=3n` against `n` points) and every *object*-based operation silently took its `rows != num_points` pass-through branch, returning stale values of the wrong length rather than gathering. **The C++ operations were never at fault** — `subset_gather_rows`, `refine.cpp`'s per-component interpolation and `reorder_scatter_rows` all derive the row stride from the array's real trailing dims — and the path-based `convert`/`convertSurface`/`convertSurfaceOps` calls were never affected either, since they never materialize a JS mesh. That last fact is why `smoke.mjs`'s pre-existing 'convertSurfaceOps keeps multi-component data' step passed throughout: any new test of this must cross the **object** boundary (`writeMesh`/`readMesh`/`refine`), not the path one. **Three files, not one:** a binding is only reachable from `loadMeshioPlusPlus()` if it is (1) registered in `bindings/wasm/js_bindings.cpp`, (2) **forwarded** in `src/wasm/src/index.mjs`'s returned object, and (3) declared in `src/wasm/index.d.ts`. Step 2 is the one that gets forgotten — it silently shipped every geometry op unreachable until v7.4.0 (and the `data_*` ops until v7.2.1). `smoke.mjs` exercises operations **through the wrapper**, never `Module.*`, precisely so a missing forward fails the build, and its reflection guard is now **exhaustive** over all 45 names rather than the geometry ops only. Note that `bindings/wasm/` is **outside the amalgamation** (which covers `src/cpp/include` + `src/cpp/src`), so a binding-only addition needs no single-header regen — `availableFormats` and `convertSurface` (v7.8.0) were both added that way. `-fwasm-exceptions` is required (applied `PUBLIC` on `meshioplusplus_core_obj` under `if(EMSCRIPTEN)`) so `ReadError`/`WriteError` surface as catchable JS `Error`s instead of aborting; `log.hpp`'s `std::osyncstream` usage has a `std::mutex`-guarded fallback for standard libraries (Emscripten's non-threaded libc++) that ship `<syncstream>` without actually defining it (`__cpp_lib_syncbuf` unset). `configure-wasm.sh` runs `embuilder build zlib` right after configuring (when zlib is on) to pre-warm Emscripten's zlib port cache single-threaded — `-sUSE_ZLIB=1` makes every translation unit trigger that port's build on first use, and a parallel `-j` build races many `em++` invocations against the same cold-cache lock, aborting with "attempt to lock the cache while a parent process is holding the lock (sanity)". CI (`.github/workflows/wasm.yml`) publishes to npm (`NPM_TOKEN` secret) only on `v*` tags (mirrors `wheels.yml`'s trigger), but since v8.0.0 it also **builds+smoke-tests on PRs** touching the wasm surface or `src/cpp/` — the job was tag-only while this build had no external dependencies, which stopped being tenable once it linked a wasm32 HDF5/netCDF; the dependency prefix is `actions/cache`d (keyed on the emsdk pin + `build-wasm-deps.sh` + `build/patches/`) so the added cost is one short job, and Eigen is checked out by targeted `git submodule update --init src/cpp/third_party/eigen` (never `submodules: true`, which would drag in Polyscope).

**C API / Fortran build** (`libmeshioplusplus` + Fortran module, for C/Fortran HPC consumers): `build/configure.sh --c-api` or `--fortran` (implies `--c-api`; both also exist in `configure.bat`, Fortran untested on MSVC) → CMake options `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_BUILD_C_API` / `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_BUILD_FORTRAN` (both OFF by default). `cmake --install <tree> --prefix <p>` installs the shared lib(s), the single pure-C header `bindings/c/include/meshioplusplus/meshioplusplus.h`, a `find_package(meshioplusplus)` config package, a relocatable pkg-config file, and (Fortran) the `.mod` **plus** `bindings/fortran/meshioplusplus.f90` (`.mod` files are compiler-specific; consumers on another compiler recompile the module — the HDF5 approach). Tests: `tests/cpp/test_c_api.cpp` (auto-globbed into the gtest suite when C_API is on) and the `fortran_api` ctest (`tests/fortran/test_fortran_api.f90`). CI-tested consumer examples: `doc/examples/c_api_example.c`, `doc/examples/fortran_example.f90`. See "Version bumps" above — `project()`'s `VERSION` feeds `mio_version()` and the `.so`'s VERSION/SOVERSION here.

**Write options** (`write_options.hpp` + `src/cpp/src/write_options.cpp`, v9.0.0): `registry_write_ex(path, mesh, format, WriteOptions{mEncoding, mCodec/mCodecSet, mFloatFormat})` is the **single owner** of "write this format with these parameters". The registry stores writers as `(path, mesh)` lambdas with one parameter set baked in per format, so nothing going through `registry_writers()` could select ASCII/binary, a block codec or a float format — the CLI had a private variant table and the flat bindings had nothing at all. That table now lives here and `src/cpp/cli/main.cpp`'s `write_binary_variant` is a thin wrapper over it, so the two cannot drift. **All-defaults dispatches straight through the registry**, so the common path stays byte-identical (and picks up formats with no parameterized entry). An option the format cannot honour is an **error** (`registry_write_supports` reports why), never silently ignored — `--codec zstd` on a Gmsh file is a mistake worth reporting. The C ABI counterpart is `mio_write_ex`/`mio_write_opts` with `mio_read_opts`' exact append-only discipline (a `reserved[5]` int64 tail; `mio_write` is `mio_write_ex(..., <defaults>)` unchanged). Note the CLI keeps passing `--float-format` only to a writer that takes one, because it has always been *ignored* elsewhere and `registry_write_supports` would otherwise turn that into a new error.

**Provenance** (`detail/provenance.hpp` + `_provenance.py`, v10.15.0/v10.16.0/v10.17.0, docs [`doc/provenance.md`](doc/provenance.md) + [`doc/formats.md`](doc/formats.md#provenance)): a single `kProvenanceTag`/`TAG` constant, built from `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_VERSION_STRING`/`__version__` respectively, is the **only** source for the one-line credit (`"Written by meshio++ v<release>"`) that every writer with a free-text header slot now emits — replacing ~25 hand-written per-writer strings that had drifted three ways: a stale `meshio` (not `meshio++`) name in several Python writers, a `(C++ core)`-vs-`v{version}` split that made the C++/Python fallback boundary visible in output bytes, and three writers (`obj`, `ply`, `exodus`) embedding `datetime.now()` — plus a fourth, `flac3d`, embedding a separate `time.ctime()` comment line the C++ writer never had — so writing the same mesh twice produced different bytes. Both engines now emit **character-identical** text (`tests/python/test_provenance.py` pins this per format); `_core.__version__` was added specifically so the Python side can assert this against what the compiled extension actually is, rather than trusting the two version sources to agree. `nastran` is the one documented exception: the C++ reader's sentinel gate (`"meshioplusplus-cpp-nastran"`, see [`doc/formats/nastran.md`](doc/formats/nastran.md)) must stay the first `$` comment line, so the tag rides as a *second* line after it, and the Python writer — which never emits the sentinel — emits only the tag. `openfoam`'s `FoamFile` banner box re-pads the credit cell to a fixed 48-column width so the box's closing `|` stays aligned regardless of the release string's length. That release was deliberately **only** the roadmap's "audit and normalize" bullet (`doc/roadmap.md` §1) — no timestamp, no source-format/operation-chain record, no `WriteOptions` plumbing; `doc/formats.md`'s Provenance section is the per-format comment-syntax audit table that bullet asked for. **v10.16.0 closed §1's bullets 2-7** — docs [`doc/provenance.md`](doc/provenance.md), the design note the roadmap asked for before implementing. Reconnaissance ruled out `WriteOptions` as the record's home: only 4 of the write paths reach `registry_write_ex` (the native CLI, `mio_write_ex`, the pipeline, the sequence driver) -- Python's own path (`_helpers.write` -> the shim -> `_core.<fmt>_write`) and WASM's (`registry_writers()`'s raw lambdas) both bypass it, so a `WriteOptions`-borne record would be invisible from the surface most callers use; growing it is also Tier A (pinned at 48 bytes). The record instead lives in a **thread-local RAII-scoped context** (`ProvenanceScope`/`Mode`/`SlotTier`/`ProvenanceRecord`/`ProvenanceNote`, the `set_buffer_allocator` shape with one change -- thread-local not process-global, since writes are not serialized), read by every writer exactly where it already read `kProvenanceTag`, so no signature anywhere changed. `Mode::Off` reproduced v10.15.0's bytes exactly and was the v10.16.0 default; **v10.17.0 flipped the default to `BestEffort` -- provenance is ON**, so an ordinary `write()` renders the assumptions raised while it ran with no scope needed (`default_provenance_mode()`/`set_default_provenance_mode`, env `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_PROVENANCE=off`). The fields needing a caller (source/target/chain) stay absent without a scope and **no timestamp is added**, so a write that loses nothing is still byte-identical to v10.15.0 and every byte-pinned suite (`test_io_baseline.py`, tikz/svg/gmsh/mdpa) stayed untouched -- exactly 3 tests moved, all of them tests *of* the old default. **Note lifetime is the subtle part and getting it wrong writes a FALSE line into a file**: a note has no lifetime without a scope, so before bounding existed `extract_surface(A)` dropping a region put `Note [regions-dropped]: extract_surface: ...` into unrelated mesh B's next-written header (reproduced, then pinned by `AnOperationsNoteDoesNotLeakIntoAnUnrelatedWrite`). The **public** write entry points (`meshioplusplus.write`, `registry_write_ex`, `mio_write`) therefore call `provenance_begin_write()`, resetting the scope-less record so only THIS write's notes render; it is a no-op inside a scope, since spanning several writes is what a scope is for. Two consequences: a note raised *before* the write (the 9 operation-side sites) is **dropped rather than misattributed** -- capture those with an explicit scope spanning ops+write -- and the **low-level C++ writers do not bound notes for themselves** (`write_vtu(...)` called directly skips the public path), the same caller-manages-state contract that layer already has. **The engine marker the roadmap's bullet 3 asks for is deliberately NOT in the file** -- it directly contradicts bullet 5's character-identity requirement, being exactly the `(C++ core)`-vs-`v{version}` drift v10.15.0 removed; it is reported through `current_provenance()`/`scope.get()` instead, which costs nothing. `SlotTier` (`None`/`Bounded`/`SingleLine`/`Block`) classifies what a writer's own call site can hold -- narrower in one case than the format's grammar in general (ansys's genuine `(0 "...")` multi-line comment section is not used, since populating it with arbitrary rendered text risks corrupting the reader's own paren-balance parsing on a note containing literal parentheses; ansys stays `SingleLine`) -- and `Mode::Required` throws only for `SlotTier::None`, never for `SingleLine`/`Bounded`: degrading to a structurally smaller slot's honest maximum is not a failure, matching `write_options.hpp`'s standing rule without making a provenance request error on the ~third of the format table with no slot at all. **The Python<->C++ bridge is load-bearing**: opening `_provenance.scope` from Python also pushes a matching `ProvenanceScope` on the C++ side (`bindings/python/_core.cpp`'s `provenance_scope_push`/`_pop`, a thread-local stack of owned scopes mirroring the pybind layer's own precedent), and every `note`/`set_source`/`set_target`/`add_operation` call mirrors into both -- without it, a Python-opened scope would be silently invisible to the ~40 of 44 formats whose write goes through the compiled writer. The **operation chain** is sourced from the vocabulary that already existed: `run_pipeline` never opens a scope itself (opt-in everywhere) but calls `provenance_set_source`/`_set_target`/`_add_operation` unconditionally (no-ops outside a scope), rendering each step as `Op(Key=value, ...)` with `std::map`-sorted keys; `_pipeline.py`'s separate pure-Python engine renders the identical shape from the same step dicts, pinned to agree with the C++ engine byte for byte for the common bool/int/string cases (a `test_cpp_and_python_pipeline_engines_agree_on_the_operation_chain` test). The **in-memory API path is the honest limit**: `read()` then `refine()` then `write()` as three separate calls has nothing tying them together short of a caller-opened scope, named rather than papered over. **Conversion-assumption capture** uses one no-op-outside-a-scope call, `provenance_note(category, detail)`, added beside an *existing* warn call; duplicate `(category, detail)` pairs collapse. Two sites are wired as the reference pattern rather than attempting all ~60-80: `detail::warn_regions_dropped` (the 9-operation choke point) now also notes `"regions-dropped"`, and the OFF writer's cell-type-skip warning is wired in **both** engines with matching wording (pinned character-identical). **Round-trip safety is structural, not a rule**: no reader retains any provenance slot (VTK legacy discards its title line, exodus never reads its own `title` attribute, OBJ/OFF/PLY/FLAC3D/MDPA skip comments generically), so "replace never append" falls out for free -- a writer always renders from the live record, never by reading and appending to the input's own block. The **timestamp** honours `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` (wins over the blanket off switch) and `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_PROVENANCE_TIMESTAMP=off`; because the block is opt-in, no existing byte-pinned test needed a suppression exemption. **C ABI**: `mio_provenance_scope_begin`/`_end` (two calls, not RAII -- backed by a thread-local stack on the binding side, the same shape the Python bridge uses) plus `mio_provenance_note`/`_set_source`/`_set_target`. **ABI**: Tier C additive (`ProvenanceMode`/`SlotTier`/`ProvenanceNote`/`ProvenanceRecord`/`ProvenanceScope` and ten new free functions, `kProvenanceTag` itself byte-identical to v10.15.0) -- `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_ABI_VERSION` stays 10, `ProvenanceNote`/`ProvenanceRecord` pinned in `test_abi_layout.cpp` from the release that introduces them (the `RefineOptions`/`RemeshOptions` "pin in advance" lesson). **v10.17.0 closed §1 entirely** (read-back + the four remaining bindings), so the roadmap section is gone. **Read-back is ONE scanner, not 44 parsers** (`detail::read_provenance_lines`/`scan_provenance_text`, twinned in `_provenance.py`): `render_block`'s CONTENT lines are format-independent by construction, so recovering them is a head-bytes pass that strips whatever marker the format uses and keeps the six shapes the renderer emits. Three wrapping shapes need more than a leading-marker strip and each earns its place -- a keyword slot that WRAPS the text (`TITLE = "..."`, `(1 "...")`), whose closer is removed **only because that opener put it there**, never unconditionally (an unconditional trailing-`)` strip silently truncated `Converted from x (fmt)` and `Operation: Clean(Weld=true)` -- found by the tests, pinned by `ScannerKeepsParenthesesThatBelongToTheContent`); a box-drawn banner (OpenFOAM), whose credit sits in an interior `|`-delimited cell; and a NUL-padded fixed-width binary slot (STL 80-byte, EnSight `str80`), handled by treating NUL as a line break so one line-oriented scan serves it too. **Exodus is the one format the scanner does not serve** -- its block rides a netCDF `title` attribute, so `read_exodus_metadata` reads it there (the file is already open for `mTimeValues`) and feeds the SAME scanner, so the two paths cannot disagree. Results land on **`MeshMetadata::mProvenance`/`mProvenanceRecognised`**, filled by `registry_read_metadata` on BOTH the native and fallback paths (it has the path either way; `metadata_from_mesh` structurally cannot, a `Mesh` not carrying it) -- a **Tier A** change, hence `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_ABI_VERSION` 10 -> 11 and `MeshMetadata`'s pin moving 256 -> 288. **Raw lines, not a re-parsed record**: a block can be hand-edited, truncated by a `Bounded` slot, or written by a later release with unknown fields, so `mRecognised` (does line 0 match our tag format?) is the honest distinction between "meshio++ wrote this" and "something left a comment here", and nothing is silently dropped or mis-attributed. **Never re-emitted** -- writers render from the live record only, which is what makes "replace never append" structural (`AReadBlockIsNeverReEmitted`, sabotage-verified). C accessors `mio_read_metadata_num_provenance_lines`/`_provenance_line`/`_provenance_recognised` are purely additive (the handle is already opaque); Python gets `read_metadata(...)["provenance"]`/`["provenance_recognised"]`; both CLIs' `info` print a `Provenance:` section, labelled `(not written by meshio++)` when unrecognised. **Bindings**: a scope cannot cross an ABI boundary as RAII, so each surface gives begin/end a lifetime in its own idiom -- Julia `provenance(f)` (`do`-block), R `mio_with_provenance(expr)` (`on.exit`), WASM `withProvenance(mode, fn)` (callback), all three closing on a throw; Fortran keeps the explicit pair, matching its own `m%free()` convention. **The assumption sweep is deliberately NOT exhaustive**, and two findings say why: most `warn()` calls are reader-side or user-error diagnostics, NOT conversion assumptions, and some are simply **stale** -- `avsucd`'s "can only write one cell data array" fires while BOTH engines demonstrably write every array, so wiring it would have recorded a loss that does not happen. Each site needs checking, not translating. `test_provenance.py::test_engines_record_the_same_notes` is the guard: wherever both engines write a given mesh they must record the same notes, so a one-sided addition fails at the format that caused it.

**XDMF time series** (`formats/xdmf_time_series.{hpp,cpp}`, v9.0.0, docs [`doc/xdmf_time_series.md`](doc/xdmf_time_series.md)): `XdmfTimeSeriesWriter` (`WritePointsCells`/`WriteData(time, mesh)`/`Finalize`, move-only RAII), the C++ counterpart of the Python-only `TimeSeriesWriter`, in all three data formats. **v9.1.0 added the three things a solver needs.** (1) **`Flush()`** — light data used to be written *only* at `Finalize()`, so a killed/crashed/still-running solve left heavy data and no readable `.xdmf`; it saves to a sibling temp file then `rename`s (a crash mid-flush cannot truncate the previous one) and flushes heavy data first (the `.xdmf` never names a dataset not yet on disk). `SetAutoFlush(true)` is **opt-in**: a flush re-serializes the whole document, so per-step flushing is quadratic in the step count, and for `"XML"` — whose heavy data is *in* the document — in the data volume too. (2) **`XdmfSeriesMode::Append`** (trailing defaulted ctor param) continues an existing collection, resolved structurally the way `read_xdmf` does; the heavy-data counter resumes by **scanning the container** (`h5::open_file_rw` + `H5Lget_name_by_idx`, or probing `<base>N.bin`) rather than trusting the document, because a mis-resumed counter silently overwrites `data0` instead of failing. Appending to a nonexistent path is *not* an error — it is a fresh series, so a restartable solver passes it unconditionally. (3) **`WriteData(time, vector<NamedArray>, vector<NamedArray>)`** — the granularity a solver has once the geometry is fixed, and under the KRATOS backend the difference between re-staging the whole ModelPart every output step and not. It shares the step-grid scaffolding and `xts_add_attribute` with the `Mesh` overload but is deliberately **not** implemented by converting a `Mesh`, which would force Int64 cell data to Float64 and change existing output; it validates row counts (stricter than the `Mesh` path, which cannot get them wrong) and emits in the caller's order rather than sorted. Fortran deliberately has **no** array overload (an array of derived types with interop pointers is a poor Fortran shape, and a Fortran solver already holds an `mio_mesh` to `add_point_data` into) — a named gap in `doc/fortran.md`. **Pimpl is load-bearing, not style**: it keeps pugixml (build-only vendored, PRIVATE include dir) and `hdf5.h` (optional dep) out of an *installed* header. Deliberately **not** in `registry.cpp` — a stateful multi-call object is not a `(path, mesh)` format. The shared payload machinery (`DataItemStore`, `attribute_type`, `pack_mixed_topology`, …) was hoisted into `detail/xdmf_common.hpp` and `write_xdmf` refactored onto it, verified by diffing pre/post output byte-for-byte including the `.h5`. The same work fixed a real reader bug: `read_xdmf` resolved `Domain/Grid[0]` — the *collection* — and threw `unknown section Grid` on any temporal file, so **XDMF time series were unreadable**; it now resolves the collection structurally, honours `ReadOptions::mTimeStep` (the second reader after exodus) and fills `MeshMetadata::mTimeValues`. Bindings: C API `mio_xdmf_series_*`; **Fortran/Julia/R** ride that C API with each language's own handle idiom — Fortran `type(mio_xdmf_series)` (type-bound `create`/`write_points_cells`/`write_data`/`finalize`/`num_steps`/`free`, optional `stat`/`errmsg`, explicit free like `mio_mesh`), Julia `XdmfSeries` (GC finalizer + deterministic `close`, plus a `do`-block form; the exported name is **`finalize!` not `finalize`**, since `Base.finalize` means "run this object's GC finalizer"), R `mio_xdmf_series()` (external pointer with its **own** tag `mio_xdmf_series_handle`, in a new `src/xdmf_series.c`, so a `mio_mesh` and a series can never be passed for one another). The rule all three repeat, and the reason `finalize` exists separately from the free: **a write failure during the implicit finalize cannot be reported** — not from a Fortran `free()`, a Julia `close`, or an R GC finalizer — so an R test must release a series *before* its tempdir is removed or the finalizer silently fails writing into a vanished path (bit the test once). WASM `createXdmfTimeSeriesWriter` — an **opaque integer handle plus free functions, not an embind `class_`**, because this package's contract is that `Mesh`/`NDArray` stay internal and JS only ever sees plain objects, and because a bound *member* function bypasses `with_js_errors` and surfaces a `WriteError` as a message-less `WebAssembly.Exception`; the handle is a **table index, not a pointer**, so a stale handle throws instead of being a use-after-free. Python gets `_core.XdmfTimeSeriesWriter` (context-manager capable) exposed **additionally**, NOT as a shim under `xdmf/time_series.py` — the `.mdpa` rule again: a tested, documented Python API is not silently re-implemented underneath.

**Installable C++ API** (`MESHIOPLUSPLUS_INSTALL_CPP`, OFF by default, docs [`doc/cpp_api.md`](doc/cpp_api.md)): installs the whole `src/cpp/include/meshioplusplus/` header tree plus one real library per mesh backend, exported into the **same** `meshioplusplusTargets` set as the C API, so one `find_package(meshioplusplus CONFIG COMPONENTS CXX|C|Fortran)` serves every surface (`check_required_components` genuinely gates them — a missing component fails at `find_package`, not at the link). This is what makes `kratos_bridge.hpp`/`ModelPart` reachable at all: the C ABI cannot hand out a `Mesh` or a `ModelPart`, which is precisely why an out-of-tree Kratos application could not consume meshio++ before. **All three backends build side by side** (`meshioplusplus::core_meshio`/`::core_native`/`::core_kratos`, `meshioplusplus::core` aliasing the `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_MESH_BACKEND` default; `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_INSTALL_CPP_BACKENDS` trims the set) so one prefix serves consumers that disagree about the backend — each variant is a **full, independent compile** of the core, which is the cost. Three implementation facts worth not rediscovering: (1) the variants compile `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_CORE_SOURCES` **directly**, not through an OBJECT library, because a STATIC library records even PRIVATE deps in `INTERFACE_LINK_LIBRARIES` as `$<LINK_ONLY:>` and `install(EXPORT)` then rejects the unexported object library (the C API dodges this only by always being SHARED); (2) every PUBLIC usage requirement is accumulated into `_mio_core_*` lists and re-applied by `meshioplusplus_configure_core_target()` rather than inherited, since the object library can be neither exported nor PUBLIC-linked — the mesh-backend macro is the one thing deliberately *not* in the shared list, being per-variant; (3) HDF5 is linked through the **`HDF5::HDF5` imported target**, not `${HDF5_C_LIBRARIES}` — the raw variable bakes absolute library paths into `meshioplusplusTargets.cmake` and makes the prefix resolve only on a machine whose HDF5 sits exactly where the build's did (fixed v9.0.0). The price, stated in the generated config's own error message, is that `FindHDF5`/`FindMPI` need the **C language enabled** in the consumer's `project()`, which a CXX-only project otherwise has no reason to do. Symbol visibility: the variants are `CXX_VISIBILITY_PRESET hidden` + `VISIBILITY_INLINES_HIDDEN`, with the public surface annotated `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_API` (`export.hpp`, modelled on the C header's `MIO_API`) — a missing annotation is a **link** error for a shared consumer, never silent, which is what makes the annotation set verifiable; `tests/consumer/` linked against a SHARED build is that check, and the `cpp-install` CI job runs it per backend across a static all-backends leg, a shared MESHIO leg and (v9.1.0) a **shared KRATOS** leg — the configuration a Kratos application actually ships, and the only one where a missing annotation on that backend is a link error. That leg must also pass `-DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_MESH_BACKEND=KRATOS`, or `meshioplusplus::core` is undefined and the default-alias sub-step fails. **`MESHIOPLUSPLUS_NO_STD_SPAN` needs no code and no private build**: `std::span` appears in exactly two lines repo-wide (`native_mesh.hpp`'s include and the inline `ConnSpan()`), so the macro is **ABI-neutral**, and the guard is `#ifndef` — a consumer whose Boost uBLAS collides with `<span>` under MSVC defines it on their own command line against *any* prefix, including one built without it. `tests/consumer/`'s `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_CONSUMER_NO_STD_SPAN` option plus a CI leg makes that a gate rather than a claim. **What to pin** (doc/cpp_api.md, and since v9.4.0 [`doc/abi.md`](doc/abi.md) for the whole contract): `COMPONENTS C` is `SOVERSION 0` + `SameMajorVersion` with append-only option structs, so `find_package(meshioplusplus 9 … C)`; `COMPONENTS CXX` makes **no ABI promise** — `Mesh`/`ModelPart`/`GeometricalEntity` are header-defined and v9.1.0 added a member to the last — so pin either the **full three-component** version `EXACT` (`X.Y.Z EXACT`, never `X.Y EXACT`: under `SameMajorVersion` an `EXACT` request is a full *string* comparison against the installed version, so a `major.minor` request matches nothing at all — both docs shipped exactly that unusable form through v9.1.0) or, more precisely, the exported `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_ABI_VERSION`. **The ABI version is deliberately NOT the release version** (v9.4.0): it moves only when the installed headers stop being compatible with an already-compiled consumer, which two of the three 9.x transitions did and the third — v9.3.0, whose entire installed-header delta was one new `inline constexpr` in `formats/exodus.hpp` — did not. It lives in **one** place, `src/cpp/include/meshioplusplus/abi_version.hpp`, which `CMakeLists.txt` *parses* rather than copying (a second copy would put a wrong `SOVERSION` on a library whose symbols say otherwise), and the C++ variants' `SOVERSION` **is** that number (`libmeshioplusplus_core_kratos.so.6` at v9.20.0) while the C and Fortran libraries stay at `SOVERSION 0`. `detail/abi_version_check.hpp` is the `mesh_backend_check.hpp` technique on this second axis — a **separate** symbol, so a right-backend/stale-headers consumer hears about the headers — with the same `gnu::used`-is-load-bearing rule and the same **MSVC + shared DLL gap**; `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_NO_ABI_VERSION_CHECK` opts out, and `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_ABI_VERSION` itself is deliberately not `-D`-overridable (that would be a knob to silence the guard while still miscompiling — hence CI stages a mismatch by patching a *copy* of the installed headers). Two gates keep the integer honest, and neither suffices alone: `tests/cpp/test_abi_layout.cpp` pins `sizeof`/`alignof` per backend (catches layout changes mechanically; the numbers are pinned only on Linux/x86_64/libstdc++, since `sizeof(std::string)` alone differs across standard libraries) and `tools/check-abi-version.sh` (in the `lint` job, which therefore needs `fetch-depth: 0`) fails a build whose installed headers changed while the ABI version did not and `doc/abi_reviews.md` records no additive review — that one catches inline-body/ODR changes, which no tool can infer. **That review lookup is keyed on `(ABI version, release version from CMakeLists.txt)` AND on the row naming every changed header, never on the ABI number alone** — the v9.4.0 shape matched any row whose first column was the current ABI, so the moment one existed (two did) every later header change matched it and the gate exited 0 reporting "records the additive review" about a *different* release, leaving Tier B completely unguarded since the layout snapshot cannot see an inline-body edit; keying on the release alone is also not enough, because a header change that skips the version bump still finds the previous release's row, which is why `doc/abi_reviews.md`'s `headers changed` column is load-bearing and must list every header (paths relative to `src/cpp/include/meshioplusplus/`). `tests/python/test_check_abi_version.py` drives the real script over a throwaway git repo and is the guard that it *fires* — written only after confirming all five of its fix-targeting cases fail against the pre-9.4.1 script, since a gate nobody tests for firing is exactly how the v9.1.0 backend guard shipped inert. **Backend-mismatch guards** (the reason `detail/mesh_backend_check.hpp` and `src/cpp/src/mesh_backend_check.cpp` exist): the macro rides in `INTERFACE_COMPILE_DEFINITIONS` so a CMake consumer cannot disagree by accident, defining two macros is a `#error` in `mesh.hpp`, and defining none while linking a NATIVE/KRATOS build is an undefined reference to `mesh_backend_is_<backend>` — that last case is the dangerous one (`mesh.hpp` silently falls through to MESHIO) and only bites non-CMake consumers, hence `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_NO_BACKEND_LINK_CHECK` to opt out. `meshioplusplus-cxx.pc` is a **second** pkg-config file, never an extension of the C one, whose `Cflags` must stay valid under `gcc -std=c99` (CI compiles `doc/examples/c_api_example.c` that way). Note also that `src/cpp/third_party/{pugixml,eigen}` are **PRIVATE** include dirs (v8.9.0): no installed header references either, and Eigen's is a submodule path that does not exist on a consumer's machine — leaving them PUBLIC broke `find_package` outright and collided with a consumer's own vendored pugixml.

**Conan / vcpkg packaging** (self-hosted, in-repo, for the C API): both live under `packages/` — the root `conanfile.py` (Conan 2.x, with the test package at `packages/conan/`, `test_type = "explicit"` so it only runs via an explicit `--test-folder=packages/conan`, as `packages.yml`'s `conan create` does) and the `packages/vcpkg/meshioplusplus/` vcpkg overlay port (`vcpkg.json` + `portfile.cmake` + `usage`) both drive the same `-DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_BUILD_C_API=ON -DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_BUILD_PYTHON=OFF` install/`find_package` path (the `meshioplusplus::meshioplusplus` target). Options/features: `with_hdf5`/`with_netcdf`/`with_zlib` (on) and `fortran`/`with_eigen`/`with_kahip` (off — Eigen is a submodule absent from a source tarball, so the MED transpose uses the fallback loop; KaHIP is on neither ConanCenter nor the vcpkg registry, so `with_kahip`/the vcpkg `kahip` feature map only the CMake flag and the consumer supplies `KAHIP_ROOT`; the lib is shared-only). The vcpkg `hdf5` feature depends on upstream vcpkg's `hdf5` port with `default-features: false` + explicit `features: ["zlib"]` (meshio++ never uses szip, only plain HDF5 + zlib) — kept for forward compatibility, but currently **ineffective**: a known vcpkg-tool limitation (`default-features: false` isn't honored reliably even for a direct manifest dependency — reproduced locally against a fresh vcpkg checkout; see microsoft/vcpkg#31230/#26664) means `hdf5`'s own default `szip` feature still gets pulled in regardless, which in turn needs `libaec`. The **actual** fix is `packages/vcpkg/libaec/`, a second overlay port (alongside `packages/vcpkg/meshioplusplus/`) that replaces vcpkg registry's `libaec` portfile — otherwise byte-identical — with one sourcing the same `v1.1.7` tag from `Deutsches-Klimarechenzentrum/libaec` on GitHub instead of `gitlab.dkrz.de`: same maintainer/org, same release content (different tarball SHA512 since GitHub and GitLab produce differently-encoded archives for identical source), reachable without the rate-limiting (HTTP 429) that had been intermittently failing the release-tag `vcpkg-build` CI job. Verified locally end-to-end (bootstrapped vcpkg, built `libaec`+`hdf5`+`netcdf-c` against this overlay set). `.github/workflows/packages.yml` validates the Conan recipe (two option legs, each on x86_64 and arm64 — see "CI" below) and the vcpkg manifest (x86_64 only; the overlay-port zip it produces is source, not an arch-specific binary) on every PR, and on `v*` tags pins the tarball SHA512 + builds the vcpkg port end-to-end. Not submitted to Conan Center / the upstream vcpkg registry (documented follow-up) — as a stopgap, `v*` tags attach ready-to-use Linux package artifacts, for both x86_64 and arm64, to the tag's GitHub Release (created if absent, via `softprops/action-gh-release`): a `conan cache save "meshioplusplus/<version>:*"` archive per option leg × arch (supplies just the recipe + one matching prebuilt binary — `conan cache restore <file>` then `conan install --requires=meshioplusplus/<version> --build=missing -o ...`; Conan Center still supplies the transitive hdf5/netcdf/zlib/openssl binaries over the network as normal, and `--build=missing` rebuilds meshioplusplus from the archived recipe if the consumer's profile doesn't match the prebuilt one — verified end-to-end both ways) and a zip of the vcpkg overlay port with the release tarball's SHA512 already pinned in (the in-repo `portfile.cmake` keeps the `SHA512 0` placeholder; only the release asset's copy is patched). Docs: [`doc/c_api.md`](doc/c_api.md#package-managers-conan-vcpkg).

**Spack packaging** (upstream, NOT self-hosted — unlike Conan/vcpkg there is no `packages/spack/` in this repo): two recipes, [`meshioplusplus`](https://github.com/spack/spack-packages/blob/develop/repos/spack_repo/builtin/packages/meshioplusplus/package.py) (`CMakePackage`, the standalone C API + optional Fortran module, mirroring the Conan/vcpkg install path — `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_BUILD_PYTHON=OFF` + `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_BUILD_C_API=ON`) and [`py-meshioplusplus`](https://github.com/spack/spack-packages/blob/develop/repos/spack_repo/builtin/packages/py_meshioplusplus/package.py) (`PythonPackage`, the pybind11 wheel via scikit-build-core `config_settings`), both merged upstream into `spack/spack-packages` ([#5624](https://github.com/spack/spack-packages/pull/5624)) and sourced from the GitHub release tarball (`v6.0.0`+; `6.0.0` predates the PyPI sdist, so every version builds from the same archive for uniformity). Variants mirror Conan/vcpkg's options/features (`+hdf5`/`+netcdf`/`+zlib`/`+zstd`/`+lz4`/`+kahip`, `meshioplusplus`'s own `+fortran`/`+cxx_api`/`+cxx_api_backends`/`parallel=`/`mesh_backend=`) rather than reinventing names. Both `conflicts("%gcc@:9")` (C++20). Since the recipes live in the `spack-packages` repo and not here, a meshio++ release does **not** need a version bump on this side to reach Spack — a maintainer (or anyone) adds the new `version(...)`/sha256 line upstream after tagging; this repo has no CI hook into that process. Docs: [`doc/installation.md`](doc/installation.md#spack) and [`doc/c_api.md`](doc/c_api.md#package-managers-conan-vcpkg-spack).

**Run tests:** `pytest tests/python/` (or `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/python/`).
Single file/test: `pytest tests/python/test_gmsh.py::test_gmsh22`.

**Coverage** (`MESHIOPLUSPLUS_COVERAGE=ON` → `--coverage -O0 -g -fprofile-update=atomic` on `meshioplusplus_core_obj`, GCC/Clang only; OFF by default so a normal `pip install` never pays for it): the `coverage` job in `ci.yml` measures **both layers in one job** so a single `lcov` capture sees the C++ core exercised by the GoogleTest binary **and** by the pytest suite through the pybind11 shims (measuring only the gtest binary badly under-reports the format code). This relies on pyproject's persistent `build-dir = "build/{wheel_tag}"` (the `.gcno/.gcda` survive the pip build) — don't split C++/Python into separate jobs. The gtest build sets `-DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_BUILD_C_API=ON` so `bindings/c/c_api.cpp` and its `test_c_api.cpp` suite are instrumented too. The Python leg also installs `pyvista`/`trimesh`/`pyarrow`/`mcp` (plus `libgl1`/`libglx-mesa0` for VTK's import) alongside `h5py`/`netCDF4` — **not just cosmetic**: the dedicated `interop` and `mcp` jobs run `_interop.py`'s and `mcp/_server.py`'s gated tests too but upload no coverage, so without these here that code (a large fraction of any interop-touching diff) is genuinely tested but reads as uncovered to Codecov's patch check, which only sees instrumented + uploaded runs (bit us on the interop PR — `codecov/patch` failed at 62% while every functional job, including `interop`, was green). Uploads go to Codecov under two flags, `python` (`src/python/meshioplusplus/`) and `cpp` (`src/cpp/`, `bindings/`, `bindings/c/`), gated on the `CODECOV_TOKEN` secret; `.codecov.yml` owns the (blocking) status thresholds and `pyproject.toml`'s `[tool.coverage.run] relative_files=true` keeps `coverage.xml` paths repo-relative. The non-MESHIO mesh-backend headers (`native_mesh`/`kratos_mesh`/`model_part`/`kratos_names`) are excluded from the denominator (both `lcov --remove` and `.codecov.yml` ignore) because this MESHIO-only job never instantiates them — they are covered by the separate `cpp-tests` backend matrix; `src/single_include/` (generated) is ignored too. Reproduce locally per the two builds in the job: an instrumented standalone `build-cpp-cov` (`-DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_BUILD_PYTHON=OFF -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Debug`, ctest) plus an instrumented editable install (`CMAKE_ARGS="-DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_COVERAGE=ON …" SKBUILD_CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Debug SKBUILD_EDITABLE_REBUILD=false`, then `pytest --cov meshioplusplus --cov-report xml`), then `lcov --capture --directory build-cpp-cov --directory build`. Locally add `-DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_WITH_NETCDF=OFF` and run `ctest -j1` (see the netCDF/MPI link quirk).

**Lint / format:** `pre-commit run -a` (isort, black, flake8). Or `black --check . && flake8 . && isort --check .`.

**Check linting before opening or updating a PR (required).** Run `isort --check`, `black --check`, and `flake8` (the commands above, scoped at minimum to every file the PR touches) before pushing — CI's `lint` job runs the same three pre-commit hooks and fails the PR on any of them, and each has bitten this repo separately: an import out of alphabetical order (isort), a line black would wrap differently (black), an unused local variable (flake8's `F841`). Fix with `isort <file>`/`black <file>` (both are auto-fixers, not just checkers) and re-run the checks clean before considering the change done — don't wait for CI to report it.

**C++ include hygiene** (`tools/include-cleanup.sh` + `.clang-tidy`): IWYU-style check via clang-tidy's `misc-include-cleaner`. `--check` (CI gate, `include-cleanup` job in `ci.yml`) fails on any **unused** include; missing-include suggestions are **advisory only** (the convention that a format's `.cpp` leans on its own format header to pull in the mesh types is kept). `--fix` removes unused includes. Deliberately-kept includes (the HDF5/netCDF format headers in `registry.cpp`, guarded optional-dep includes) carry `// IWYU pragma: keep`. Run the gate where HDF5/netCDF are installed (as CI does) or their format registrations compile out and look spuriously unused. `.clang-format` still owns ordering (`SortIncludes: Never`).

**Single-header amalgamation** (`tools/amalgamate.sh` + `tools/amalgamate/amalgamate.py`): generates the committed, STB-style header-only `src/single_include/meshioplusplus/meshioplusplus.hpp` (all of `src/cpp/include` + `src/cpp/src` + bundled pugixml; declarations always visible, `.cpp` bodies behind `#define MESHIOPLUSPLUS_IMPLEMENTATION`). Backend defaults MESHIO/sequential; HDF5/netCDF/zlib/Eigen stay behind `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_HAS_*`. The generator emits every header once at file scope in dependency order (so no header is trapped inside another's `#ifdef`). `--smoke` regenerates + compiles it (decls-only, impl, two-TU link). The `amalgamation` CI job regenerates, `git diff --exit-code`s (fails if stale — **regenerate and commit after any `src/cpp/` change**), and smoke-compiles. Anonymous-namespace helpers must stay uniquely named across `src/cpp/src/*.cpp` (they concatenate into one TU under the impl macro; format-prefixed like `su2_tokens`/`GmshCursor`). Docs: [`doc/single_header.md`](doc/single_header.md).

**CLI usage:**
```bash
meshioplusplus convert input.msh output.vtk
meshioplusplus info mesh.msh
meshioplusplus ascii mesh.msh         # convert to ASCII
meshioplusplus binary mesh.msh        # convert to binary
meshioplusplus view mesh.msh          # interactive viewer (desktop or browser)
meshioplusplus screenshot mesh.msh out.png   # headless render (polyscope only)
```

**Native CLI viewer** (`src/cpp/cli/view_payload.{hpp,cpp}` + `polyscope_view.{hpp,cpp}`, `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_WITH_POLYSCOPE`, **OFF by default**): Polyscope (MIT) vendored as a git submodule at `src/cpp/third_party/polyscope` — it is in no package manager and its docs offer only `add_subdirectory`/FetchContent. It has **its own** submodules, so `git submodule update --init --recursive` is required; a plain `--init` leaves an `EXISTS`-guarded no-op (the Eigen pattern) and the viewer is simply absent. **It links to the CLI target ONLY, never to `meshioplusplus_core_obj`** — unlike KaHIP, which is in the core because partitioning is a core operation. Viewing is not, so `_core`/C API/Fortran/WASM cannot acquire an OpenGL dependency through it and "the core is dependency-free" stays literally true. Release binaries are unaffected: the flag defaults off and `cli.yml`'s existing `! ldd | grep libstdc++` assertion is the guard. **The mapping is split at the same seam as Python's**: `view_payload.cpp` is deliberately free of every Polyscope header, so it compiles and is gtested (`test_view_payload.cpp`, 14 cases) in the *default* build with no GL, while `polyscope_view.cpp` is the only TU including Polyscope and the only thing the flag guards. `view_payload.cpp` is the **twin of `_viewer.py`'s `_to_polyscope_payload` — KEEP THEM IN SYNC**; both are needed because Python's must work with no compiled core, so moving the logic would relocate the duplication rather than remove it, and the two test suites assert the same invariants deliberately. Two facts read from the vendored headers rather than the docs: the mixed-cell padding sentinel is **`INVALID_IND_32` (UINT32_MAX)**, not the negative value the Python docs describe (the container is `std::array<uint32_t,8>`); and **`registerTetHexMesh` concatenates tets-then-hexes regardless of input order**, which would silently re-map every per-cell quantity — so the padded array must be built by us in block order, and a test pins that. `view`/`screenshot` exist in every build and throw naming the flag when compiled out (the `partition_kahip_parts` contract). CI: the `cli-polyscope` job in `ci.yml`, never `cli.yml`.

**Interactive viewer** (`src/python/meshioplusplus/_viewer.py` + `_viewer_browser.py`, web app in `src/viewer/`, docs [`doc/viewer.md`](doc/viewer.md)): one entry point `view(mesh, backend="auto"|"polyscope"|"browser", kind=, color_by=, name=, show=)` plus `screenshot(mesh, path, ...)` and `has_viewer()`, and CLI verbs `view`/`screenshot`. **Polyscope is a PYTHON-ONLY optional dependency** behind the `[viewer]` extra — lazily imported inside the two functions that need it, never at module scope, and it never reaches the C++/WASM/C/Fortran core; a missing install raises naming `pip install meshioplusplus[viewer]`. The bulk of the module is the **pure** `_to_polyscope_payload(mesh, kind)` (no renderer import, no display, no mutation), which is what makes the routing testable in the default CI matrix with polyscope absent. Routing facts that are contracts, not preferences: polyscope volume meshes hold **only tetrahedra and hexahedra**, so wedge/pyramid/higher-order go through `convert_cells(simplexify)` but hexes deliberately do not; a mesh with both tets and hexes is packed into a negative-padded `mixed_cells` array **here** rather than handed over as separate `tets=`/`hexes=` arguments, because otherwise the row order — and so every per-cell quantity — would depend on polyscope's internal merge order; each structure type has its own `defined_on` vocabulary (surface `vertices`/`faces`, volume `vertices`/`cells`, curve `nodes`/`edges`, point cloud **none at all**); `_init_polyscope` is idempotent because polyscope refuses to re-init with a different backend, so a headless screenshot followed by a window would otherwise raise. Cell data is tied to rendered primitives by the `cell_source` global-cell-index array from `_dimension_rows`, whose counter advances over *skipped* blocks — a gmsh file whose `triangle` block follows a boundary-marker `line` block is what makes that necessary, and the wrong answer still renders. Colours are detected by array **name** (`color`/`rgb`/`*_color`/`*_rgb`), never inferred from a [0,1] range. Every lossy step appends to a `notes` list surfaced as a warning. `_viewer_browser.py` writes **VTP** (not VTU — vtk.js has no unstructured-grid model) into the committed single-file bundle at `src/python/meshioplusplus/_viewer_assets/viewer.html`; its `_renderable_surface` is the **Python twin of the WASM `convertSurfaceOps([])` binding and the two must stay in step** (pinned by a test, not by coincidence). The offline page has no WASM and so cannot *run* operations: anything it should show is baked in by Python — `build_page(..., quality=True)` attaches metrics **before** the boundary extraction so the existing parent-id gather carries them for free, `stats=True` (default) embeds the volume mesh's geometry, and `color_by` resolves to the viewer's `location:name:component` key and **raises** on an unknown name. All three go through exact-string payload slots that CI greps for.

**Interoperability layer** (`src/python/meshioplusplus/_interop.py`, docs [`doc/interop.md`](doc/interop.md)): converts a `Mesh` to/from the **in-memory** objects of PyVista, trimesh, Apache Arrow/Parquet, pandas and polars with no file round-trip. **Python-only, over the numpy the readers already return — it adds no format, touches no binding, and the C++/WASM/C/Fortran core is untouched and stays dependency-free.** Structured exactly like `_viewer.py` and for the same reason: the bulk is a **pure payload layer** (`_to_vtk_payload`/`_to_triangles_payload`/`_to_table_payload` + frozen dataclasses) that imports no third-party library, mutates nothing, and is therefore tested in the **default** CI matrix with none of the optional libs installed; the public `to_*`/`from_*` are thin wrappers importing their target *inside* the function and raising a named `pip install meshioplusplus[...]` error (`_require`), with `has_pyvista`/`has_trimesh`/`has_arrow`/`has_pandas`/`has_polars` (+ `has_open3d`/`has_dolfinx` → `False`, Phase 2) as the predicates. **Reuse, not transcription**: the meshio↔VTK type map/node orderings come from `_vtk_common.py` (`meshio_to_vtk_type`, `meshio_to_vtk_order`, and `vtk_cells_from_data` for the reverse — note its `offsets` are *end* offsets, so `from_pyvista` passes `grid.offset[1:]`), and the global block-major cell index comes from `_regions.block_bases` (the Python owner of `detail/cell_index.hpp`) — nothing re-derives a block offset (the `_dimension_rows` counter bug is the cautionary tale). **The zero-copy contract**: buffers are shared when the target takes the array as-is; every `to_*` takes `zero_copy_only` (`False` → each copy lands in `notes`, surfaced as a warning; `True` → `InteropError` naming the array and reason). It governs arrays that *exist in the Mesh* (points, each data array, single-block connectivity) but **not derived ones** — VTK's `offsets`/`celltypes` have no meshio++ counterpart and a multi-block mesh has no single connectivity array, so making those fatal would reject every mixed-type mesh; they go to the payload's separate `derived` list, which is deliberately **not** warned (it is a data-model difference, not a loss). Copies are forced by int32→int64 connectivity, a `wedge` block (meshio's gmsh-Prism order ≠ VTK's), 2-D point padding, non-contiguity, `bool`→uint8, and any cross-block concatenation. **Lifetime is a hard requirement** — the readers hand back capsule-backed numpy owned by the C++ core — so every wrapper carries a `_meshioplusplus_refs` list of the shared arrays rather than relying on VTK's `_numpy_reference`; pinned by a convert→`del`→`gc.collect()`→re-read test. **PyVista**: `pv.UnstructuredGrid(offsets, conn, celltypes, points)` **does not exist** (only the legacy `(cells, cell_type, points)` layout, which always copies), so the zero-copy route through `vtkCellArray.SetData(numpy_to_vtkIdTypeArray(..., deep=False))` + `grid.SetCells(...)` is **mandatory, not a preference**. Blocks VTK cannot express (`polyhedron`, `custom`, unmapped) are dropped-with-a-note, never an exception. **Regions**: Point/Cell export as int8 `region:<name>` masks **plus** a JSON sidecar (`meshioplusplus:regions` in `grid.field_data` / trimesh's `metadata`) carrying `dim`/`tag`, which a mask cannot — without it a gmsh physical tag would not survive; `Side` regions are dropped by name (no VTK `(cell, facet)` concept). **trimesh** is triangles-only and composes the *existing* ops (`extract_surface` → `convert_cells("linearize")` → `convert_cells("simplexify")`), needs `process=False` (the default merges vertices and silently renumbers), and gets **no second region policy** — it inherits those ops' documented behaviour. **Arrow/Parquet** is a **tabular data export, NOT a mesh format**: deliberately not in `registry.cpp`/`_helpers`, so `convert mesh.vtu out.parquet` does not work; multi-component arrays are `fixed_size_list` (never `_0`/`_1`/`_2` — the flat JS mesh representation taught that); `ChunkedArray.combine_chunks()` **copies even for a single chunk**, so `from_arrow` takes `chunk(0)` (measured, and `_arrow_shares` asserts sharing rather than assuming it). CLI: a tenth `data export IN OUT.parquet [--location point|cell]` sub-verb in `_cli/_data.py`, **Python CLI only** (a documented native-CLI gap). **pandas/polars** (v9.26.0, roadmap §1's first bullet): `to_pandas(mesh, location, zero_copy_only)`/`to_polars(mesh, location)` sit on the same `_to_table_payload` seam (which gained an `op` parameter for its error message) — pandas via the **pure** `_frame_columns(payload)` (testable with pandas absent), because pandas columns are 1-D and pandas **alone** flattens a multi-component array into suffixed `v_0`/`v_1`/`v_2` columns (first-produced name wins a collision, later dropped with a note; the grouping is recorded in `df.attrs["meshioplusplus:components"]` alongside the full payload metadata, so nothing is lost); polars keeps the true `(n,k)` shape as `pl.Array` columns and needs no suffix rule. `to_pandas` measures scalar sharing with `np.shares_memory` (built with `pd.DataFrame(..., copy=False)` — load-bearing, pandas ≥2.0 copies by default; no `_meshioplusplus_refs` needed since a shared column's `.base` chain keeps the capsule alive) and its expansion always copies, so `zero_copy_only=True` raises there pointing at `to_arrow`; **`to_polars` is the one deliberate exception to "every `to_*` takes `zero_copy_only`"** — polars always copies into its own Arrow-backed buffers, so the flag could only ever raise — and polars has no attrs/metadata slot, so the `meshioplusplus:*` metadata rides only the Arrow/pandas paths. There is deliberately **no `from_pandas`/`from_polars`** (`from_arrow` returns plain arrays; a frame is one dict comprehension away). Extras `[pyvista]`/`[trimesh]`/`[arrow]`/`[pandas]`/`[polars]`/`[interop]`, kept **out of `[all]`** (which means "the optional deps the *formats* need" — h5py/netCDF4; `[viewer]`/`[kahip]`/`[codecs]` are the precedent). CI: the `interop` job in `ci.yml` (plain pip + `libgl1` for the VTK wheel's import); the per-backend C++ matrix is untouched. Open3D/DOLFINx are Phase 2 stubs raising `NotImplementedError` — the constraints (Open3D's copying `Vector3dVector` and ~400 MB wheel; DOLFINx's single-cell-type limit, `ufl`/`basix` domain, MPI communicator, VTK→basix node permutation via `cell_perm_gmsh`, and conda/apt-only install) are recorded in `doc/interop.md`.

**Blender bridge** (`src/python/meshioplusplus/_blender.py` + the 4.2+ extension in `src/blender/`, docs [`doc/blender.md`](doc/blender.md), v10.21.0 — closed roadmap §3's first bullet): `to_blender(mesh)` -> a `bpy.types.Mesh` datablock, `from_blender(obj_or_mesh)` back, `has_blender()` the predicate. **Python-only over the numpy the readers already return — the C++/WASM/C/Fortran core is untouched.** Same pure/gated split as `_interop.py` and `mcp/`: the whole behaviour is `_to_blender_payload` + `_mesh_from_blender_arrays` (no `bpy` import at all, tested in the default matrix), and the two public functions are thin wrappers importing `bpy` inside. **n-gons are KEPT, and that is the design's one genuine difference from `to_trimesh`** — a `Trimesh` holds triangles only, so `_interop._triangulate` ends in `convert_cells("simplexify")`; Blender's verts+loops+polygons model is `vtkPolyData`-shaped and holds n-gons natively, so simplexifying would destroy exactly the quad topology a hex-dominant import came for.
