The reduction is therefore **`_viewer_browser._renderable_surface`, CALLED not copied** (its docstring binds it to the WASM `convertSurface` twin that "must stay in step"; a second caller costs nothing, a second implementation would move that contract out of the file documenting it) — so a mesh looks identical in Blender, `view()` and the hosted demo, and the `record_parent_ids` gather means a solid arrives coloured by its per-cell material. `ops` is rebuilt from the *same two predicates* that function branches on rather than by changing its signature; the volume branch checks only `dim == 3` blocks, since `extract_surface` never emits a supplied 2-D block. **The insight that removed most of the new code**: Blender's loop layout IS VTK 9's cell array renamed (`loop_vertices`=connectivity, `loop_total`=per-cell count, `loop_start`=offset), so the forward path reuses `_interop._vtk_block_conn` (which already handles the ragged-`polygon` Python-list shape) and the inverse reuses `_vtk_common.vtk_cells_from_data` — the same function `from_pyvista` uses. That function splits on **runs** of equal cell type, so the inverse first stable-sorts polygons by side count; without it an alternating tri/quad mesh returns one `CellBlock` per cell. Regions ride `_interop`'s `REGION_PREFIX` masks + `REGION_META_KEY` sidecar **verbatim**, so a region survives `to_pyvista` -> `from_blender`. **There is deliberately no `[blender]` extra**: `bpy` on PyPI is a several-hundred-MB wheel pinned to one CPython minor, and inside Blender `bpy` is a builtin pip must never touch — so the gate is `_gpu._require_framework`, which gained a backward-compatible **`qualifier=`** kwarg (defaulted so every torch/jax/physicsnemo message stays byte-identical; pinned by a test) because its hard-coded "(pick the wheel matching your accelerator)" is wrong here. No `zero_copy_only` either — Blender stores coordinates as float32 and derives loops/polygons, so the flag could only ever raise (`to_polars`' precedent). Three traps worth not rediscovering: `from __future__ import annotations` **must not** appear in `addon/operators.py`, because Blender registers properties by reading `__annotations__` and under PEP 563 every one becomes a string and registration silently yields an operator with no properties; pyflakes reads string literals *inside* annotations as forward references, so that one file carries a scoped `per-file-ignores = F821, F722` in `.flake8`; and `blender_manifest.toml.in`'s own header comment must not contain the substitution tokens, since they are replaced everywhere in the file, comments included (found by reading a generated manifest). **`blender_manifest.toml` is GENERATED, not committed** — the bundled wheel *file names* carry the version, so a hand-maintained `wheels = [...]` would rot on the next bump; this keeps the version-file list at ten and the `blender-packaging` CI job asserts manifest-version == pyproject-version and manifest-wheels == zip-contents instead (sabotage-verified to fire on a wrong version, a missing wheel and a missing source file). **`rich` is bundled and numpy is not**: `__init__.py:2` imports `_cli` eagerly so rich is a hard runtime dep, while a second numpy ahead of Blender's own breaks `bpy`'s numpy interop. The `blender` CI job pins **Python 3.11**, not 3.12 — the `bpy` wheel is built against the exact CPython minor Blender embeds. `to_blender`/`from_blender`/`has_blender` are in `_NOT_TOOLS` (in-memory handoffs with no path form), and the add-on needed one small public addition rather than the ParaView plugin's reach into private registries: **`meshioplusplus.formats()`** (`_helpers.py`), which `mcp/_tools.formats_payload()` now delegates to and `tool_formats` *claims* in its `wraps`, so the parity guard is satisfied by a claim and not an exemption. **The notebook rule is deliberately not honoured here** — notebooks are re-executed in CI and `bpy` is a huge CPython-pinned dependency — so this follows the PhysicsNeMo precedent with a separately-run `example/blender/` directory instead.

**GPU handoff** (`src/python/meshioplusplus/_gpu.py`, docs [`doc/gpu.md`](doc/gpu.md)): moves a mesh's arrays to/from device memory through the standard exchange protocols — **DLPack primary** (`to_dlpack` returns a frozen `DevicePayload` of host numpy arrays, each natively speaking `__dlpack__`/`__dlpack_device__`; DLPack was chosen because it is framework-neutral AND covers host arrays (`kDLCPU`), so one export path serves CPU and GPU consumers and the host half is fully CI-testable with no GPU), `__cuda_array_interface__` (CAI) **consumed** on ingest and exposed for free by the CuPy arrays `to_cupy` returns. **Python-only over the numpy the readers already return — the C++/WASM/C/Fortran core is untouched and stays dependency-free.** Same pure-payload split as `_interop.py` (whose `InteropError`/`_copy_note`/`_derived_note`/`_prepare_data_array`/`_emit`/`_importable` it reuses — one data policy, not two): the bulk is `_to_device_payload` (no third-party imports), `to_cupy`/`from_cupy` are thin gated wrappers. **The honesty rule (non-negotiable): a host→device move is always a bus transfer** — the feature removes the file round-trip and every *extra* copy around that one transfer; "zero-copy" is used only for host buffer sharing (`to_dlpack` honors `zero_copy_only` — the existing HOST contract, unmuddied) and on-device adoption; `to_cupy` deliberately takes **no** `zero_copy_only` (a lie on a 100%-copy path) and `from_cupy` performs one deliberate, noted device→host copy per array (its ladder: numpy as-is → `np.from_dlpack` for any `kDLCPU` exporter, no CuPy needed → `.get()` → CuPy CAI/DLPack adoption + one copy back). Payload shape: `cells` = per-block `(type, conn)` (ragged blocks dropped-with-note), **cell_data stays per-block** and `block_bases` (from `_regions.block_bases`, never re-derived) rides along so consumers concatenate block-major themselves; **regions ride as plain `(name, kind, dim, tag, entries)` index arrays, NOT the mask+sidecar convention** — that convention exists only because VTK/trimesh have no structured region slot, and index arrays are lossless here (`Side` `(cell,facet)` pairs survive — the only interop target that keeps them; cell/side entries renumbered at export when a ragged block dropped). Dtypes stay canonical float64/int64; `float32=True`/`int32=True` are explicit noted opt-ins, int32 range-checked (`InteropError`, never wrapped). Optional pinned staging (`pinned=True`, `stream=` for async DMA — staging buffers kept on `payload.refs`, caller must sync); **the C++ half of reading directly into pinned memory landed in v8.5.0**: `meshioplusplus::set_buffer_allocator` (`ndarray.hpp`) installs a process-global `BufferAllocator` (plain C callbacks) consulted at every owning-`NDArray` allocation — each buffer pins the allocator it was born with via its own `shared_ptr`, so uninstalling never orphans live buffers and the numpy capsule handoff needs no change (the buffer's deleter fires inside `~NDArray`); copies allocate through the *current* hook (pinned-ness doesn't propagate), views are unaffected; gtested in `tests/cpp/test_ndarray_allocator.cpp`. The Python/CuPy wiring (`pinned_reads()` context manager + `to_cupy(pinned=True)` fast path — GIL in both callbacks, `Py_IsInitialized()` guard on the free path) is the recorded Phase-2 follow-up (doc/gpu.md). **Packaging finding: there is deliberately NO `[gpu]` pip extra** — CuPy wheels are CUDA-version-specific (`cupy-cuda13x`/`cupy-cuda12x`/`cupy-cuda11x`/ROCm), so a pinned extra would break for most users; `_gpu._require_cupy` raises naming the wheel recipe (deliberately not `_interop._require`, whose `pip install meshioplusplus[...]` phrasing is baked in), and `has_cupy` (importable) is distinct from `has_cuda_device` (a device actually answers — CuPy imports fine with no GPU and fails at first use). **CI has no GPU**: `tests/python/test_gpu.py`'s always-on half (pure payloads + the DLPack host round-trip via `np.from_dlpack`, sharing measured not assumed) runs in the default matrix, the coverage job and the `interop` job; the gated device half (`importorskip("cupy")` + `getDeviceCount() > 0`) is **not covered by public CI** and its CuPy-only lines read as uncovered on the Codecov patch check by design — documented, not excluded. **`to_torch`/`to_jax`** (v9.27.0, roadmap §1's tensor handoff) live here too: thin adopters over `_to_device_payload` returning a `DevicePayload` of framework tensors — `to_torch` host adoption is genuinely zero-copy via `torch.from_dlpack` (measured), `device=` does one recorded `.to(device)` transfer per array; `to_jax` places on JAX's default device and falls back to `jnp.asarray` (counted + warned once) where `jax_enable_x64=False` rejects 64-bit adoption, with **no `device=`** (placement belongs to JAX). **No `[torch]`/`[jax]` extra, deliberately** — torch's default Linux wheel is ~900 MB of bundled CUDA and JAX's accelerator variants live in its own extras, so `_require_framework` raises naming `pip install torch`/`pip install jax` (the `_require_cupy` shape, not `_interop._require`); `has_torch`/`has_jax` are the predicates, and like CuPy the adoption tests `importorskip` and are not exercised by public CI.

**ML data handling** (`src/python/meshioplusplus/_ml.py`, docs [`doc/ml.md`](doc/ml.md), v9.27.0 — closed roadmap §1 together with `to_torch`/`to_jax` above and v9.26.0's frames): `edge_index`, `feature_matrix` and `write_dataset`, all pure Python over existing machinery — no C++/binding change. **`edge_index(mesh, kind="node"|"cell", undirected=True)`** → `(2, E)` C-contiguous int64 in the PyG/DGL layout: the node graph reuses `_smooth._REFINE_EDGES` (imported, `_smooth._edge_graph` untouched — its byte-identity contract with the C++ core must not be risked) with polygon blocks routed to the closed-ring rule **by type name, never raggedness** (a uniform n-gon stores rectangularly — the `cgns.cpp:476` lesson), polyhedron/unknown types contributing nothing with one warning; the cell dual reuses `_partition._dual_graph` (facet-sharing, global block-major ids, ragged raises). `undirected=True` (default) emits both directions lexsorted — deterministic, pinned by a repeated-runs test. **`feature_matrix(mesh, location, fields=None, coords=True, regions=True)`** → frozen `FeatureMatrix(matrix (N,F) float64, columns tuple, schema dict)`; the **column order is the versioned contract** (`FEATURE_SCHEMA_VERSION`): coords → data arrays (sorted, or the explicit `fields` order; multi-component expanded via `_interop._frame_columns` — the pandas suffix rule, ONE rule repo-wide) → `region:<name>` one-hots in `Region.key` order; the cell table's structural `block`/`cell_type`/`cell` columns are identifiers and never included; `schema["sources"]` describes every final column. **`write_dataset(source, path, location, format="parquet"|"zarr"|"hdf5", mesh_id="stem"|"index")`** → a `mesh_id`-keyed dataset from any `sequence_entries` source (glob/list/multi-step file), reading via the same per-entry `read(..., time_step=)` loop as `read_sequence` so the **streaming invariant (one mesh alive) is preserved**; the **schema is strict** — first mesh defines columns+dtypes, mismatch is a named error, never a union — and a failed run leaves **no manifest** (`abort()`, a partial dataset must not look complete). Parquet = hive `path/mesh_id=<id>/data.parquet` (each file is `to_arrow`'s output + a `mesh_id` column + per-file metadata) + a `meshioplusplus_dataset.json` manifest (`DATASET_LAYOUT_VERSION` 1); zarr/hdf5 = one subgroup per mesh with one chunked dataset per **numeric** column (the object-dtype `cell_type` column is dropped-with-warning, its info in the `cell_types` attr — deliberate, avoids the variable-string dtype portability swamp) + the manifest in root attrs; `_zarr_write_array` bridges the zarr-python 2.x (`group.array`) / 3.x (`create_array`) API split, extra `zarr = ["zarr>=2.16"]` (h5py rides `[all]`, parquet rides `[arrow]`). Surfaces: CLI `data export-dataset` (Python CLI only, `_cli/_data.py`), MCP `export_dataset` (gated `arrow`, `_resolve_pattern`-sandboxed glob), `edge_index`/`feature_matrix`/`FeatureMatrix`/`has_zarr` + the `_gpu` four in `_NOT_TOOLS` (in-memory). CI: the `interop` job installs `.[interop,zarr]` and runs `test_ml.py`; the coverage job's pip line carries zarr for the Codecov patch check.

**Dataset manifests** (`src/python/meshioplusplus/_dataset.py`, docs [`doc/datasets.md`](doc/datasets.md), v9.28.0): `DatasetManifest`/`DatasetEntry` — a **hand-editable** settings-family JSON (PascalCase keys, `"Version": 1`, strict unknown-key errors naming the offender, error prefix `meshio++: dataset:`) cataloguing many solution outputs for ML training: each entry records the **TimeSeries plan inputs** (`Source`: exactly one of `Pattern`/`Path`/`Paths`, plus `Format`/`Times`/`TimeFrom`/`Sort` — `Sort` valid only with `Paths`), a `Split`, `Tags`, a slash-separated `Group`, `Notes` and an open `Metadata` object. **Two rules are load-bearing**: the manifest is the single source of truth (CLI, MCP and a text editor all read/write the same file; serialization is stable `indent=2` + trailing newline so hand edits and tool edits interleave — pinned by a hand-edit-between-CLI-calls test), and entries record plan inputs, **never the resolved plan** (resolved paths would be a hidden cache — the roadmap's own requirement). Relative sources resolve against **the manifest file's directory** (`base_dir`, captured at `load`, re-anchored by `save`; dict/JSON-text loads use the CWD; entries carry it as a `compare=False` field so it never enters equality or the document), which is what makes a campaign directory move as one portable unit — and why the CLI/MCP `add` store sources `os.path.relpath`'d to the manifest dir. `load` is tri-modal (`_resolve_settings`' dict | JSON text | path rule); `add(source, validate_source=True)` expands the plan once so an empty glob fails at add time by name; `assign_splits(fractions, seed, by_group)` is a deterministic `random.Random(seed)` shuffle of the sorted ids (fractions must sum to 1; `by_group` assigns whole groups — the leakage guard); `entries(split=, tags=, group=)` filters exact/all-of/path-segment-prefix respectively; `entry.time_series(**read_kwargs)` maps `Source` 1:1 onto `TimeSeries`. Deliberately **separate from `write_dataset`'s `meshioplusplus_dataset.json`** (machine-written snake_case output artefact vs hand-edited PascalCase input; the relationship is directional only). Surfaces: exported `DatasetManifest`/`DatasetEntry` (after the `"_cli"` sentinel; `DatasetEntry` in `_NOT_TOOLS`), the **second nested CLI group `dataset`** (`_cli/_dataset.py`: `add`/`list`/`split`/`tag`/`annotate`, the `_data.py` wiring verbatim, `list --json` per `_regions.py`; Python CLI only like `data export`), and three **ungated** MCP tools `dataset_add`/`dataset_list`/`dataset_update` (`dataset_add` claims `wraps=("DatasetManifest",)`; sandbox covers the manifest path AND every path it names or resolves — a hand-edited manifest is client input too, the pipeline-tool rule, pinned by an outside-root refusal test). Pure stdlib+numpy — tests (`tests/python/test_dataset.py`) run in the default matrix. **The browser dataset manager** (v9.29.0) is a fourth surface over the same JSON — see the browser-viewer entry below for its strict TS twin and serialization-parity rules.

**PhysicsNeMo adapter** (`src/python/meshioplusplus/physicsnemo/`, docs [`doc/physicsnemo.md`](doc/physicsnemo.md), v9.28.0 — closed roadmap §1's remaining non-UI items; worked example `example/physicsnemo/`): meshes → the tensors NVIDIA PhysicsNeMo's datapipes expect, over `feature_matrix`/`edge_index`/the manifest — no C++/binding change. **The `mcp/` split applied again, and the subpackage is deliberately NOT imported by `import meshioplusplus`** (the `mcp` precedent): PhysicsNeMo's floor is Python ≥3.11 against the package's 3.8, and a class subclassing an optional-dep base can't live in an eagerly imported module — `__init__.py` is pure numpy (ALL behaviour), `_reader.py` is the only module importing `physicsnemo`, `_pyg.py` the only one importing `torch_geometric`, both reached via lazy `make_reader`/`make_dataset` factories. **There is deliberately no `[physicsnemo]` extra** — `nvidia-physicsnemo` hard-depends on `torch>=2.10` (the exact wheel pyproject.toml:73-75 refuses to pin); errors go through `_gpu._require_framework` (which gained a backward-compatible `doc=` kwarg) naming `pip install nvidia-physicsnemo`/`pip install torch_geometric`, pinned by a `meshioplusplus[`-absent message test. Recon facts the design stands on (verified 2026-08-06 against 2.1.1, recorded in the doc): **DGL is gone, PyG is the only graph backend**; two datapipe generations coexist and the sanctioned Gen-2 extension point is the two-method `Reader` ABC (`_load_sample(i) -> dict[str, Tensor]` + `__len__`; `@register()` under try/except — Hydra addressability is a bonus, not a pinned contract); the stable edge-feature convention is `cat((pos[row]-pos[col], ‖·‖))` (source minus destination — owned by `graph_sample`, pinned by a hand-computed test); Gen-1 stats files are `node_stats.json`/`edge_stats.json` with `{field}_mean`/`{field}_std` keys (what `field_stats`/`edge_stats` emit, streaming, one mesh alive). `graph_sample(mesh, fields=, target_fields=, target_mesh=, target_delta=, target_offset=, regions=, kind=, undirected=, edge_features=, float32=True)` → frozen `GraphSample(arrays, x_columns, y_columns, schema)`: `pos` never folds into `x` (MGN convention), `x`/`y` are `feature_matrix(coords=False)` (region one-hots = the node-type slot), `kind="cell"` coherently switches pos/x/y to centroids (`_partition._centroids`) and cell-located data; `float32=True` is the training-convention default, `edge_index` stays int64. **v9.30.0 closed both v9.28.0 deferrals.** (1) **t→t+1 pairing**: `target_offset=n` (on `iter_samples`/`make_reader`/`make_dataset`) pairs step k with k+n — the shared `_read_sample(series, step, graph_kwargs)` helper is the single owner of the read-and-sample step across all three consumers (it replaced a triplicated `series[step]` line), each entry contributes `len(series)−n` samples with a one-per-entry warning when too short (at index-build, never per epoch), and a paired sample honestly costs **two reads / at-most-two-meshes-alive** (TimeSeries caches nothing — stated, not cached away); `target_delta=True` makes y the increment (MGN convention), normalized by `field_stats(delta=)` emitting `{field}_diff_mean/_diff_std` (identity checks on the `delta` flag — `0 == False` in Python, so `delta=0` must raise by name, not silently mean "off"); a remeshed series fails the row-count check by name; the schema gained `target_offset`/`target_delta` so `GRAPH_SAMPLE_VERSION` bumped 1→2 (stored v1 schemas now compare unequal — the drift guard working, called out in the CHANGELOG). (2) **The `physicsnemo.mesh.Mesh` bridge** `to_physicsnemo(mesh, manifold_dim="auto", float32=True)`/`from_physicsnemo(pm)` — built despite the recorded deferral (user decision), gated + pinned `>=2.1,<2.2`, risk stated: the target holds exactly ONE simplex kind (cells tensor's trailing dim; mixed dims are "separate Mesh objects" upstream), so the pure `_to_physicsnemo_payload` selects one topological dimension, tessellates via the existing `convert_cells` linearize→simplexify (cell_data replicates to children natively), drops other-dim blocks/regions/non-numeric data **with notes** (surfaced via `_interop._emit` → the rich-console `warn`, which is NOT the `warnings` module — tests assert on stderr via `capsys`, never `pytest.warns`), and returns freshly-owned buffers (upstream in-place edits invalidate its caches — never share memory); `_mesh.py` is the ONLY module importing `physicsnemo.mesh` (`import physicsnemo` pulls NVIDIA Warp, ~1.5 s — the lazy-import reason) and touches only the tensorclass constructor + public attributes, never `.pmsh`; exports live in the subpackage `__all__` only, so the MCP parity guard (top-level `__all__` only) is untouched by construction. **Training uses the PyG path deliberately** (PyG's DataLoader batches ragged graphs natively; TensorDict has no such convention — stated, not implied); one empirical finding from executing the example: **MeshGraphNet's GNN layers import `torch_scatter` directly** (part of the `gnns` extra), whose prebuilt wheels lag torch releases — none for torch 2.13 at the time, so the example pins torch 2.12+cu130 and documents the wheel-index install. The example (200 manufactured Poisson `q=-∆T` cases → `preprocess.json` sequence-pipeline recipe → manifest via the real `dataset` CLI → MeshGraphNet training → `T_pred`/`T_error` written back as `point_data`) was executed on a real GPU (RTX 2000 Ada, 100 epochs/73.8 s, mean test RMSE 0.0040) with stats/renders committed; scripts not notebooks, since training cannot ride the notebook re-execution rule. **Public CI installs neither torch nor physicsnemo** (ci.yml:725-727 precedent): the pure half (`tests/python/test_physicsnemo.py`) runs in the default matrix, the gated halves `importorskip`.

**MCP server** (`src/python/meshioplusplus/mcp/`, docs [`doc/mcp.md`](doc/mcp.md)): exposes the whole Python surface to AI agents over the Model Context Protocol — 57 tools mirroring the CLI verbs (inspection: `formats`/`sniff`/`info`/`stats`/`quality`/`data_info`/`regions`/`bandwidth`/`data_preview`/`diff`; `convert` with `mode`/`compression` params subsuming the CLI's ascii/binary/compress/decompress verbs via the same writer-kwargs mapping; all 20 path-based mesh operations; `data_manage`/`data_convert`/`data_calc`/`data_condition`; gated `data_export` (`[arrow]`) and `screenshot` (`[viewer]`, returned as MCP image content); plus `pipeline`, which runs a whole settings.json and `_resolve`-sandboxes the paths *inside* the document, not just the settings file; plus `sequence` for a transient/multi-file run, whose `_resolve_pattern` containment-checks a glob's *directory* component before expanding it -- `_resolve`'s `os.path.isfile` rejects a pattern outright, and the directory is the obvious sandbox escape; plus the three ungated `dataset_add`/`dataset_list`/`dataset_update` manifest tools, v9.28.0 — see the dataset-manifests entry above) plus two resources (`meshioplusplus://formats`, `://version`). **Stateless and file-path based by design** — input path(s) → public-API call → output path(s) → strict-JSON report; no mesh lives between calls, so arbitrarily large meshes never enter the protocol. Same pure/gated split as `_interop.py` and for the same two reasons: `_tools.py` (ALL behaviour: the `_resolve` sandbox, the `_json_safe` sanitizer — numpy→lists, NaN/Inf→`None` with a `non_finite_replaced` count, arrays >1000 elements → `{"truncated", "preview", ...}` wrappers — and one `tool_*` function per tool) imports nothing beyond meshioplusplus+numpy+stdlib and runs in the default CI matrix on every supported Python, while `_server.py` is the only module importing the `mcp` SDK — whose **own floor is Python ≥3.10** against the package's 3.8, the second reason the split is load-bearing, not stylistic. `_server.py`'s typed wrappers contribute exactly the JSON schemas (from annotations) and tool descriptions (docstrings), never behaviour, and every call is guarded into `{"error", "error_type"}` payloads — a tool never surfaces a traceback. `TOOL_REGISTRY` in `_tools.py` is the single source of truth (ordered name → `{fn, wraps, gated}`): `_server` registers from it and the **parity guard** (`test_mcp.py::test_every_operation_has_a_tool`) asserts every operation in `meshioplusplus.__all__` (minus the formats prefix and the explicit `_NOT_TOOLS` exemptions — in-memory interop/GPU, `view`, classes, plumbing) is claimed by some tool's `wraps`, so API/server drift is a red build (see "Keeping docs in sync"). Path sandbox: default unrestricted, opt-in `--root DIR` / `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_MCP_ROOT` with realpath containment. Entry points: console script `meshioplusplus-mcp` → `meshioplusplus.mcp:main` (deliberately NOT `._server:main`, so a missing extra prints the named `pip install meshioplusplus[mcp]` error instead of an ImportError traceback) and `python -m meshioplusplus.mcp`; stdio transport only. Extra `mcp = ["mcp>=1.2,<2"]`, NOT in `[all]` (the interop precedent). **The `<2` upper bound is load-bearing, not caution**: the SDK's 2.0.0 removed `mcp.server.fastmcp` outright, replacing it with a different server API (`mcp.server.mcpserver`), so `_server.py`'s `from mcp.server.fastmcp import FastMCP` raises `ModuleNotFoundError` there. It was an unbounded `>=1.2` that let the `mcp` CI job resolve 2.0.0 and go red on an unrelated PR -- and note that only a **fresh non-editable** `pip install ".[mcp]"` (what that job does) reproduces it, since an existing venv keeps whatever 1.x it already resolved. Porting to the 2.x API is a separate piece of work; `_tools.py` imports no SDK and is unaffected either way. One ergonomic addition over the raw API: `data_calc` accepts the CLI's `"NAME = EXPR"` spelling (split on the FIRST `=`, the documented colon rule). CI: the `mcp` job (below); the default matrix runs the pure half with the SDK absent.

**Browser viewer** (`src/viewer/`, Vite + **TypeScript** + `@kitware/vtk.js`): a web app that **CONSUMES the published `@meshioplusplus/wasm` package**, not the core — vtk.js/Vite/Playwright are isolated under `src/viewer/`'s own `package.json` and never touch the root or `src/wasm/`. Two builds from one source: `build:web` (the Pages demo, WASM worker + file picker) and `build:embed` (a single self-contained file shipped in the wheel, where the WASM client is **aliased to a stub** — dead-code elimination alone is not enough, since Vite's worker plugin emits `new Worker(new URL(...))` chunks at *transform* time). Deployed by **`docs.yml`** as a second static island at `/viewer/` beside Doxygen's `/api/` — a repo gets one Pages deployment, so a separate workflow would fight it over the `pages` concurrency group. That job must run emsdk + `./build/configure-wasm.sh --build` first, because `src/wasm/dist/` is gitignored. **npm *copies* a `file:` dependency and a later `npm install` does not refresh the copy**, so `vite.config.mjs` compares both the wrapper's exports and the installed `.wasm` against `src/wasm/dist/` at `buildStart` and fails with the `rm -rf node_modules/@meshioplusplus` recipe; CI is immune because it builds then runs `npm ci`. Viewer samples and fixtures carry a **Git LFS exemption** (`.gitattributes`) — an LFS-less checkout would otherwise hand the app 130-byte pointer files. Playwright asserts on the documented `window.__viewerState` hook rather than pixels, so it needs no GPU. Its specs live at `tests/viewer/` (unit tests at `tests/viewer/unit/`), not under `src/viewer/`, per the repo-wide test unification — but they still need `src/viewer/`'s own `node_modules` (`@playwright/test`, `esbuild`) to resolve, and Node's ESM resolver only walks up from the *importing file's* location, which `tests/` isn't an ancestor of. `src/viewer/tools/link-test-deps.mjs` (run via `postinstall`) symlinks `tests/viewer/node_modules` to `src/viewer/node_modules` for the Playwright specs' bare `@playwright/test` imports; `test:unit`'s runner (`tests/viewer/unit/build-and-run.mjs`) instead resolves `esbuild` explicitly via `createRequire` rooted at `src/viewer/package.json`, since it only needs one resolved entry point rather than transparent resolution for every spec file. **Click-to-inspect** uses `vtkCellPicker` (CPU ray/cell, so renderer-independent) on click only — its listener must go on the **container**, not the canvas, because vtk.js takes pointer capture on the container and a canvas-bound `click` never fires at all; provenance comes from the `surface:parent_cell` that `convertSurfaceOps(keepProvenance: true)` retains and `scalars.ts` filters out of the colour-by menu. **The app runs meshio++'s own operations** (quality/clean/smooth/refine/partition/section/isosurface) through the single `convertSurfaceOps` binding: the panel owns the pipeline as a list, every change re-sends the *whole* list, and the worker replays it against the **original file bytes kept staged in MEMFS** — which is why `apply` deliberately never unlinks, why undo is exact rather than an inverse, and why multi-component data survives (no mesh re-enters JS). **TypeScript conventions**: `as any` is banned in `src/` and CI-grepped; gaps go in `vtk-augmentations.d.ts` (declaration merging, still type-checked) or `vtk-shims.ts` (named `as unknown as` helpers) — the augmentation file must **not** be named `vtk-shims.d.ts`, since TS would resolve it and `vtk-shims.ts` to one module, keep the `.ts`, and silently drop the declarations. `window.__viewerState` is typed in `src/types.ts` so a new field with un-updated tests is a compile error. Two vtk.js findings worth not rediscovering: `setInterpolateScalarsBeforeMapping(true)` renders a range spanning zero **entirely in the warm half** in 32.9.0 (measured: 0 blue px vs 275k red), so it is off; and the coincident-topology polygon offset belongs on the **edge** mapper, because a negative offset moves toward the viewer and putting it on the body hides the wireframe behind its own surface. `vtkClipClosedSurface` is unusable for a coloured cut (its point-data interpolation is commented out upstream), which is why the section goes through meshio++'s `crop_plane` + re-skin instead. Regenerate the wheel asset with `npm run build:embed && cp dist-embed/index.html ../python/meshioplusplus/_viewer_assets/viewer.html`; the CI check on it is **structural (10% size drift), never a `git diff`** — Rollup output is not reproducible across npm patch releases. **The dataset-manager page** (`dataset.html` + `src/dataset/`, v9.29.0, docs [`doc/datasets.md`](doc/datasets.md#curating-in-the-browser)): a SECOND web-build page curating `DatasetManifest` JSONs — `rollupOptions.input` lists both pages **only when `!embedded`** (the embed build keeps its single implicit entry; CI's `ls dist-embed | wc -l == 1` is the guard, and the embed bundle's bytes are untouched since the page's CSS/markup are separate files and the worker client is aliased out). `src/dataset/manifest.ts` is the **strict TS twin of `_dataset.py`** (unknown-key refusal with the same `meshio++: dataset:` messages; `stringifyManifest` reproduces `DatasetManifest.save`'s bytes — `JSON.stringify(x, null, 2)` matches Python's `json.dump(indent=2)` layout element-for-element, plus a `\uXXXX` escape pass for `ensure_ascii` parity and a trailing newline — pinned byte-for-byte against a Python-`save()`d fixture in `tests/viewer/unit/manifest.test.mjs`; the one documented gap is JS's missing int/float split, so a Python `0.0` normalizes to `0` once). `assign_splits` is deliberately NOT in the UI (its `random.Random(seed)` shuffle cannot be reproduced in JS — a "same seed, different assignment" trap; the CLI keeps it). Worker: four **additive** request kinds (`stageEntry`/`previewStep`/`summaryStep`/`evictEntry`) beside the untouched viewer `session` — staging preserves directory structure under `/data/e<n>/` (unlike `open`'s `/`→`_` flattening; `..`/absolute segments rejected), the plan comes from `sequenceEntries` (the wasm twin of `DatasetEntry.entries()` — the single ordering/times authority), a multi-step file is fanned out ONCE via `timeseriesToSequence` because `convertSurfaceOps` has no step selector (scrubbing then costs one cheap single-file render per tick), summaries are `readMeshSelective`+`dataInfo` (mesh-taking is fine for scalar aggregates, never a rendering path), and exactly ONE entry is resident (MEMFS never reclaims — `removeTree` eviction before each stage). File access (`src/dataset/fs.ts`): FSA `showDirectoryPicker`+`createWritable` (in-place save, Chromium) with a `webkitdirectory`+download-blob fallback; the picked directory IS the manifest's `base_dir` so relative sources resolve like Python's. Tests: `tests/viewer/unit/{manifest,glob,suggest}.test.mjs` + `tests/viewer/dataset.spec.mjs` asserting on the typed `window.__datasetState` (fallback flow driven by Playwright's directory `setInputFiles`; the FSA flow by an `addInitScript` mock — **init scripts do not run for `setContent`, only real navigations**, learned by spike). `REQUIRED_WASM_API` grew by `sequenceEntries`/`timeseriesToSequence`/`readMeshSelective`/`dataInfo` — no wasm rebuild, all four were already bound and forwarded.

**Docs (VitePress):** `cd doc && npm install && npm run docs:build` (dev: `npm run docs:dev`). `doc/.vitepress/config.mts` groups the sidebar by theme (Introduction / Concepts / Reading and writing / Mesh operations in five sub-groups / Data operations / Pipelines and tools / Integrations / Language surfaces / Project / Formats) — a new page goes into its group, never appended to a flat list — and its `srcExclude` keeps the source-directory READMEs (`icons/`, `logo/`, `diagrams/`) from building as pages. The `head` carries Open Graph/Twitter-card tags naming `doc/public/social-preview.png` (a copy of `doc/logo/social-preview.png`), and the edit link points at `master`, the repository's default branch (a `main` link 404s — the README's raw-URL images had that exact defect until v10.21.x's doc pass). [`doc/architecture.md`](doc/architecture.md) is the overview page: one C++ core, six surfaces, the tools on them, with every box linked to its owning page; a new surface, integration or operation group belongs on it.

**Doxygen API reference** (`doc/Doxyfile`): C++/C API docs generated from `src/cpp/include` + `bindings/c/include` (`EXTRACT_ALL=YES`, no third-party headers in scope). `docs.yml` runs `doxygen Doxyfile` (from `doc/`, needs `apt-get install doxygen`) into the gitignored `doc/doxygen-build/html`, then copies it into the VitePress build as `doc/.vitepress/dist/api/` after `docs:build` — a separate static HTML island, not VitePress-rendered pages, linked from the nav/sidebar as "API (Doxygen)". Reproduce locally: `cd doc && doxygen Doxyfile && npm run docs:build && cp -r doxygen-build/html .vitepress/dist/api`, then `npm run docs:preview`.

**Icons** (`doc/icons/`): eighteen TikZ toolbar icons for `src/viewer/`, `tikz-ui/*.tex` → `pdflatex` → `dvisvgm` → `svg-ui/*.svg` → `build-icons.mjs` → the generated `src/viewer/src/ui/icons.ts` (an `IconId` union, so `tsc` checks every reference). Same toolchain as `doc/logo/build.sh`; **not** the sibling MDPA/CAD-Preview pipeline, which uses `pdftocairo` and therefore different post-processing regexes. Icons are drawn in three **sentinel colours** (`iconfg`/`iconfg60`/`iconfg30` in `preamble.tex`) mapped to `currentColor` at known opacities — drawing in black would mean guessing dvisvgm's encoding. The generator **strips every `id` attribute**: dvisvgm wraps output in `<g id='page1'>` and eighteen inlined icons would collide on `getElementById`, which is the app's entire test hook. `svg-ui/*.svg` is committed so the TS regenerates without TeX; `doc/icons/check-icons.mjs` is the CI gate (in `lint`, no TeX, no build).

**Diagrams** (`doc/diagrams/`, docs [`doc/diagrams/README.md`](doc/diagrams/README.md)): the 30 explanatory figures under `doc/public/diagrams/` — the architecture overview, the mesh-backend/data-model/regions/CSR pictures, the pipeline/sequence/provenance/interop/data/ML flows, the ABI decision tree, the roadmap map, the seven cell-type node-ordering figures and the algorithm figures (skin hashing, 2-3/3-2 flips, subdivide, agglomerate, marching cases, BCC stuffing, refine masks, undo_green, slice-vs-crop) — are **all generated** by `python3 doc/diagrams/gen_diagrams.py` (standard library only, no numpy, no `meshioplusplus` import, so it runs in the `lint` job with no build) through one palette (`diaglib/palette.py`, the `dataviz` reference palette: C++ core blue, Python orange, C-ABI family violet, WASM/green-closure green, formats aqua, data yellow, regions magenta, refusal/red-cell red) and one deterministic SVG builder (`diaglib/svg.py`: two-decimal coordinates, fixed attribute order, no timestamps), each committed with a 2x PNG twin (`rsvg-convert`, falling back to `inkscape`/ImageMagick; PNG bytes are **not** pinned, only their existence and size, since rasterisers differ per machine). `--check` is the CI gate (in `lint`, beside `check-icons.mjs`): stale SVG, missing twin, a figure no page embeds, or a page naming a figure no function produces all fail by name, and `tests/python/test_doc_diagrams.py` pins the same plus the geometric contract. **The cell-type figures derive from the code's own tables, never transcribe them**: `diaglib/tables.py` reads `_skin._CELL_FACES`, `_surface._CELL_EDGES`, `_convert_cells._ELEVATE` and `_common.num_nodes_per_cell` with `ast` (they have package-relative imports) and loads `_refine_templates.py` as a module (it imports only `itertools`); mid-edge nodes are the midpoints those tables name, face/body centres the centroids of `QUAD_FACES` rows, and the test cross-checks every drawn node against them and pins the `ast` copies equal to the live package (`importorskip`, runs wherever `_core` is importable). The only hand-typed geometry is each linear element's corner positions, harvested from the orphaned `doc/cell_types.tex` that these figures superseded (deleted; it was wired into no build and needed a TeX toolchain). **Dark-mode rule**: every figure sits on an explicit light paper rectangle — an `<img>`-loaded SVG cannot see VitePress's `.dark` class and a `prefers-color-scheme` query would follow the OS, not the site toggle — so figures are self-contained and read identically on the dark theme, in the PNG, on GitHub and in a notebook. One convention worth stating because the figures made it visible: `pyramid14` is drawn as `pyramid13` plus a base-centre node 13, which is what `detail/cell_faces.hpp`, `_skin.py` and the CGNS table all assume, while the gmsh reader (`gmsh/common.py`, `gmsh.cpp`) permutes only `pyramid13` and passes gmsh's edge-lexicographic type 14 through unpermuted — recorded as a gap in `doc/roadmap.md` §9, not fixed in the docs pass. Adding a figure = a function in `figures/*.py` + its `FIGURES` entry + an `![alt](/diagrams/<name>.svg)` embed in a page (the README uses the raw-URL form on `master`), then regenerate and commit SVG + PNG.

**Logo:** built with TikZ — the **Stanford Bunny** (`example/Bunny.stl`,
"Stanford Bunny — Digitized!" by MakerBot, CC-BY, thingiverse thing:88208),
rendered by meshio++ itself. `doc/logo/build.sh` runs `doc/logo/gen_logo_tikz.py`
(reads the STL with meshioplusplus, decimates to ~900 faces with PyVista,
projects through `meshioplusplus._projection` — the same isometric camera +
painter's sort as the SVG/TikZ 3D writers — and colors faces blue→teal by
projected height → `doc/logo/_mesh_icon.tex`), compiles
`doc/logo/logo.tex`/`logo-icon.tex` with `pdflatex`, and converts to SVG via
`dvisvgm` (PNG via PyMuPDF), then `doc/logo/make_icon_assets.py` (PyMuPDF +
Pillow; best-effort, skipped with a warning if unavailable) derives
`doc/logo/logo-icon-square.png` (the icon padded into a square, transparent —
README badge + favicon source), `doc/public/favicon.ico` (16/32/48/64px,
written straight there), and `doc/logo/social-preview.png` (1280x640 banner on
white, for GitHub's repo social-preview image — Settings → General → Social
preview, upload-only, no API). Committed assets: `doc/logo/logo-with-text.svg`
(README banner + `doc/public/logo.svg`), `doc/logo/logo-icon.svg` (non-square,
docs favicon/nav) — after regenerating, re-copy the SVGs/PNGs into
`doc/public/` (`favicon.ico` lands there directly; `social-preview.png` must be copied too, since `doc/.vitepress/config.mts`'s Open Graph tags name it). The old blob generator
is in git history; the older pygmsh `doc/logo/logo.py` is superseded (see
`doc/logo/README.md`).

**Example notebooks** (`example/python/*.ipynb`): read the bundled `example/example.msh`
with meshio++ and render/convert it (PyVista off-screen via a VTU round-trip,
matplotlib fallback). Committed **with outputs**; re-execute with
`PYVISTA_OFF_SCREEN=true jupyter nbconvert --to notebook --execute --inplace example/python/*.ipynb`.

**C++ example notebooks** (`example/cpp/*.ipynb`, docs [`example/cpp/README.md`](example/cpp/README.md)): the same tour called directly against the **C++ core** — no Python — on the [xeus-cpp](https://github.com/compiler-research/xeus-cpp) `clang-repl`-based Jupyter kernel (conda-forge only, no pip wheel; installed into a gitignored `.micromamba/` env, not `.venv`), against the committed [single-header amalgamation](doc/single_header.md). Renders go through meshio++'s own SVG writer (`mio_notebook.hpp`'s `render()`/`bar_chart()`/`histogram_chart()` helpers), never PyVista, cropping large meshes first since an SVG's size scales with facet count unlike a raster PNG (the `tools/gen_doc_images.py` lesson). Reads `example/cpp/example.vtu`, not `example/example.msh` directly — Gmsh 4.1's `$Entities` section was a C++-reader gap with no Python fallback available in a C++-only kernel (closed in v9.7.0; the `.vtu` is kept because the notebooks are committed with outputs); it was pre-converted once via the Python bindings (command in the README). Committed **with outputs**; re-execute per the README's micromamba + nbconvert recipe. Notable xeus-cpp/amalgamation gotchas (detailed in the README): cells share one persistent global namespace so top-level scratch variables must not collide with each other or with symbols the amalgamation itself declares (`namespace fs = std::filesystem;` bit this once); no auto-print of a cell's trailing expression, so rich output needs an explicit `xcpp::display(...)`; a capture-default lambda (`[&]`/`[=]`) cannot appear directly at a cell's top level (clang-repl rejects it); only 3 colormaps are built in (`viridis`/`coolwarm`/`turbo`); VTU/VTP's registry-default writer bakes in zlib, which the amalgamation doesn't compile in by default, so call `write_vtu`/`write_vtp` directly with `zlib=false`.

**Julia / R example notebooks** (`example/julia/*.ipynb` / `example/r/*.ipynb`, docs [`example/julia/README.md`](example/julia/README.md) / [`example/r/README.md`](example/r/README.md)): the same three-notebook tour (01 read/visualize, 02 convert/inspect, 03 the full ~20-section operations tour) called through the **`MeshioPlusPlus` Julia package** and **`meshioplusplus` R package** — no Python, no C++ core directly, both riding the installed flat C API exactly as Fortran does. Kernels: [IJulia](https://github.com/JuliaLang/IJulia.jl) (`julia -e 'using Pkg; Pkg.add("IJulia")'`, a standard system-wide install, no `--project` baked in — each notebook's first cell `Pkg.activate`s `bindings/julia/MeshioPlusPlus` itself) and [IRkernel](https://irkernel.github.io/) (installed alongside the R package into a `r-notebooks` env in the **same** gitignored `.micromamba/` root the C++ kernel uses — `micromamba create -n r-notebooks -c conda-forge r-base r-irkernel c-compiler`, the `c-compiler` package being required since conda-forge's `r-base` pulls in no compiler by default). Both consume a **prebuilt** `inst-nohdf5/` C API prefix (gitignored, `-DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_WITH_HDF5=OFF -DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_WITH_NETCDF=OFF`) — HDF5 off is **load-bearing for Julia specifically**: on Debian/Ubuntu a HDF5-enabled build links `libhdf5_openmpi`, which links the system `libcurl`, and an IJulia kernel subprocess's `dlopen` of that library can fail with `libcurl.so.4: version 'CURL_OPENSSL_4' not found` since Julia bundles its own incompatible `libcurl` (a real Debian/Ubuntu + Julia interaction, reproduced by toggling HDF5 on vs. off, not a meshio++ bug — this also means the `julia` CI job in `ci.yml` must build with HDF5 off, not just netCDF off, or it would likely fail the same way on real Ubuntu runners); R has no such conflict but reuses the same prefix so the `02_convert_and_inspect.ipynb` file-size comparisons stay apples-to-apples (XDMF's data format follows the build). Rendering mirrors the C++ notebooks' reasoning (no PyVista) but hits an *additional* constraint the C++ notebooks don't: **the flat C API's `mio_write`/`mio_write` can't carry per-call SVG styling at all** (registry.cpp's `(path, mesh)` lambdas take no parameters, unlike C++'s direct `write_svg(...)` call with explicit `color_by`/`cmap`/etc.), so every render in these two notebook sets is **plain, uncoloured** — quality/field distributions that the C++ notebook shows as a coloured mesh are shown as a histogram/bar chart instead (`mio_notebook.jl`'s `bar_chart`/`histogram_chart`, hand-rolled SVG; `mio_notebook.R`'s `mio_bar_chart`/`mio_histogram`, plain base-R graphics IRkernel captures as inline PNG with no extra package — meshio++'s own colormap tables aren't reachable from the C API either, so neither reuses them). Both reuse `example/cpp/example.vtu` (copied, not re-derived — both are Git-LFS so this is cheap) for the same Gmsh-`$Entities` C++-reader-gap reason the C++ notebooks do (that gap closed in v9.7.0; the `.vtu` stays because the notebooks are committed with outputs). **Two real findings surfaced while writing these** (both already fixed in the bindings and documented in `doc/julia.md`/`doc/r.md`/both `example/*/README.md`): (1) `smooth()`'s `mu` default was wrongly hardcoded `-0.53` in **both** the Julia and R bindings (the real default, per Fortran's `meshioplusplus.f90:1853` and Python's `mp.smooth`, is `-0.34`) — a `-0.53` pass-band reliably *degrades* a tangled mesh instead of recovering it, caught by cross-checking the notebooks' synthetic jittered-hex smoothing demo against the Python bindings (which recovered cleanly) before either Julia or R did; (2) R's data setters (`mio_add_point_data`/`mio_append_cell_data`/`mio_add_field_data`) always write `Float64` regardless of the R vector's storage mode — R has no integer type reaching the C ABI on the *write* side either — so `mio_split(by = "region")` cannot be driven by a tag built fresh in R (only one already present in a read file, or produced by the C++ core itself like `isosurface()`'s `iso:index`); `example/r/03_mesh_operations.ipynb`'s split demo uses `by = "type"` for exactly this reason, and this is now a documented `doc/r.md` gap distinct from the Julia binding, which has no such restriction. (3) Writing the notebooks' **"Transient sequences"** section (v9.12.0 follow-up: `Sequence`/`mio_sequence()` random access, natural-numeric ordering, fan-in/fan-out, a per-step `run_sequence_file` chain) surfaced a genuine flat-ABI gap: `mio_sequence_to_timeseries` had **no way to select XDMF's `"XML"` data format**, which these HDF5-off notebook environments specifically need (the default `"HDF"` throws without HDF5, and there was no options-taking variant at all — unlike `mio_write`, which at least has `mio_write_ex`). Closed with `mio_sequence_to_timeseries_ex(seq, path, format, mio_write_opts*)` (only `encoding` has anywhere to go there; `Codec`/`FloatFormat` fail by name, since the transient writer bypasses the registry entirely) plus an `ascii`/`ascii=TRUE` keyword on the Julia/R wrappers — the same write_options.hpp rule ("an option the writer cannot honour is an error, never silently ignored") applied to a second writer. Committed **with outputs**; re-execute per each README's kernel-setup + nbconvert recipe.

**Benchmarks** (`benchmark/`): `bench.py` times read/write on formats both
libraries support — meshio++ vs the legacy pure-Python `meshio` (imported from
`/home/vicente/src/meshio_legacy/src` via `sys.path`, no build). `inputs.py`
provides the real `example/example.msh` bracket (the headline input) + a
synthetic numpy tet grid (also used for a size-scaling sweep). `01_benchmark.ipynb`
runs it, writes `results.csv`, and regenerates the plots in
`doc/public/benchmarks/` (`benchmark_times`/`_speedup` = the bracket,
`benchmark_scaling` = speedup vs mesh size) shown on `doc/benchmarks.md`. The needed extras
(`pyvista matplotlib jupyter nbconvert ipykernel`) are installed into `.venv`
with `uv pip install`. `bench_hotpath.py` is a standalone (no-legacy) micro-bench
for the C++ container hot paths — it round-trips a shared-vertex triangle grid
through WKT and times the reader's per-vertex dedup; A/B a `main` build vs a branch
by comparing its `min` (WKT read is parse-bound, so container-level effects sit near
the machine's jitter floor). `bench_backends.sh` + `src/cpp/benchmark/bench_backends.cpp`
(built with `-DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_BUILD_BENCHMARKS=ON`) compare the **mesh backends**:
one binary per backend (the backend is compile-time), CSV collated into
`benchmark/results_backends.csv` — ingest / accessor traversal / `to_modelpart`
(KRATOS materialization) / per-format file round-trips on a synthetic tet cube;
results table in `doc/benchmarks.md`.

## Architecture

**Core data model** (`src/python/meshioplusplus/_mesh.py`, pure Python, unchanged):
- `Mesh`: holds `points`, `cells` (list of `CellBlock`), `point_data`, `cell_data`, `field_data`, `point_sets`, `cell_sets`, optionally `gmsh_periodic`/`info`
- `CellBlock`: a cell type string (e.g. `"triangle"`, `"tetra10"`) + numpy node-index array
- `_common.py`: `num_nodes_per_cell`; `topological_dimension` in `_mesh.py`

**C++ core** (`src/cpp/`, `bindings/`):
- `src/cpp/include/meshioplusplus/` headers (`ndarray.hpp`, `mesh.hpp`, `mesh_api.hpp`, `cell_type.hpp`, `types.hpp`, `registry.hpp`, `region.hpp`, `backends/*`, `detail/*`, `formats/*`), `src/cpp/src/formats/*.cpp`, compiled with `bindings/python/_core.cpp` into `meshioplusplus._core`.
- **Format dispatch registry** (`registry.hpp` + `src/cpp/src/registry.cpp`, compiled into `meshioplusplus_core_obj`): the C++-level `format name -> read/write function` tables, `extension -> default format` map, `resolve_format()`, and `registry_compiled_out()` (names the missing optional dep for HDF5/netCDF formats absent from a build; their extensions are mapped unconditionally so errors say "no HDF5 support" instead of "cannot infer format"). Shared by the **flat bindings** — WASM (`bindings/wasm/js_bindings.cpp`, which no longer carries its own tables) and the C API (`bindings/c/`) — while the pybind11 binding keeps its one-function-per-format surface with dispatch in Python (`_helpers.py`). Parameterized writers get fixed defaults here (documented per entry; matching the Python reference defaults, except xdmf which follows the build: HDF when HDF5 is available, XML otherwise). **A new C++ format must also be registered here** (reader/writer entry + extension default) or the flat bindings won't see it.
- **C API** (`bindings/c/`): `include/meshioplusplus/meshioplusplus.h` (the single installed pure-C99 header; the C++ headers make no ABI promise) + `c_api.cpp`, built into the installable `libmeshioplusplus` shared lib (SOVERSION 0) when `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_BUILD_C_API=ON`. Flat whole-mesh API over the **uniform mesh API only**, so it compiles under all three mesh backends and the gtest C-API suite runs per-backend in CI. Contract: `mio_status` codes + thread-local `mio_last_error()` (no exception crosses the ABI — every extern "C" body runs in `guarded()`/`guarded_ptr()`); setters copy, getters are zero-copy borrows valid until the next mutating call; strings via caller-buffer + required-length return (never `c_str()` of a per-backend-possibly-temporary `Type()`); row-major arrays, 0-based connectivity (int32 widened to int64 on ingest); side-channel structs (point_sets/cell_sets/MED families) are dropped like WASM. **Ragged blocks cross in both directions as of v9.15.0** (see the polyhedra note below): `mio_mesh_add_polygon_block`/`_polyhedron_block` build one from flat CSR, and an opaque `mio_poly_conn` **snapshot** (`_create`/`_get_shape`/`_nodes`/`_face_offsets`/`_cell_offsets`/`_free`) reads one back. That snapshot is the one deliberate exception to the zero-copy-borrow rule and is *stronger* than it — it survives mutating calls — because MESHIO stores ragged blocks as nested vectors with no offsets array to point at, KRATOS rebuilds blocks lazily, and `c_api.cpp` cannot reach `NativeMesh::Blocks()` since it compiles under all three backends. `mio_mesh_cell_block_info_ex` (a reserved-tail struct, the `mio_read_opts` discipline) reports `is_polyhedron`/`num_faces`/`num_nodes`; the five-argument `mio_mesh_cell_block_info` is untouched, and `mio_mesh_cell_block_conn` still returns `MIO_ERR_UNSUPPORTED` for ragged since there is no rectangular buffer to borrow. `mio_read_metadata_cell_block` reports **no** `is_polyhedron` and takes `int*` where the mesh path takes `int32_t*` — both frozen, `MeshMetadata` being layout-pinned; documented rather than fixed. The `mio_cell_type` enum duplicates the cell-type list in the C header and `c_api.cpp` static_asserts every entry against `CellType`, so drift is a compile error.
- **Fortran module** (`bindings/fortran/meshioplusplus.f90`, module `meshioplusplus`, `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_BUILD_FORTRAN=ON` which force-enables the C API): modern OO Fortran 2008 (`type(mio_mesh)` with type-bound procedures, HDF5/PETSc style) over the C API via ISO_C_BINDING; raw `bind(c)` interfaces are private (`c_mio_*`). Conventions: arrays are Fortran-shaped — points `(dim, n)`, conn `(npc, ncells)` — which is the *same memory* as the C row-major shapes (no transpose anywhere); connectivity is 1-based, shifted ±1 inside the copying setters/getters only; `points_ptr` is the zero-copy borrow (no shift needed); every fallible procedure takes optional `stat`/`errmsg`, absent `stat` + failure = print + `error stop` (stdlib pattern); handles freed explicitly (`m%free()`, no finalizer).
- **Julia / R bindings** (`bindings/julia/MeshioPlusPlus/`, `bindings/r/meshioplusplus/`, docs [`doc/julia.md`](doc/julia.md) / [`doc/r.md`](doc/r.md)): the fourth and fifth consumers of the **installed** C API, alongside Fortran. **No new C++ and no core change** — both build entirely OUTSIDE the CMake tree and consume `libmeshioplusplus` + the installed `.pc`, so `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_BUILD_C_API=ON` + `cmake --install` is the prerequisite for both (`inst/` is gitignored). Full **Fortran parity**: lifecycle, read/write (+ `ReadOptions`/metadata), all setters/getters, regions, and all ~24 operations including the opaque-result ones. **Both languages are column-major, so Fortran's two rules carry over verbatim and are the load-bearing design facts**: points `(dim, n)` / conn `(npc, ncells)` are the *same memory* as the C row-major shapes (**no transpose anywhere** — the one exception is `transform`'s 4x4 affine matrix, which *is* transposed, being a mathematical object rather than a mesh array), and connectivity is 1-based with the ±1 shift **inside the copying accessors only**. Index maps / permutations follow Fortran's rule to the letter — `+1` with the `-1` "pruned/absent" sentinel becoming **`0`** (`meshioplusplus.f90:2042`) — and `partition_labels` returns part *ids*, deliberately unshifted; region `Side` entries shift the cell column but **not** the facet column. **Ownership rule shared by both**: the opaque results (`split`/`partition`/`reorder`/`refine`/`decimate`/`convert_cells`) are always drained through `_take_mesh`, never `_result_mesh`, so every mesh handed to the user owns its handle and no piece can dangle when the result is freed. **Julia**: `ccall` via a runtime `dlopen`/`dlsym` cache (`ccall((:sym, lib), …)` needs a compile-time-constant library, so the path — `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_LIB` first, then `Libdl.find_library` — cannot be used that way); GC **finalizer** rather than Fortran's explicit `m%free()` (`close` is the deterministic form); genuine zero-copy borrows via `unsafe_wrap`, wrapped in a `MeshBorrow <: AbstractArray` that holds the owning `Mesh` (GC) **and** a mutation-generation snapshot, so using a borrow after a mutating call raises `BorrowError` instead of reading stale memory — the C header's rule 3 enforced, not merely documented; `read`/`write`/`convert`/`merge`/`split`/`diff` are **not exported** (they shadow `Base`), everything else is. **R**: plain `.Call` over R's C API, **not Rcpp** (zero `Imports`, no C++ toolchain in `R CMD check`), external pointer + registered finalizer, and **copy-only** — R vectors are R-managed, so there is no borrow and deliberately **no `_ptr` accessor at all**; the 0-based reader is `mio_connectivity_raw`, named so nobody reads it as a borrow. R has no native int64, so every int64 array (connectivity, region entries, maps, perms) comes back as `double` (exact to 2^53, no `bit64` dependency) with the stored dtype reported in a `"dtype"` attribute. The same limitation runs the other way too: **R's data *setters* always write `Float64`** (`mio_add_point_data`/`mio_append_cell_data`/`mio_add_field_data` hardcode `MIO_FLOAT64` regardless of the R vector's storage mode), so `mio_split(by = "region")`, which needs a genuinely integer cell-data tag, cannot be driven by a tag array built fresh in R — only one already present in a *read* file (a gmsh physical group, an `isosurface()`-produced `iso:index`, …) works; `example/r/03_mesh_operations.ipynb` hit exactly this and uses `by = "type"` instead. **`smooth`'s `mu` default is `-0.34`, not `-0.53`** — both the Julia and R bindings initially hardcoded an invented `-0.53` (never checked against the real default in Fortran's `meshioplusplus.f90:1853` / Python's `mp.smooth`), which is a *much* wider Taubin pass-band and reliably **degrades** quality instead of recovering it (caught by cross-checking a synthetic jittered-hex smoothing demo against the Python bindings while writing `example/julia/03_mesh_operations.ipynb` and `example/r/03_mesh_operations.ipynb` — Python recovered cleanly, Julia/R got worse with the wrong default, both matched Python once fixed). R's `configure` generates `src/Makevars` from `src/Makevars.in` (pkg-config → `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_HOME`) because a committed `Makevars` with a `$(shell …)` earns an `--as-cran` non-portable-flags NOTE — the `sf`/`xml2` pattern. Every export is `mio_`-prefixed to stay clear of base R's `points`/`stats`/`split`/`merge`. **Licence exception**: `bindings/julia/` is **GPL-3.0**, NOT MIT — copyleft, not permission-required: no permission is needed to use/modify/sell it commercially, but *conveying* (distributing) it or a modified version requires doing so under GPL-3.0 too with source available; purely private use carries no obligation. GPL-3.0 is OSI-approved so General-registry registration is possible (a separate follow-up, not done), and there is deliberately no JLL yet; `bindings/r/` stays MIT. Both READMEs and doc pages repeat the *same* C-ABI gap list (sets beyond regions, `frozen` masks, per-cell-type counts, combined `data_manage`; **ragged connectivity left that list in v9.15.0** — Julia gets nested vectors via `polygon_block`/`polyhedron_block` + `add_polygon_block!`/`add_polyhedron_block!`, R nested lists via `mio_polygon_block()`/`mio_polyhedron_block()` + the two `mio_add_*` setters, and Fortran the 1-based CSR triple, each language's *natural* shape over the one flat ABI, exactly as column-major arrays and 1-based indices already are; `connectivity`/`mio_connectivity()` still raise, a ragged block having no matrix) plus one **format** gap worth not rediscovering: **gmsh does not round-trip named regions** — the writer emits `$PhysicalNames` but attaches no physical tag to an entity in `$Elements`, so the reader has nothing to rebuild the group from (pre-existing, reproducible from Python; abaqus works and is what both test suites use). CI: the `julia` and `r` jobs in `ci.yml`, both mirroring the `cpp-tests` NATIVE leg's install-then-consume shape, each also asserting that a **missing library fails by name**.
- **Mesh backends** (`mesh.hpp` dispatch; mirrors the parallel-backend pattern): `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_MESH_BACKEND` = `MESHIO|NATIVE|KRATOS` selects, at compile time, which structure `meshioplusplus::Mesh` is — exactly one `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_MESH_BACKEND_*` macro is defined. `MESHIO` (default, `backends/meshio_mesh.hpp`) is the meshio-mirroring `Mesh`/`CellBlock` and is **required** when `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_BUILD_PYTHON=ON` (CMake `FATAL_ERROR` otherwise; `np_conversions.hpp` `#error`s too) — PyPI/wheels are unaffected by the other backends. `NATIVE` (`backends/native_mesh.hpp`) is canonical statically-typed storage (Float64 points, Int64 connectivity, `CellType` enum from `cell_type.hpp`, CSR ragged blocks, lazy whole-mesh CSR via `GlobalConnectivity()`); the WASM build uses it. `KRATOS` (`backends/kratos_mesh.hpp`) wraps a Kratos-style `ModelPart` (`backends/model_part.hpp`: Nodes/Elements/Conditions with 1-based Ids, nested SubModelParts sharing root-owned entities, simplified per-entity variables) — ingest stages canonically, `GetModelPart()` materializes lazily (Elements = blocks of max topological dimension, Conditions = lower, names from `backends/kratos_names.hpp` ported from `_mdpa.py`; integer tag cell-data like `gmsh:physical` auto-becomes SubModelParts, toggle `SetBuildSubModelPartsFromTags(false)`), writers serve from staging so round-trips never pay for the ModelPart; after direct ModelPart mutation call `InvalidateBlocks()` — whose rebuild is **lossless as of v9.0.0**: ragged pass-through blocks are carried through at their original positions (cell_data slices included; they never became entities, so a ModelPart edit cannot have touched them) and SubModelParts are read back as named `Cell`/`Point` regions, where before both were silently dropped. SubModelPart **nesting** survives too as of v9.1.0, flattened into region names with `'/'` (the separator `mdpa.cpp` already emitted): `RestoreRegions` walks recursively and `BuildSubModelPartsFromRegions` splits and walks/creates the chain, adding members to the leaf. `'.'` stays reserved (`FullName()` joins with it) and a name containing one is a warn-and-skip rather than a `std::invalid_argument` escaping the lazy accessor. Only a region's `mDim`/`mTag` still cannot survive (recovered from a staged region of the same name, else -1) -- a SubModelPart stores a name and member ids and nothing else, so `-1` is the truthful answer rather than a gap. **Entity names and properties ids survive as of v9.1.0**: `SetBlockEntityName`/`BlockEntityName` are KRATOS-only extras (NATIVE's `PointsData()`/`ConnSpan()` shape) carrying the Kratos spelling per block through `Materialize()` and back through `CollectEntityBlocks` (whose run key compares *effective* names, so an explicit `Element3D4N` still merges with unnamed entities of the same type), and `Materialize()` now reads each entity's properties id from `gmsh:physical` instead of leaving every entity on 0. `GetModelPart()` also has a **`const` overload** (v9.0.0) so a wrapper taking `const Mesh&` can reach it — materialization stays lazy, so `mpRoot`/`mMaterialized` are `mutable`, the same cache-on-demand pattern the staging accessors already use. **Entity names, Properties and the resolver (v9.1.0):** `GeometricalEntity` carries an optional Kratos name (`Name()`/`HasName()`, empty = derive from the cell type) as an interned `const std::string*` from a root-owned `detail::NamePool` — **not** a `std::string`: one distinct name per *block* but one entity per *cell*, so owning it would add ~320 MB to a 10 M-element ModelPart; `unordered_set` is node-based so the pointers survive rehash and a ModelPart move. New **five-argument** `CreateNew{Element,Condition}` `CellType` overloads carry it on the bulk path (distinct arity — no existing call changes). `ModelPart` also gained a real **Properties store** (`CreateNewProperties`/`GetProperties`/`Properties()`) holding `PropertyValue` key/value pairs from the backend-neutral `properties.hpp` shared with MDPA. meshio++ **cannot** fill a real `Kratos::Properties` itself — that needs `KratosComponents<Variable<T>>::Get`, Kratos's registry, deliberately not linked — so a fourth `to_model_part` overload hands each value to a caller-supplied applier callback. `bridge_traits` gained a **detection-guarded `NameOf`** (`if constexpr (requires { e.Name(); })`) so every existing duck-typed source still compiles and simply reports no name. `kratos_bridge.hpp` (header-only, backend-independent) `to_model_part`/`from_model_part` populate/read ANY Kratos-like class — incl. real `Kratos::ModelPart` via a properties-getter — with one O(n) bulk-create pass; CI compile-checks it against real CoSimIO headers (fetched at CI time only — CoSimIO's BSD-4-clause-with-advertising license is never vendored into this MIT repo; `model_part.hpp` is a clean-room implementation). `_core.__mesh_backend__` / JS `meshBackend()` report the active backend. **Adding a backend** = one CMake branch defining `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_MESH_BACKEND_<NAME>` + one `#elif` in `mesh.hpp` + a `backends/<name>_mesh.hpp` implementing the uniform API (`tests/cpp/test_mesh_api.cpp` must pass). CI runs the gtest suite once per backend (`ci.yml` `cpp-tests` matrix).
- **Uniform mesh API rule** (`mesh_api.hpp`): format code (`src/cpp/src/formats/*`, shared `detail/*` helpers, `bindings/wasm/`) MUST go through the uniform methods — ingest `AssignPoints`/`AddCellBlock`/`AddPolygonBlock`/`AddPolyhedronBlock`/`AddPointData`/`AddCellData`/`AppendCellData`/`AddFieldData`, accessors `Points()`/`PointDim()`/`Cells(i)`/`CellRange()` (`Mesh::CellView` with `Type()`/`NumCells()`/`NodesPerCell()`/`Conn()`/`IsRagged()`/ragged `Row`/`Face`) and `PointDataNames()`/`PointData(name)`/`CellData(name, block)`/`CellDataNumBlocks`/`FieldData(name)` (+ `Has*`/`Num*`) — and never backend struct members; that's what lets one format compile under all three backends. **Property sets** (`properties.hpp`, v9.2.0) are on the same footing -- `AddPropertySet`/`NumPropertySets`/`GetPropertySet`/`HasPropertySet`/`FindPropertySet`, ascending by `mId`; the accessor is `GetPropertySet` and not `PropertySet(i)` precisely because of the `Region(i)` name-hiding trap. They are keyed by **id, not entity index**, so no `region_remap` counterpart exists or is needed, and the operation rule is shape-preserving ops carry them (`clean`/`smooth`/`transform`/`attach_quality`/data ops) while restructuring and multi-input ones do not (`merge` would collide on id, plus crop/split/partition/diff). Hoist `Points()`/`Conn()`/data lookups out of per-element hot loops. The one sanctioned exception is `bindings/python/np_conversions.hpp` (pinned to MESHIO). NATIVE/KRATOS canonicalize dtypes at ingest *within kind* (floats→Float64, ints→Int64, never int→float — "first integer cell_data is the tag" conventions survive); owning canonical arrays are moved, not copied.
- **Zero-copy at the I/O boundary**: readers return capsule-backed writeable numpy; writers view numpy memory (`bindings/python/np_conversions.hpp`). The conversion layer carries points/cells/point_data/cell_data/field_data **and named regions** (`region.hpp` — see the regions entry below and [`doc/regions.md`](doc/regions.md)), but **not** `mesh.info` or `gmsh_periodic`. Regions are the model behind the Python `Mesh`'s `.regions` and behind its `point_sets`/`cell_sets`, which are now compat **views** over them; regions are the one thing here that is *copied* rather than viewed on the write path, because `Region::Canonicalize` sorts entries in place and the numpy buffer is not ours to reorder. Formats that still need other out-of-band data carry it via a **side-channel struct** the binding `setattr`s onto the Python `Mesh` (e.g. `MedInfo` for MED's `CHA`/step metadata, `OpenFoamInfo` for `cell_tags`); `AnsysInfo`/`UnvInfo` remain the Phase-2 candidates to absorb into regions. **Field-only formats** (`mff`/`dex`/`ip`, the Modulef/FLUX/ANSYS-Fluent field files) carry no geometry: they read/write a geometry-less `Mesh` (no cells, field values in `point_data`; `dex`/`ip` also fill `points` from the file's coordinates, `mff` has none so its `points` has zero columns) — there is no companion-mesh auto-pairing.
- **Ragged cell blocks**: every backend supports optional ragged blocks — 1-level (jagged polygons, `AddPolygonBlock`) and 2-level (list of faces per cell, `AddPolyhedronBlock`) — for types that can't fit a rectangular `NDArray`, read back via `CellView`'s `RowSize`/`Row` and `NumFaces`/`Face`. MESHIO stores them as nested vectors (`CellBlock::mPolygonRows`/`mPolyhedronRows`), NATIVE as CSR offset arrays, KRATOS as staging pass-through (no ModelPart entities). Zero-copy applies only to rectangular blocks; ragged blocks are **copied** across the Python boundary. `py_to_mesh`'s `allow_ragged` flag is **off by default** (so every rectangular-only writer keeps safely rejecting ragged meshes → Python fallback) and the ragged-aware bindings opt in (~15 sites now, not just MED write as its doc comment still claims). **The full contract — the two shapes, the flat CSR vocabulary shared by every binding, the winding rule (meshio++ REPAIRS rather than requires outward winding, because real meshes and this repo's own `helpers.py` fixture are inconsistently wound), and the corner-average-fan answer for non-planar faces — is [`doc/polyhedra.md`](doc/polyhedra.md).** The **geometric kernel** is `detail/polyhedron.hpp` (v9.16.0): `CellRings` is the one shape a polyhedron's global-id faces and `cell_faces.hpp`'s local-index rows both reduce to — and for a *tabulated* cell `cell_rings` interns nodes in **connectivity order**, so the ring indices ARE `cell_faces`' own `mNodes`, which `gradient` and `cell_volume_from_corners` both depend on. `orient_rings` repairs winding (BFS over the shared-edge dual, then a global flip on a negative volume) and reports `Unorientable` only for a genuinely non-closed/non-orientable face set. `poly_measure` gives volume/centroid/area by the **corner-average fan**, and `FacetKey` is the variable-arity facet key (4-entry inline fast path) that replaced `surface.cpp`'s fixed `array<int64_t,4>` — **one** key type across shapes is what lets a hexahedron and a polyhedron meeting on a face cancel each other out. **The apex being the face's corner average rather than the ring's first node is load-bearing**: the apex is then a function of the FACE, so two cells sharing a warped quad triangulate it identically whatever their local node orders (`PolyhedronKernel.CellsSharingAWarpedFaceAgreeOnIt`, which fails against the old fan). Note a *structured* generator numbers shared faces consistently and so escapes the defect by luck — an earlier version of that test used a generated hex block and passed against BOTH decompositions, i.e. was inert. v9.16.0 also deduplicated the three copy-pasted origin-referenced volume/area helpers (`stats.cpp`, `detail/data_ops.cpp`, `clean.cpp`) onto the kernel — a **Breaking** change for non-planar faces only — and `_stats.py`'s numpy twin moved in lockstep, since Windows CI runs the Python fallback and would otherwise report different volumes; `test_cpp_matches_python` gained a warped fixture because a cube cannot tell the two fans apart (a parallelogram's corner average IS its area centroid). Polyhedral ops as of v9.16.0: `stats`, `gradient` (Green-Gauss over the rings — the op the roadmap correctly calls naturally polyhedral), `quality` (a **reduced** set — volume/inverted/degenerate, everything else NaN, and `inverted` means *unorientable* since winding is not a contract), `extract_surface`/`extract_skin` (n-gon faces land in a ragged `polygon` block), `smooth`'s inversion guard, and `cell_measure` (so `data_average`'s measure weighting). **Still skipping them**: `smooth`'s *boundary* marking, `partition`'s KaHIP dual graph (SFC is unaffected), and `clean`'s degenerate/duplicate detection. One trap worth repeating here: a block whose cells happen to share a node count stores rectangularly and is therefore **not** `IsRagged()` even though its type is `polygon`, so code that must reject polyhedra tests the **type name**, never `IsRagged()` (`cgns.cpp:476` learned this). Note also the CSR naming skew: the bindings' `face_offsets`/`cell_offsets` are NATIVE's `mRowOffsets`/`mFaceOffsets` respectively.
- **Named regions** (`src/cpp/include/meshioplusplus/region.hpp` + `src/cpp/src/region.cpp`, Python `_regions.py`, docs [`doc/regions.md`](doc/regions.md)): `Region{mName, mKind, mDim, mTag, mEntries}` — the unified model of a *named group of entities*, which every set-capable format spells differently (gmsh physical groups, Exodus blocks/node sets/side sets, Abaqus `*NSET`/`*ELSET`/`*SURFACE`, MED families, UNV groups, Ansys components, OpenFOAM patches, Kratos SubModelParts). Three kinds: `Point` (point indices), `Cell` (**global block-major** cell indices — the same numbering `partition_labels` uses, owned by `detail/cell_index.hpp` so nothing re-derives it) and `Side` (`(global cell, local facet)` pairs, facets numbered per `detail/cell_faces.hpp` / `cell_edges.hpp`) — **`Side` has no `point_sets`/`cell_sets` equivalent at all** and is only reachable through `.regions`. `mDim`/`mTag` (-1 = unspecified/none) exist because a gmsh physical group is per-dimension and carries an integer tag two groups of different dimensions may share. Entries are **canonicalized** (sorted + de-duplicated, lexicographically on the pair for `Side`) by `AddRegion` on every backend, which is what makes region equality exact and output byte-identical across MESHIO/NATIVE/KRATOS and thread counts — and what makes a remapped set come back **sorted** rather than in the permutation's order (a visible v8.1.0 change). Storage is `NDArray` Int64 rather than `std::vector` so the read path adopts the buffer zero-copy; the write path copies once. Uniform-API surface: `AddRegion`/`NumRegions`/`Region(i)`/`RegionNames()` (sorted, de-duplicated)/`HasRegion(name[, kind])`/`FindRegion(name, kind)` — **a member function named `Region` hides the type `meshioplusplus::Region` for the rest of each backend's class body, so every declaration after it must qualify it**. The three backends share `detail::RegionList` (in `region.hpp`) so they cannot drift on ordering/canonicalization; KRATOS additionally materializes Point/Cell regions as **SubModelParts**, running **before** the `SetBuildSubModelPartsFromTags` inference so an explicit region wins the name (the existing `HasSubModelPart` guard already implements that), and skipping `Side` with a warn. **Python compat is deprecation-free**: `mesh.regions` is the storage and `point_sets`/`cell_sets` are `dict`-**subclass** write-through views over it (a `dict` subclass, not a bare `MutableMapping`, because `numpy.testing.assert_equal` and plenty of user code branch on `isinstance(x, dict)`). Two accommodations make that lossless: a `cell_sets` entry that is **not** cell-index data takes a private `_extra_cell_sets` passthrough and comes back verbatim (gmsh's `gmsh:bounding_entities` holds signed entity tags — the predicate is *non-negative integers*, deliberately **not** "in range", since 475/476 populated blocks of `example.msh` carry a negative and an in-range test would instead reject a genuine but sloppy cell set that meshio has always accepted), and a `None` block becomes an empty array (global indices cannot express "absent" separately from "empty"; every consumer already treated them alike, and the Abaqus writer's `len(v[ic])` would have raised on a real `None`). `Mesh.__init__` must add sets through the **views**, not the property setters — the setters replace all regions of their kind and would wipe the `regions=` argument every C++-backed reader passes. Flat surfaces: C API opaque `mio_regions` (`_create`/`_count`/`_name`/`_info`/`_entries`/`_free` + `mio_mesh_add_region`, with `mio_region_kind` static_asserted against `RegionKind`), Fortran `m%regions(keys=, entries=)`/`m%add_region(...)` (point/cell indices 1-based, the side **facet** column deliberately not shifted — `partition_labels`' rule), and WASM where regions ride **on the mesh object** so `readMesh`/`writeMesh`/`convert` carry them with no new callable — hence three places (bind in `js_bindings.cpp`, declare in `index.d.ts`, exercise in `tests/wasm/smoke.mjs`) rather than the usual four, and the exhaustive forward guard stays at its then-current count. **Phase 1 formats: gmsh, abaqus and MED** (full round-trip) **plus exodus read-only** (v8.6.0 — element blocks → `Cell` named from `eb_names`/tagged from `eb_prop1`, node sets → `Point`, side sets → `Side`; its *writer* still emits neither `eb_names` nor side sets, so it is a region **source**, recorded in `test_region_roundtrip.py`'s `READ_ONLY_REGIONS` rather than the round-trip `MATRIX`, which by construction cannot hold a write-incapable format). MED joined Phase 1 in v9.6.0 — see the "Note on `med`" entry below. UNV/Ansys/OpenFOAM/XDMF/VTU — and exodus's writer — remain deferred in the `PHASE_2` table. **Exodus side numbering is not `cell_faces.hpp` numbering**, so its side sets go through `exo_face_index` (the `abq_face_index` twin, with a Python twin in `_exodus.py`); a gtest pins every entry against `cell_faces` *by node set* rather than trusting the transcription, because a wrong entry still yields a valid-looking facet index pointing confidently at the wrong face.
- **Region remapping** (`src/cpp/include/meshioplusplus/detail/region_remap.hpp` + `src/cpp/src/detail/region_remap.cpp`): the single owner of "carry a mesh's regions through an operation", replacing nine near-identical per-operation Python `_remap_sets` implementations. It must be **told** which shape the operation's cell map is in, because they genuinely differ and guessing would silently corrupt regions: `Direct` (`map[c]` is *the* output cell, -1 dropped — crop/split/clean/partition/reorder), `FirstChild` (`map[c]` is the first of a contiguous child run ending at the next non-negative entry — convert_cells' simplexify/refine/decimate; correct for 1:1 maps too, but `Direct` must **not** be replaced by it, since a permutation's map is not monotone) and `Global` (one flat array indexed by input *global* cell — merge, whose maps are per input *mesh*). A `Side` entry survives only if its cell does **and** the facet still exists (same output cell type, facet index in range); under `FirstChild` side regions are dropped by name. A region that loses every entry is still carried as an **empty group** — the name is information, and that is what the shims did. `build_cell_subset` calls it internally, so crop/split/partition agree for free (it is the only place that knows the `drop_empty_blocks` block shift). `merge` uses the per-region `remap_region` instead, because it must rename between remapping and storing (two inputs sharing a region name would otherwise collide on the `(kind, name, dim, tag)` key). Ops that cannot carry regions (`slice`, `isosurface`, `extract_surface`/`extract_skin`) call `warn_regions_dropped` — never silent.
- **Operations layer** (`src/cpp/src/operations/*.cpp` + `src/cpp/include/meshioplusplus/operations/*.hpp`): computations *on* a mesh (not file formats — deliberately **not** registered in `registry.cpp`).
