Each operation is uniform-mesh-API-only (compiles under all three backends — the KRATOS `Mesh` is **not** copy-constructible, so never `Mesh out = in;`; clone via the uniform API as `quality_clone_mesh` does), parallelizes its hot loop with `parallel_for`, and is exposed across **every** binding surface (pybind `_core`, C API `mio_*`, Fortran type-bound procedures, WASM embind) plus a CLI verb. `.cpp` anon-namespace helpers are uniquely prefixed (`surface_`/`quality_`/`sniff_`/`reorder_`/`diff_`/`merge_`/`transform_`/`clean_`/`crop_`/`split_`/`stats_`/`ccells_`/`sub_`/`agg_`/`refine_`/`ug_`/`partition_`/`smooth_`/`interp_`/`slice_`/`iso_`/`decim_`/`grad_`, and for the data bundle `dmanage_`/`davg_`/`calc_`/`dcond_`/`dinfo_` — **distinct per file**, since a shared `data_` prefix across five TUs would collide) because the amalgamation concatenates TUs; `detail/` header inline helpers (`geometry.hpp`, `cell_edges.hpp`, `cell_subdivision.hpp`, `subset.hpp`, `space_filling.hpp`, `node_adjacency.hpp`, `data_ops.hpp`, `spatial_hash.hpp`, `marching.hpp`, `cell_index.hpp`, `region_remap.hpp`, `cell_adjacency.hpp`) are exempt. New `src/cpp/src/operations/*.cpp` are auto-globbed by CMake and `rglob`ed by the amalgamator — regenerate + commit the single header after any change. The **crop** and **split** operations share `detail/subset.hpp`'s `build_cell_subset` (prune-points-to-kept-cells + remap connectivity/data, `drop_empty_blocks` flag for split's homogeneous pieces); the five **data operations** share `detail/data_ops.hpp`'s `clone_mesh(mesh, filter)` (backend-agnostic geometry copy + per-array keep/rename decision — the workaround for the uniform API having no remove/rename for data arrays), `FiniteStats` (chunked parallel reduction, serial combine, non-finite values excluded and counted) and `cell_measure`. The **convert_cells** (elevate mode) and **refine** operations share `detail/cell_subdivision.hpp`'s `cell_refine_edges`/`cell_refine_quad_faces` — the single owner of "the edges / quad faces of type T, in the order T's higher-order node slots expect" (it delegates the 2D rows to `cell_edges.hpp`, which stays surface-only); two transcriptions of the tetra10/hexahedron20/wedge15 orderings would drift silently, since a mismatch still yields a valid, oriented, volume-conserving mesh — just one with permuted quadratic mid-nodes. **The hexahedron27 face-centre convention (v9.9.0)**: `cell_faces.cpp`'s `hexahedron27` table used to assign mid-face nodes 20/22/23 as a permuted 3-cycle of the real `vtkTriQuadraticHexahedron::Faces` order (`20=(0,4,7,3)`, `21=(1,2,6,5)`, `22=(0,1,5,4)`, `23=(3,7,6,2)`, `24`=bottom, `25`=top, `26`=body), which put `extract_surface`/`extract_skin`'s quad9 mid-face node at the wrong position — never a wrong *topology*, since facet keying uses corners only. Fixing it required a **lockstep** change across five tables, because `refine` derives node `20+k` from the k-th row of `cell_refine_quad_faces` and nothing else: `cell_faces.cpp`, `_skin.py`'s `_CELL_FACES` (the single owner of the Python face table), `cell_subdivision.cpp`'s quad-face rows, and `refine_templates.cpp` + `_refine_templates.py`'s absolute 20–23 references (permuted by the same 3-cycle). Two facts make this safe rather than risky, and both are worth not rediscovering: `refine.cpp` **never reads `cell_faces()`** (its only callers are surface/smooth/quality/stats/clean/partition/data_ops/region_remap), and every *partial* version of the change trips an existing test — correcting only `cell_faces` fails `CellSubdivision.QuadFacesAgreeWithCellFaces`, and correcting `cell_subdivision` without the templates fails `RefineTemplates.ChildrenArePositivelyOrientedAndConserveMeasure` **and** `TemplatesOnlyReferenceNodesTheirMaskCreates` (both verified to fire before the fix landed, per this repo's "an oracle nobody tests for firing ships inert" rule). The format layer (`cgns.cpp`'s HEXA_27 permutation, `gmsh.cpp`'s hexahedron27 perm, `test_cgns.cpp`'s reference fixture) was **already** on the corrected convention, so this made the codebase self-consistent rather than changing a convention. Also note `test_skin.cpp`'s `SkinFaceTables.OutwardWindingAndNodeCounts` already asserted geometrically that `mNodes[8]` sits at the face's corner average — it passed before only because its own fixture encoded the defect, so correcting the fixture is what turned it into a live oracle. One user-visible consequence: `refine` numbers new nodes in slot order, so the six face-centre points of every refined hexahedron get different **ids/coordinate order** (identical geometry). The **reorder** and **partition** operations share `detail/space_filling.hpp`'s `sfc_part1by2`/`sfc_morton_key`/`sfc_hilbert_key` (the pure Morton/Skilling-Hilbert key transforms, hoisted verbatim from reorder in v7.6.0 — reorder's output stayed byte-identical); the Mesh-coupled bbox/quantization stays per-operation (reorder quantizes node coordinates, partition cell centroids). The **reorder** and **smooth** operations also share `detail/node_adjacency.hpp`'s `build_node_adjacency` — one CSR node-to-node graph with two neighbour definitions, because the consumers genuinely want different graphs: `NodeAdjacencyKind::Clique` (every pair of nodes co-occurring in a cell — the FEM stiffness sparsity pattern, which is exactly what a bandwidth-reducing renumbering must minimise) for **reorder**, `NodeAdjacencyKind::Edge` (only nodes joined by an actual cell edge, topology from `cell_subdivision.hpp`'s `cell_refine_edges` so the three cannot drift) for **smooth**; `node_edge_topology_known` reports the blocks that contribute no edges so a caller can pin their nodes. The **merge** and **interpolate** operations share `detail/spatial_hash.hpp`'s `GridKey`/`GridKeyHash`/`grid_quantize`/`SpatialGrid` (the bucket-grid spatial hash, hoisted verbatim from merge's weld in v7.13.0 — merge's output stayed byte-identical; the hash only buckets and the map is never iterated, so it cannot affect output): merge uses the fixed 27-cell `ForEachIn27` scan, interpolate additionally the expanding `ForEachInShell` search (clamped to the occupied-key bbox) and `InsertBox` for simplex bounding boxes. **slice** does **not** use `spatial_hash` — its cut points are shared *by topology* (a crossing on a shared simplexified edge, keyed by the sorted endpoint-id pair), so it dedups by edge key with no coordinate tolerance (surface.cpp/refine.cpp's phase-split idiom). The **slice** and **isosurface** operations share `detail/marching.hpp`'s `marching_prepare`/`marching_cut` (the whole marching-tetrahedra cutter — simplexify, sign-mask case table, watertight edge dedup, winding, degeneracy rule, `point_data` interpolation and `cell_data` replication — hoisted verbatim from slice in v7.15.0, the way `spatial_hash.hpp` was hoisted from merge; slice's output stayed byte-identical and its test suites were not touched). It is **two-phase** because the expensive part (simplexify) must be shared across several isovalues *and* because the caller resolves its scalar against the **simplexified** mesh (`MarchingInput::Simp()`) — which is what lets isosurface contour a `point_data` array that rode through the linearization. The one genuinely parameterized rule is `MarchingOrientation`: `FixedDirection` reproduces slice's `dot(newell, normal) < 0` flip against the user's plane normal verbatim (keeping byte-identity structural rather than argued), while `FieldGradient` orients toward increasing field via a per-simplex reference direction stored **as a field on the face struct, not a parallel array** (the `ProjectedFace::mSourceCell` precedent). The **partition** and **gradient** operations share `detail/cell_adjacency.hpp`'s `CellIncidence` + `build_cell_node_incidence`/`invert_cell_node_incidence`/`cell_node_neighbors` — the single owner of **cell-to-cell adjacency by shared node** (a different object from `node_adjacency.hpp`'s node-to-node graph): partition grows its ghost layers over it breadth-first, gradient fits its least-squares stencil over it, and the `cell_node_neighbors` ascending/de-duplicated order is a determinism contract, not an implementation detail. Hoisted out of `partition.cpp` in v9.10.0, and **not a byte-identical hoist**: the private version read dense connectivity as `Conn().As<std::int64_t>()`, which performs no dtype check, so on a MESHIO-backed mesh carrying Int32 connectivity from numpy every node id was two fused Int32 entries — most failed the range filter and vanished, leaving `partition(..., ghost_layers>0)` halos **silently too small** (plus a read past the last row). The shared version goes through the dtype-agnostic `detail::cell_node_ids`; output is unchanged for `ghost_layers == 0` and for Int64 connectivity, and the regression test uses Int32 precisely because the existing suite was green only by having Int64 fixtures. The twenty-seven **mesh** operations (on geometry) and five **data** operations (on data arrays) are listed below.
  - **Surface/skin extraction** (`operations/surface.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_surface.py`/`_skin.py`): `extract_surface(mesh, record_parent_ids=false)` picks the dimension automatically (3D volume → boundary faces, 2D surface → boundary edges via `detail/cell_edges.hpp`); `extract_skin(mesh, linearize=false)` (declared in `skin.hpp`) is the volume-only special case — **both share the one implementation in `surface.cpp`** (`extract_skin` calls it in force-face mode, byte-identical to the old `skin.cpp` it absorbed). Kratos `SkinDetectionProcess` face-hashing (sorted corner-node key occurring exactly once = boundary); face topology in `detail/cell_faces.hpp` (Python twin in `_skin.py`, must stay in sync; outward winding enforced by a gtest Newell-normal invariant in `test_skin.cpp`), edge topology in `detail/cell_edges.hpp` (twin in `_surface.py`). Supported volume types: tetra(10), hexahedron(20/27), wedge(15/18), pyramid(13/14) — `wedge18` gained its `cell_faces` row in v7.5.0 (refine needed its quad-face centres), which made it newly skinnable. Output blocks in fixed canonical order (triangle, triangle6, quad, quad8, quad9 / line, line3), points compacted ascending original-id (numpy fallback relies on `np.unique` sortedness for identical output — `tests/python/test_skin.py`/`test_extract_surface.py`), `point_data` subset, other data dropped. Two-phase parallel: keys built in `parallel_for` into disjoint slots, then a **serial** dedup/emit so output is byte-identical across backends and thread counts. `record_parent_ids` attaches Int64 `surface:parent_cell` cell_data (per-output-facet owning input-cell index). **Consumers:** `write_stl`/`write_ply` take a trailing `skin` flag (default **true** — writes the extracted linearized skin, STL triangulating quads; `skin=false` = legacy drop-with-warning; pinned true in `registry.cpp`), and the SVG/TikZ writers project the skin (below).
  - **Mesh quality** (`operations/quality.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_quality.py`): `compute_quality(mesh)` → `QualityReport` (per-metric summaries + histograms, per-block `cell_data` arrays, inverted/degenerate counts); `attach_quality(mesh)` returns a clone with the metrics as `cell_data` (names `quality:<metric>`). Metrics (triangle/quad/tetra/hex/wedge/pyramid, quadratic variants on corners; NaN where N/A): volume/area, scaled_jacobian, aspect_ratio, skewness, min/max_angle, warpage, min/max_dihedral, inverted, degenerate. Verdict/VTK formulas; ideal element → ideal value (SJ=1, aspect=1, skew=0, tri angles 60°, tetra dihedral ≈70.53°) — pinned by `test_quality.cpp`/`tests/python/test_quality.py`. Reusable vec3 math in `detail/geometry.hpp`.
  - **Format sniffing** (`operations/sniff.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_sniff.py`): `sniff_format(path)` returns a format name only on a confident leading-bytes signature match (vtk/vtu/vtp/gmsh/ply/off/xdmf/stl-ascii/abaqus), else `""` (ambiguous magics like generic HDF5 or headerless binary STL are never claimed). Wired as a **read-only** fallback in the read paths (`_helpers._read_file`, C `mio_read`, WASM `read_mesh`) when the extension gives nothing — never on a write path.
  - **Reordering / renumbering** (`operations/reorder.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_reorder.py`): `reorder(mesh, ReorderMethod::RCM|Morton|Hilbert)` → `ReorderResult{mMesh, mNodePermutation, mCellPermutations}` — a **pure permutation** (no nodes/cells added/removed, geometry + all data preserved) returning the applied node/cell permutations (old→new, Int64). RCM builds the node-adjacency CSR (element cliques — nodes sharing a cell are neighbours), picks a pseudo-peripheral start per connected component (rooted-level-structure heuristic), Cuthill–McKee BFS by increasing degree, then reverses; Morton/Hilbert quantize coords to 21 bits/axis over the bbox and stable-sort along the curve. Element perm orders each block's cells by min new-node index. Applies to points/point_data (scatter by node perm, dtype-preserving byte-row gather via `parallel_for_bw`) + connectivity (Int64, remap+reorder) + cell_data (reorder by per-block cell perm); field_data unchanged; ragged/polyhedron blocks supported. `compute_bandwidth(mesh)` = max over cells of (max−min node index). The BFS/argsort stay serial for cross-backend determinism (surface.cpp's phase-split philosophy). Named regions (and so `point_sets`/`cell_sets`) are remapped **in the C++ core** via `detail/region_remap.hpp`; the Python shim only remaps when the numpy fallback ran. `mesh.info`/`gmsh_periodic` are not carried through. The C API uses an opaque `mio_reorder_result` handle (mesh borrow + zero-copy perm getters + `mio_reorder_result_take_mesh` ownership transfer for Fortran); Fortran/WASM expose node perm (Fortran, 1-based) / full node+cell perms (WASM object). CLI verb `reorder … --method rcm|morton|hilbert [--report]`.
  - **Comparison / diff** (`operations/diff.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_diff.py`): `diff(mesh_a, mesh_b, DiffOptions{atol,rtol,unordered,max_reported})` → `DiffReport` (verdict `Identical`/`EqualWithinTolerance`/`Different`; per-section `ArrayDiff`/`BlockDiff`/`DataDiff` with max abs/rel error, worst flat index, mismatch counts, keys-only-in-one, first-N mismatching cells); `meshes_equal(a,b,atol,rtol)` = `verdict != Different` (ordered). Tolerance rule `abs_err <= atol + rtol*|expected|` (expected from `a`); "identical" is exact equality. **Ordered** path (default) compares points/cells/data index-by-index — the elementwise reduce is chunked and `parallel_for`-parallel, combined serially (deterministic). **Unordered** (`unordered=true`) matches points by proximity via a bucket-grid coordinate hash (no O(N²)); conservative — sets `mCorrespondenceFailed` + verdict `Different` rather than guessing when no one-to-one match exists. Only points/cells/`point_data`/`cell_data`/`field_data` are reachable in C++; **named `point_sets`/`cell_sets` are compared in the Python shim only** (added/removed/changed → a `"sets"` report section, folded into the verdict) — C/Fortran/WASM never see sets. Python `diff` returns a nested dict (with the numpy fallback returning an identically-keyed dict), `meshes_equal` is a boolean; `_format_report` renders the CLI output. C API: `mio_diff`/`mio_meshes_equal` + opaque `mio_diff_result` (verdict/point-summary/per-block getters, `mio_diff_result_free`). Fortran `m%equals`/`m%diff` (verdict 0/1/2). WASM `diff`/`meshesEqual`. CLI verb `diff A B [--atol --rtol --unordered --exact --quiet]` — **exit code is nonzero when meshes differ** (0 for identical or equal-within-tolerance; `--exact` makes only identical pass) for CI/Makefile use.
  - **Merge / weld** (`operations/merge.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_merge.py`): `merge(vector<const Mesh*>, MergeOptions{weld,atol,source_tag,data_policy,drop_duplicate_cells})` → `MergeResult{mMesh, mPointMaps, mCellMaps}` — combine 2+ meshes into one. **Concatenate** (default, O(N)): prefix-sum point offsets, offset connectivity, merge cell blocks by `Type()` (first-seen order), concat data. **Weld** (`weld=true`): coincident points within `atol` fused via a spatial-hash bucket grid — quantize to `atol`-cells (`parallel_for`), **serial** first-occurrence dedup checking the 27-neighbourhood (keep-first, deterministic), then `parallel_for_bw` gather + connectivity remap (surface.cpp's phase-split philosophy, no O(N²)). `source_tag` (default on) adds Int64 `source_mesh_id` cell_data (originating input per cell). `data_policy` `Intersection` (default; keep keys in all inputs) vs `Fill` (union, NaN→Float64 for missing rows); differing dtypes promote to Float64; 1-D scalar data stays 1-D. `drop_duplicate_cells` (weld-only) drops cells with identical sorted connectivity (keep-first). `field_data` key collisions namespace by source id (`"i:name"`). `mPointMaps`/`mCellMaps` (per-input old→new index, -1 for dropped cells) exist so the **Python shim** can remap+namespace the shim-only `point_sets`/`cell_sets` (never reach C++, like diff); `mesh.info`/`gmsh_periodic` not carried. Ragged/polyhedron blocks supported in C++ (the numpy fallback `_merge_py` is rectangular-only, raising otherwise). C API `mio_merge(meshes, count, weld, atol, source_tag, data_policy/*0=intersection,1=fill*/, drop_duplicate_cells)` → new `mio_mesh*`. Fortran module-level `mio_merge(meshes(:), ...)` → `type(mio_mesh)`. WASM `merge([...], weld, atol, sourceTag, dataPolicy, dropDuplicateCells)`. CLI verb `merge FILE… OUTFILE [--weld --atol --data-policy intersection|fill --drop-duplicate-cells --no-source-tag --quiet]` (last positional = output; prints an in/out/welded summary).
  - **Transform** (`operations/transform.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_transform.py`): `transform(mesh, AffineTransform{4x4 row-major}, rotate_vector_data=false)` → new `Mesh` — affine transform of point coords (points change; connectivity/`cell_data`/`field_data` carried; `point_data` carried unless `rotate_vector_data` rotates trailing-dim-3 vectors `R·v` / trailing-dim-9 tensors `R·A·Rᵀ`). Builders `transform_translation`/`_scale`/`_units`/`_rotation`(Rodrigues)/`_from_matrix`/`_compose`. Points transformed in `parallel_for_bw` (dtype preserved). Negative-determinant transforms `log::warn` orientation-may-flip. `point_sets`/`cell_sets` pass through unchanged (Python shim). Python API builds the matrix from `translate`/`scale`/`rotate=(axis,deg)`/`matrix`/`scale_units` kwargs (compose order units→scale→rotate→translate). C API `mio_transform(mesh, double[16], rotate_vector_data)` → `mio_mesh*`. Fortran `m%transform(matrix(16), rotate_vector_data=...)`. WASM `transform(mesh, matrix, rotateVectorData)`. CLI `transform IN OUT --translate|--scale|--rotate|--matrix|--scale-units [--rotate-data]` (exactly one source; negatives need `--opt=` form).
  - **Clean** (`operations/clean.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_clean.py`): `clean(mesh, CleanOptions{weld,atol,remove_orphans,drop_degenerate,drop_duplicate_cells})` → `CleanResult{mMesh, mPointMap, mCellMaps, mPointsWelded, mPointsRemovedOrphan, mCellsDroppedDegenerate, mCellsDroppedDuplicate}` — one-pass weld/prune/de-dup. Order: weld (spatial-hash bucket grid, keep-first, `clean_build_weld_map`, no O(N²)) → drop degenerate (repeated corner node, or |measure|<eps via `detail/geometry`+`cell_faces`) → drop duplicate (sorted-conn key, keep-first per block) → remove orphans (compact). Defaults: no weld, the other three on (matches CLI no-flag default). `point_data` keep-first on weld; regions (and so sets) remapped in the C++ core via `detail/region_remap.hpp` (dropped entries removed). Ragged/polyhedron supported in C++; numpy fallback rectangular-only (reuses `_merge._weld_map`). C API `mio_clean(mesh, weld, atol, remove_orphans, drop_degenerate, drop_duplicate_cells, &n_welded, &n_orphan, &n_degen, &n_dup)` → `mio_mesh*`. Fortran `m%clean(...)` with optional count out-args. WASM `clean(...)` → `{mesh, pointsWelded, ...}`. CLI `clean IN OUT [--weld --atol --remove-orphans --drop-degenerate --drop-duplicates]` (no step flags = default set).
  - **Crop** (`operations/crop.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_crop.py`): `crop_bbox(mesh, lo[3], hi[3], CropMode::All|Any, record_ids=false)` and `crop_halfspace(mesh, point[3], normal[3], mode, record_ids)` → `CropResult{mMesh, mPointMap, mCellMaps}` — subset by AABB (`lo≤p≤hi`) or half-space (`(p−point)·normal≥0`). Per-point inside mask in `parallel_for`; keep cell if ALL (default) or ANY node inside (phase-split since v8.5.0: parallel per-cell flag pass, serial ascending compaction); then `detail::build_cell_subset` prunes points + remaps (its remap/gather/map loops `parallel_for_bw` since v8.5.0). `record_ids` attaches Int64 `crop:original_point_id` point_data + `crop:original_cell_id` cell_data. Regions (and so sets) remapped in the C++ core via `detail/region_remap.hpp`. C API `mio_crop_bbox`/`mio_crop_plane(..., mode/*0=all,1=any*/, record_ids)` → `mio_mesh*`. Fortran `m%crop_bbox(lo,hi,mode=...)`/`m%crop_plane(point,normal,mode=...)`. WASM `cropBbox`/`cropPlane`. CLI `crop IN OUT --bbox …|--plane … [--mode all|any --record-ids]` (negatives need `--bbox=` form). Python API `crop(mesh, bbox=…|plane=(pt,nrm), mode, record_ids)`.
  - **Split** (`operations/split.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_split.py`): `split(mesh, SplitBy::Type|Component|Tag|Region, tag_name="")` → `SplitResult{vector<SplitPiece{mKey, mMesh, mPointMap, mCellMaps}>}` — partition into submeshes. **Type**: one piece per first-seen cell type. **Component**: union-find (`split_uf_*`) over node-sharing cell adjacency, one piece per connected component (keys `"0"`,`"1"`,…). **Tag**: distinct integer values of a named `cell_data` (auto-detect first integer cell_data if empty; keys = value). **Region** (v8.7.0, string `"regions"` — plural, deliberately distinct from the pre-existing singular `"region"`, which stays an untouched alias for `Tag`): one piece per named `RegionKind::Cell` region (`rMesh.RegionNames()` filtered to Cell, entries resolved through `detail::block_bases`/`global_to_block_row` — never re-derived), key = region name. **The one criterion that is not a partition** — regions may overlap, so a cell can land in zero, one, or several pieces, each region processed independently; Point/Side regions produce no piece at all (split's contract is whole submeshes, and there is no sound default for "these facets alone"). Each piece pruned via `detail::build_cell_subset(..., drop_empty_blocks=true)`, which **already remaps every region onto every piece** (via `detail::remap_regions`) regardless of criterion, so `Region` needed no extra plumbing there. **File-writing is a caller concern** — the core returns pieces; the CLI/Python expand the `out_{key}.vtu` pattern. Python `split(mesh, by="type"|"region"|"regions"|"component", tag=None)` → ordered `dict {key: Mesh}`; **`by="region"` keeps its exact pre-8.7.0 behaviour untouched** (`cell_sets` when present, Python-only, else the integer tag) — `by="regions"` is the new, additive, cross-binding-capable criterion, with a pure-Python `_groups_by_region` fallback mirroring the C++ one when `_core` is unavailable. Since `split_by_from_name` is the single free-string dispatch point every binding already goes through (C API/Fortran/Julia/R/WASM's `by` parameter), adding `Region` there was the *entire* cross-binding cost — no per-binding plumbing was needed, unlike a genuinely new field. Sets remapped per piece in the shim (for the legacy `by="region"` Python path only; the C++ `"regions"` path carries regions itself). C API opaque `mio_split_result` (`mio_split(mesh, by, tag_name)` + `_count`/`_key`/`_mesh`/`_take_mesh`/`_free`). Fortran `m%split(by, tag_name=..., keys=...)` → `type(mio_mesh) array`. WASM `split(mesh, by, tagName)` → array of `{key, mesh}`. CLI `split IN OUT_{key}.EXT --by type|region|regions|component [--tag NAME]`. A companion **`regions` CLI verb** (v8.7.0, both CLIs, `_cli/_regions.py` / `cmd_regions` in `main.cpp`) lists a mesh's regions (name/kind/dim/tag/entry count, optionally `--json`) by going through `read_metadata`/`registry_read_metadata` rather than a full read — the direct consumer of the `MeshMetadata::mRegions` work above.
  - **Stats** (`operations/stats.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_stats.py`): `compute_stats(mesh)` → `StatsReport{mNumPoints, mNumCells, mBBoxMin/Max/Extent/Centroid[3], mCellTypeCounts(vector<pair<name,count>>, first-seen), mTotalArea, mSignedVolume, mUnsignedVolume, mNumInverted}` — read-only geometric stats (the geometric complement to the topological `info`). bbox/centroid via chunked `parallel_for` reduction; per-cell area (tri/quad Newell) and 3D signed volume (divergence theorem over `cell_faces`, `parallel_for`); total area = 2D-cell areas + boundary area of 3D cells (via `extract_surface`); inverted = 3D cells with negative signed volume. pybind/Python/WASM return a dict/object incl. `cell_type_counts`; **C/Fortran fill a scalar `mio_stats_report` struct (per-cell-type counts omitted — documented flat-ABI gap)**. C API `mio_stats(mesh, mio_stats_report*)`. Fortran `report = m%stats()` (`type(mio_stats_report)`, `bind(c)`). WASM `stats(mesh)`. CLI `stats IN [--json]` (human table or JSON).
  - **Cell conversion** (`operations/convert_cells.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_convert_cells.py`): `convert_cells(mesh, ConvertCellsOptions{mode, record_parent_ids})` → `ConvertCellsResult{mMesh, mPointMap, mCellMaps}` — converts the *element representation*, not the format. Three modes. **Linearize**: every higher-order cell → its linear base via the new `ccells_linear_base` table (line3→line, tetra10→tetra, hexahedron20/24/27/64→hexahedron, ...); corner nodes always lead the meshio/VTK orderings so it is "keep the leading N columns", never a permutation; then points orphaned by the drop are pruned with surface.cpp's `used`/`remap` + byte-`memcpy` pattern (**`detail::build_cell_subset` is NOT reusable** — it is a pure row/point selector that preserves each block's type and node count). **Simplexify**: fixed templates — quad→2 triangle, polygon(n)→(n−2)-triangle fan (both the jagged *and* the rectangular uniform-n-gon storage shape), pyramid→2 tetra, wedge→3 tetra, hexahedron→6 tetra (canonical Freudenthal fan around the main diagonal 0–6, so it conforms across a shared face only when neighbours agree on the diagonal — fixed, not per-cell, for determinism); higher-order input is linearized first (which also prunes what the decomposition would orphan); points untouched, each parent's `cell_data` row replicated to its children; a polyhedron block raises. **Elevate**: linear → serendipity quadratic (triangle→triangle6, hexahedron→hexahedron20, ...), one new node per unique edge at the midpoint with `point_data` = endpoint mean; the full-Lagrange targets needing face/body centres (quad9, hexahedron27) raise by name — an explicit v1 non-goal. Edge orderings come from the shared `detail/cell_subdivision.hpp` (`cell_refine_edges`) because the order is a contract with each quadratic type's node layout (tetra10 nodes 4–9 = edges 01,12,02,03,13,23) and **refine needs the identical order**; `ccells_elevate_table` now only pairs each source type with its target and corner count. **Every mode preserves block structure 1:1** (output has exactly `NumCellBlocks()` blocks in input order) which is what keeps the one-array-per-block `cell_data` invariant trivially correct. All three are **idempotent on cells they do not apply to**, so they are safe on a mixed-order mesh. Determinism: templates are fixed, and the elevate mid-edge numbering comes from a **serial** dedup pass over a `parallel_for`-filled disjoint-slot buffer (surface.cpp's phase-split idiom), never a concurrent hash insert. `record_parent_ids` attaches Int64 `convert:parent_cell` cell_data (per-output-cell source index *within its own block*). Regions (and so sets) are remapped in the C++ core via `detail/region_remap.hpp`'s `FirstChild` map shape — under simplexify a cell region **expands to the parent's children** (contiguous, derived from `mCellMaps`' first-child indices); side regions are dropped by name, since a child cell's facets have no correspondence with the parent's. C API uses an opaque `mio_convert_cells_result` (`_mesh`/`_take_mesh`/zero-copy `_point_map`/`_num_cell_maps`/`_cell_map`/`_free`, modelled on `mio_split_result`). Fortran `m%convert_cells(mode [, record_parent_ids, point_map])`. WASM `convertCells(mesh, mode, recordParentIds)`. CLI verb `convert-cells IN OUT --mode linearize|simplexify|elevate [--record-parent-ids]` — deliberately distinct from the format-conversion `convert` verb.
  - **Polyhedral refinement** (`operations/subdivide.{hpp,cpp}`, docs [`doc/subdivide.md`](doc/subdivide.md), v10.3.0, closing roadmap §1's polyhedral-refinement half): `subdivide(mesh, SubdivideOptions{record_parent_ids})` → `SubdivideResult{mMesh, mCellMaps}` — splits every eligible 3D cell into one polyhedral child per face, connected to a new interior point. `refine` and `decimate` both raise by name on a polyhedron, pointing at `convert_cells(Simplexify)` — both are built on fixed same-type subdivision templates, and an arbitrary polyhedron has none. `subdivide` needs **no per-type table at all**: it goes through `detail::cell_rings` — the same uniform face-ring abstraction `gradient`/`compute_quality` already use — which treats a tabulated type (reduced to corners for a quadratic variant) and an existing polyhedron block identically, so the same code handles every 3D cell type the mesh already supports. Per cell: `cell_rings` → `orient_rings` (throw `Unorientable`, the same guard `convert_cells`'s own polyhedron branch uses) → one new interior point, the plain average of the cell's own corner nodes (`rings.mNodes`) — deliberately **not** `poly_measure().mCentroid`, a different, volume-weighted point; using the corner average is what makes the children's total volume *literally the same sum* as the parent's own volume computation, checkable to a tight tolerance rather than merely equal by the divergence theorem. For each face, one polyhedron child is emitted whose boundary is that face **unchanged** (original winding, so a neighbouring cell across it still sees the identical face) plus one new triangle per face edge back to the interior point — **automatically conforming**, since a shared face between two input cells is never touched, so unlike `refine` there is no closure, no 2:1 balance, no hanging-node bookkeeping. **One polyhedron output block per input block, with genuinely mixed cell shapes inside — no grouping by distinct node count.** `AddPolyhedronBlock` stores cells as ragged CSR with no same-shape constraint (a single wedge cell's 5 faces already produce 2 tetrahedron-shaped children and 3 pyramid-shaped children in one coherent block); the `polyhedronN`-per-node-count convention some *readers* (CGNS's `NFACE_n`, OpenFOAM, EnSight) use is a format-compatibility choice those readers make, not a mesh-API requirement. Keeping one output block per input block also keeps the block correspondence 1:1, which is what lets regions carry through `detail::region_remap.hpp`'s `CellMapKind::FirstChild` (`convert_cells`'s own one-to-many shape) with **no `mPointMap`** at all — subdivide never prunes or renumbers an original point, so a null map (identity) is exactly correct, and new apex points are by construction unreferenced by any existing Point region. Point and Cell regions survive; named **Side** regions do not (`FirstChild` drops them unconditionally, the same limitation `convert_cells(Simplexify)` already has). Non-3D blocks and 3D blocks with no `cell_faces` row (the full-Lagrange family — `hexahedron64`, `tetra20`, `VTK_LAGRANGE_*`) pass through unchanged. `record_parent_ids` attaches Int64 `subdivide:parent_cell` cell_data (per-output-cell source index within its own block), replicated by raw byte memcpy per row (`convert_cells.cpp`'s own pattern for the identical "each child inherits its parent's row" shape). **C++-core only, with no numpy fallback at all** — unlike most operations, which fall back to a pure-Python reference: the winding repair is a discrete branch on the sign of an enclosed volume, and `_subdivide.py`'s public function unconditionally raises `NotImplementedError` naming the reason when `_core` is unavailable, for any input, following `_convert_cells.py:142`'s and `_smooth.py`'s precedent for exactly this class of guard. **A real, independently-discovered bug surfaced and was fixed while wiring the WASM binding**: `has_skinnable_cells` (`surface.cpp`, used to gate `convertSurfaceOps`/`convert_surface` and the `skin=true` default on the STL/PLY/SVG/TikZ writers) excluded **every** ragged block including polyhedra, even though `extract_surface`/`extract_skin` have supported polyhedron blocks since v9.16.0 — so a mesh with only a polyhedron block (subdivide's own output being the first thing to construct one) silently took the unskinned fallback path everywhere `has_skinnable_cells` gates. Fixed by adding the same `cb.IsPolyhedron() || ...` exception `surface_extract`'s own pre-scan already makes; pinned by `Skin.HasSkinnableCellsRecognizesAPolyhedronOnlyMesh`. C API opaque `mio_subdivide_result` (`_mesh`/`_take_mesh`/`_num_cell_maps`/`_cell_map`/`_free`, modelled on `mio_convert_cells_result` but **without** a point-map accessor). Fortran `m%subdivide([record_parent_ids, stat, errmsg])`. Julia `subdivide(mesh; record_parent_ids=false)` → `(; mesh, cell_maps)`. R `mio_subdivide(mesh, record_parent_ids=FALSE)` → a named list of `mesh`/`cell_maps` (its own `subdivide_cell_maps` helper, since `result_maps()` assumes a point map every other opaque-result op has). WASM `subdivide(mesh, recordParentIds)`, reachable generically as a `Subdivide` pipeline step (`{RecordParentIds}` in both `pipe_op_table()` and `_OP_TABLE`) with no extra WASM dispatch code needed, since `apply_one_op`/`convertSurfaceOps` already route through the shared `apply_pipeline_step`. CLI verb `subdivide IN OUT [--record-parent-ids]` in both CLIs.
  - **Polyhedral coarsening** (`operations/agglomerate.{hpp,cpp}`, docs [`doc/agglomerate.md`](doc/agglomerate.md), v10.4.0, closing roadmap §1's polyhedral-coarsening half — the last piece of the "polyhedral refinement and coarsening" gap, `subdivide`'s many-to-one sibling): `agglomerate(mesh, AgglomerateOptions{target_group_size})` → `AgglomerateResult{mMesh, mCellMap}` — merges groups of cells into single larger polyhedral cells. `decimate` still raises by name on a polyhedron, pointing at `convert_cells(Simplexify)` — its fixed-template QEM edge collapse has no analogue for merging arbitrary polyhedral cells; `agglomerate` is a genuinely different algorithm, **greedy seed-and-grow over the shared-face dual** (`detail::build_global_faces`'s `mOwner`/`mNeighbour` compact-space pairing — the same machinery the OpenFOAM writer and the CGNS `NGON_n`/`NFACE_n` writer share), never `detail/cell_adjacency.hpp`'s node-adjacency (`partition`'s ghost layers, `gradient`'s stencil): merging on shared-node adjacency could fuse two cells touching only at a pinch-point vertex, and the two headers key cells in genuinely different index spaces (global block-major vs compact volume-cell) that would silently misindex if mixed. A mesh containing a **non-manifold** face (used by 3+ cells) is refused by name — the owner/neighbour classification is only well-defined on a 2-manifold face, mirroring `subdivide`'s own throw-on-`Unorientable` precedent. Seeds are chosen by ascending compact id; a group absorbs its unclaimed face-neighbour with the largest **accumulated** shared-face area (summed over every face the group's current members share with that neighbour, via an ordered `std::set<FrontierKey>` keyed `(-area, id)` so ties break on ascending id), until `mTargetGroupSize` is reached or the frontier empties (a short group at a mesh boundary or pocket is expected, not an error); `mTargetGroupSize=1` is a true identity grouping. **Emit**: for each group, walk every member's `CellFaces(c)` row; a face whose `other` side is also in the group is internal and dropped (both sides hit this branch, so it is never double-counted); every other face is kept, wound forward or reversed exactly per the sign `mCellFaces` already stores for that member — a direct transcription of the CGNS `NFACE_n` writer's own sign handling, needing no new orientation logic since the merged cell simply inherits each member's local winding at its surviving faces. **One polyhedron output block total, mixed shapes inside — the same no-node-count-grouping simplification `subdivide` established.** Non-volume blocks (2D/1D, or any 3D block with no `cell_faces` row) pass through unchanged, in original relative order; the merged block is emitted at the position the **first** original volume block occupied, so a mesh whose volume cells already form one contiguous run keeps its overall block order. **Points are never compacted** — `subdivide`'s own precedent (never prune/renumber; `clean(remove_orphans=True)` is the documented follow-up), so `mPointMap` stays null (identity) and `point_data`/`field_data` need no remapping at all. `cell_data` for a pass-through block is copied verbatim; for the merged block, each group's row is its **first member's** row (ascending order within the group, the same keep-first convention `clean`'s point weld already uses — there is no single principled value for e.g. a material tag once cells with possibly different values merge); an array whose block count does not match the input mesh is dropped with a `log::warn` rather than guessed at. **Regions use `CellMapKind::Global`, confirmed simpler than `merge`'s own usage of it, not an adaptation**: `merge.cpp` drives `remap_region` in a per-input-mesh loop *specifically for region-name namespacing on collision* across its N inputs — a concern that does not exist for agglomerate's single input mesh, which builds **one** flat map sized to `detail::total_cells(detail::block_bases(rMesh))` (filled via `gf.mCellToGlobal` for grouped cells, a separate identity pass for pass-through blocks' own global range) and calls the **batch** `detail::remap_regions()` **once**. Point and Cell regions survive; named **Side** regions never do with real entries (a merged cell's type is always `"polyhedron"`, which never matches an original type name, so `remap_region`'s Side branch drops every entry) but the region is still *carried* as a named empty group, the same "the name is information" convention every operation here follows — verified by a dedicated test, since an earlier draft of this operation's own test assumed outright removal and was wrong. **C++-core only, with no numpy fallback at all** — the same reasoning `subdivide` already documents: the emit step depends transitively on `orient_rings`-repaired `GlobalFaces`, a discrete winding branch a second implementation could disagree with near-degenerate cells; `_agglomerate.py` unconditionally raises `NotImplementedError` when `_core` is unavailable. C API opaque `mio_agglomerate_result` (`_mesh`/`_take_mesh`/`_cell_map`/`_free` — **no `block` parameter on `_cell_map`, and correspondingly no `_num_cell_maps` accessor at all**, unlike `mio_subdivide_result`: there is exactly one flat array, an output cell's index being a function of which group it joined rather than which input block it came from). Fortran `m%agglomerate([target_group_size, stat, errmsg])`. Julia `agglomerate(mesh; target_group_size=8)` → `(; mesh, cell_map)` (singular, flat, via the existing single-array `_result_map` helper — `_result_cell_maps`'s block-indexed helper does not fit). R `mio_agglomerate(mesh, target_group_size=8)` → a named list of `mesh`/`cell_map` (its own small single-array reader, `subdivide_cell_maps`'s per-block helper likewise not reused). WASM `agglomerate(mesh, targetGroupSize)` (plain `int`, not `int64_t`, matching every other WASM count-like parameter — e.g. `refine`'s `levels`, `partition`'s `nparts` — to avoid forcing BigInt marshaling on the JS caller for what is never more than a small count), reachable generically as an `Agglomerate` pipeline step (`{TargetGroupSize}` in both `pipe_op_table()` and `_OP_TABLE`) with no extra WASM dispatch code needed. CLI verb `agglomerate IN OUT [--target-group-size N]` in both CLIs.
  - **Refinement** (`operations/refine.{hpp,cpp}` + `detail/refine_templates.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_refine.py` + `_refine_templates.py`, docs [`doc/refine.md`](doc/refine.md)): `refine(mesh, RefineOptions{levels, record_parent_ids, cells, region, predicate_array/op/value, closure, record_levels})` → `RefineResult{mMesh, mPointMap, mCellMaps}` — subdivide cells into congruent children of the **same** type, the resolution-*increasing* counterpart to convert_cells. With no selector every cell is refined (**uniform**, byte-identical to pre-v9.5.0); with one, only those cells are, and the hanging nodes that leaves are closed up so the output is still conforming (**selective/adaptive**). Fixed full-split templates: line→2, triangle→4 (1-to-4), quad→4 (+face centre), tetra→8, wedge→8, hexahedron→8; local new-node ids coincide with each type's full-Lagrange layout (line3/triangle6/quad9/tetra10/wedge18/hexahedron27), which is what makes the tables readable against the reference elements. Tetra's interior diagonal is the fixed opposite-edge pair 4–9 (ring 6→7→8→5) — for **determinism only**, since it is strictly interior and so, unlike convert_cells' hex-simplexify diagonal 0–6, never affects conformity. A wedge refines as triangle-4-split × 2 levels and its mid-level triangle's edge midpoints **are** its 3 quad-face centres, hence no body node. **The whole operation is driven by one set — which EDGES carry a node — and everything else is DERIVED from it**: an edge node iff the edge is split, a quad-face centre iff **all four** of that face's edges are, a hex body node iff **all twelve** are. Two cells sharing an entity read the same edges, so they cannot disagree — conformity is structural rather than sampled — and with every edge split the rules collapse into the uniform templates, which is what makes uniform output byte-identical. One `RefineNodeKey = array<int64,4>` map serves both entity kinds, edges keyed `{-1,-1,a,b}` and faces `sort{p,q,r,s}`, so the key spaces cannot collide by construction (surface.cpp's padding trick) and the arity is recoverable from the key. Phase-split determinism: `parallel_for` key fill into disjoint slots → **serial** first-seen dedup numbering ENTITIES (point ids are handed out in that same first-seen order once phase 4 decides which entities earn one) → `parallel_for_bw` coordinate/connectivity/data passes; body centres are numbered after the deduped range. **`detail/refine_templates.hpp` owns the (type, split-edge mask) tables.** Each type's **admissible** masks are closed under intersection and contain the full mask — a Moore family — so `refine_promote_mask` (the smallest admissible superset) is a **monotone idempotent closure operator** and the mesh-wide fixed point it drives is unique and independent of traversal order: determinism follows from the algebra, not from a convention. `RefineClosure::RedGreen` (default) promotes to that superset; `Propagate` promotes any non-empty mask straight to full, which is always conforming for every cell type but **converges to uniform refinement of the whole edge-connected component** — the always-works baseline and the test oracle, not an adaptivity mode. **`Balanced` is the third and the only one whose cost is bounded by the selection**: it does not close at all, splitting a cell fully or not at all and merely enforcing 2:1 balance (a cell is drawn in iff some cell sharing a **NODE** with it would end up more than one level above it). Node adjacency is load-bearing, not stylistic -- across a hanging interface the coarse cell spans a whole edge while the fine cell has half of it, so they are *different* entities and an edge-keyed rule is blind to exactly the coarse/fine adjacency 2:1 balance polices (it was written edge-keyed first and the invariant test then read a max level gap of 0, i.e. it silently saw no adjacency at all). On a mesh of uniform level nothing propagates: one cell of a 4x4x4 hex block costs 64->71 cells, against 125 RedGreen and 512 Propagate, and balancing only bites from the second pass onwards. The price is that the output is **1-irregular and NOT conforming** -- every constrained node is reported in the Int64 `refine:hanging` `point_data` array (`kRefineHangingName`, attached only when there are any, so the conforming closures are unaffected), and the rule generating it is stated over *entities* ("a node whose entity some emitted cell touches without referencing it") rather than per closure, which is what makes it cover quad-face centres as well as edge midpoints -- the test oracle initially missed exactly those. **v9.23.0 fixed two defects that only a SECOND balanced pass could show, and both are worth not rediscovering.** (1) That rule must be evaluated over the **emitted** cells, not the input cells' entities: a cell the balance rule draws in is split into children whose own sub-edges the input cell never had, so a node the neighbouring refinement places on one of them is constrained without any input entity naming it -- checking the input reported 42 of 84 constrained nodes on a two-level balanced refine of a 4x4x4 hex block, and 90 of 402 at three levels, against a doc that promises "neither a superset nor a subset". (2) Worse, the mesh was **torn**, not merely 1-irregular: when balance drew in a coarse cell whose edge already held a hanging node, that cell's refinement keyed the edge by its endpoints and allocated a *fresh* midpoint, leaving both sides referencing distinct but exactly coincident nodes (12 such positions at `levels=2`, 96 at `levels=3`). The fix is **`refine:entity`** (`kRefineEntityName`), an Int64 `(n,4)` `point_data` array recording each point's originating entity -- literally the `RefineNodeKey`, `{-1,-1,a,b}` for an edge midpoint, sorted `{p,q,r,s}` for a quad-face centre, all -1 for a point refine did not create (an original point, or a body centre, which is interior to one cell and can never be shared) -- so a later pass **reuses** the existing node. Reserved and *maintained* like `refine:level`: attached only when a pass leaves hanging nodes and kept thereafter, so RedGreen/Propagate/uniform output stays byte-identical. Both reserved point arrays are **excluded from the generic `point_data` carry loop**, which was interpolating the Int64 hanging flag to 0.5 and truncating it. Entries name point indices, which refine never renumbers but `reorder`/`clean`/`crop`/`merge` do and `transform`/`smooth` move, so a **stale-key guard** (every entry must still reproduce its own point's coordinates, by the same arithmetic that wrote them, round-tripped through the points' own dtype) warns and drops the array rather than grafting one entity's node onto another. The two oracles -- distinct referenced node ids at one position, and set equality against an independently recomputed constrained set -- were each verified to fire by sabotage, and they fire on *different* sabotages. Admissible sets: all masks for line/triangle; the two **opposite** edge pairs for quad (a quadrangulation of an n-gon satisfies `4Q = B + 2I`, so an odd boundary count is impossible and one or three split edges have NO all-quad subdivision at any number of interior nodes — promoting to the opposite pair is the finest type-preserving answer and confines a refined quad to one grid row); unions of the three parallel edge classes for hexahedron (one dual sheet); triangle-edges / verticals / both for wedge; and for tetra any mask up to two edges plus the four face-triples (three edges at a common vertex are excluded **deliberately** — they would put the ambiguous two-edge case on all three incident faces). The 27 tetra entries are **generated** from six orbit representatives by the 12 **even** permutations of its corners (even ⇒ orientation-preserving ⇒ children inherit positive winding), never transcribed; the promotion table is built by brute force straight from the definition. **Green children always have the parent's cell type**, which is what keeps output blocks 1:1 with input blocks and therefore leaves `mCellMaps`/`FirstChild`/`refine:parent_cell`/`cell_data` slicing and every binding's map shape untouched — mixed-type green (quad→triangles) is a documented non-goal for exactly that reason. A cell with two *adjacent* split edges on one face leaves a quad remnant needing a diagonal, and the neighbour across that face must agree: the template stores **both** variants plus two local corner ids, and the emitter picks by **global node id** (diagonal from the smaller-id surviving corner), never by local numbering. Per-cell child counts vary, so `mCellMaps` comes from a **serial ascending prefix sum** (still monotone, still contiguous, every entry non-negative — an untouched cell is a run of length 1) and `cell_data` is a `parent_of_child` **gather** rather than an `i/nchildren` divide. New-node coordinates and `point_data` are the **mean of the entity's corners** (order-independent, so neighbours agree bit-for-bit with no tie-break); dtype is preserved. `levels` loops `refine_once` borrow-then-move (KRATOS `Mesh` is not copy-constructible) and composes maps **before** the move, with level k>1 seeded by the children of level k−1's fully split cells; `record_parent_ids` names the **original** ancestor. **`refine:level`** (opt-in `mRecordLevels`, `kRefineLevelName`) records each cell's depth: +1 per full split, a green child **inherits** its parent's level (a closure is not a refinement). The name is **reserved** — an input already carrying it is *updated* rather than replicated whatever the flag says, so successive passes accumulate; that is the one observable behaviour change in v9.5.0. Green cells are **not undone** by `refine` itself before a later refinement pass over them, so repeated selective passes directly on `refine`'s own output degrade quality without bound -- `undo_green` (below) is the answer, reading a green group's original connectivity back from the caller-retained coarse mesh rather than reconstructing it.

**v10.1.0 added the persistent hierarchy `RefineOptions::mRecordHierarchy` roadmap §1 asked for**: two new reserved Int64 `cell_data` arrays, `kRefineCellIdName`/`kRefineParentIdName` (`"refine:cell_id"`/`"refine:parent_id"`), on the exact reserved-and-maintained pattern `refine:level` already establishes. **The hierarchy is a link between two meshes, not a tree inside one**: a multigrid (or future green-undo) caller keeps every pass's OUTPUT mesh, and the fine mesh's `parent_id` resolves against the coarse mesh's `cell_id` — implicit (the mesh's global block-major index, via `detail::block_bases`/`detail::total_cells`, never re-derived) when the coarse mesh carries none of its own. An unsplit cell keeps its own id and is its own parent (`parent_id == cell_id`); every cell of a split run gets a fresh id and carries the run's parent id. **Computed entirely post-hoc**, in one new function `refine_attach_hierarchy` (beside `refine_attach_parent_ids`, same shape) called once after the whole `levels` loop — never inside `refine_once` and never per internal pass — because `RefineResult::mCellMaps` already composes through every internal level via `refine_compose_maps`, so "the mesh passed to this call" is unambiguous regardless of `mLevels` with no per-pass bookkeeping at all: a composed run of length one (only ever possible when its mask was 0, since every admissible non-empty mask yields more than one child) is a cell no level ever split and keeps its own identity; every other run gets fresh ids from a single counter walking the final mesh's own block/cell order (`id_base = max(existing cell_id, existing parent_id) + 1`, taken over BOTH arrays defensively — a parent's id can outlive its own row once split, though a lineage produced entirely by this operation never actually needs the `parent_id` term, since child ids always exceed their own parent's). This is exactly why rules 1 and 6 do NOT need separate per-level handling the way an earlier design sketch assumed: `refine:parent_cell`'s "original ancestor, not the immediate parent" behavior falls out for free from the same composed-map resolution, pinned by a dedicated test asserting the two arrays induce an identical partition. Given both meshes, red/green/untouched classify with **no third array**: untouched has `parent_id == cell_id`; green has `parent_id != cell_id` and the same `refine:level` as its parent; red has `refine:level` one more than its parent's. `mRecordHierarchy` also **forces `refine:entity` to be attached** (a new `ForceEntity` parameter threaded into `refine_once`, `bool` alongside `RecordLevels`) even when the closure leaves no hanging node — the normal `redgreen`/`propagate` case, where it would otherwise never appear — since that array already records exactly which 2 or 4 coarse nodes each new fine node is the mean of: combined with `mPointMap` being the identity, it completes the multigrid coarse→fine transfer operator (prolongation stencil + weights) for free, closing the half of the roadmap motivation cell parentage alone could not reach. The uniqueness guard (`refine_read_hierarchy`, a `RefineHierarchyState{Absent,Valid,Invalid}` tri-state mirroring `refine_read_entity_keys`'s staleness policy) drops both arrays with a warning — never trusts, never throws — on a duplicate or negative id, e.g. from `merge`-ing two independently-hierarchied meshes; both new names join the phase-8 cell_data skip list alongside `kRefineLevelName`. **Interacts with `split`**: an unqualified `split(mesh, by="tag")` auto-picks the first sorted integer `cell_data` array, and `"refine:cell_id"` sorts ahead of ordinary material tags — name the array explicitly on a mesh carrying it (documented, not fixed — orthogonal to `split.cpp`, whose own auto-pick logic is untouched); the deliberate flip side is that `split(mesh, by="tag", tag="refine:parent_id")` **is** the sibling-grouping recipe (one piece per distinct parent, singleton per untouched cell). `RefineResult` is **unchanged** — the arrays ride the mesh, so no new C accessor/Fortran out-arg/Julia-R return key/pipeline counter was needed. **ABI**: `RefineOptions` gaining `mRecordHierarchy` bumped `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_ABI_VERSION` 6→7 (`RefineOptions` is passed by const-ref through the exported `refine()`) and closed two guard gaps the v9.12.0 pipeline row had already named the failure mode of: `RefineOptions` is now pinned in `tests/cpp/test_abi_layout.cpp` (it never had been, despite `SdfOptions`/`VoxelOptions` being pinned from the release that introduced them), and the Fortran `mio_refine_opts_t` mirror gained a runtime `check_refine_opts_layout()` size guard (the Julia `_check_abi_layout()` precedent, since Fortran has no compile-time `static_assert` and was previously the only unguarded mirror of the three). On the flat ABI `record_hierarchy` is an `int64_t` consuming one of `mio_refine_opts`'s former `reserved[6]` slots (→ `reserved[5]`) — the `mio_read_opts` `lenient` precedent, not the `reserved_pad` int32 (which stays available for a future int32 option) — so `sizeof(mio_refine_opts)` stays 112 and the `static_assert` pinning it is unchanged. Selection is resolved once against the **input** mesh: `mCells` are global block-major indices (via `detail/cell_index.hpp`, bounds-checked), `mRegion` resolves Cell→its cells / Point→every cell with ANY node in it / Side→**error** (a facet is not a cell), and the predicate is a scalar `cell_data` threshold whose **non-finite values never match** — `compute_quality` reports NaN where a metric does not apply, so rejecting the array would break `quality:*` predicates, the headline use case. Setting two selectors is an error, not a precedence rule. Volume is exact for line/triangle/quad/tetra always but for wedge/hexahedron **only when the parent is affine** — a general trilinear hex's children do not sum to the parent (geometry, not a bug), so conservation tests must use an affine fixture. Higher-order cells, **pyramid** (6-pyramid+4-tetra breaks the same-type and 1:1-block invariants) and ragged blocks **raise by name**. Output is byte-identical across the three mesh backends, thread counts, and the C++/numpy boundary (`test_cpp_matches_python_selective`); the numpy twin's tables are checked against the C++ ones through `_core.refine_mask_table`, not transcribed. Conformity is pinned by three oracles in `test_refine.cpp`, none redundant: facet counting, **no output node at another cell's edge midpoint** (the one that actually finds a hanging node — facet counting cannot, since a hanging node leaves 1 big + 2 small facets all at count 1), and boundary-area preservation; `TheConformityOraclesActuallyFire` proves they are not inert. `test_refine_templates.cpp` asserts the algebra directly (Moore family, closure operator, tiling, orientation, that a template only references nodes its own mask creates). C API opaque `mio_refine_result` plus **`mio_refine_ex` + `mio_refine_opts`** (`mio_read_opts`' append-only `reserved` discipline, `sizeof` static_asserted at 112 and mirrored in Fortran/Julia, whose load-time layout guard caught a wrong constant during development); `mio_refine` unchanged and delegating. Fortran `m%refine(levels [, record_parent_ids, point_map, cells, region, where_array, where_op, where_value, closure, record_levels, record_hierarchy])` (new optionals appended **after** `errmsg` so no positional call shifts). Julia/R keyword args, `cells` 1-based on both, `record_hierarchy` too. WASM `refine(mesh, levels, recordParentIds, options)` where `options` also takes `recordHierarchy` + the `convertSurfaceOps` `{op:'refine', ...}` branch (dispatched generically off `pipeline_op_table()` since v9.11.0, so `RecordHierarchy` joining that table's `"Refine"` row is the entire WASM-pipeline cost) — where the comparison key is **`compare`, not `op`**, since `op` is the step's own discriminant. CLI verb `refine IN OUT [--levels N] [--record-parent-ids] [--cells i,j,k | --region NAME | --where "q < 0.3"] [--closure redgreen|propagate] [--record-levels] [--record-hierarchy]` in both CLIs; the browser viewer's Refine chip is **unchanged** (`RecordHierarchy` is a step key the pipeline validator accepts but no chip control sets, exactly as many other option keys already are).
  - **Green-element undo** (`operations/undo_green.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_undo_green.py`, docs [`doc/undo_green.md`](doc/undo_green.md), v10.5.0, closing roadmap §1's green-undo bullet): `undo_green(coarse, fine)` → `UndoGreenResult{mMesh, mCellMaps, mNumGroupsUndone, mNumCellsRemoved}` — restores `refine`'s transitional (green) cells back to their original parent, the missing half of "restore a transitional cell to its parent and re-split from scratch before a new refinement pass" the `refine:level` doc comment above has named since v9.5.0. **A TWO-mesh operation, the repo's second after `interpolate`**: `coarse` is the mesh a prior `refine(coarse, ..., record_hierarchy=True, record_levels=True)` call was run on, `fine` is that call's output. **Design: lookup and substitution, not reconstruction** — the roadmap's own original wording ("invert `refine_templates.cpp`'s subdivision tables against a sibling run") describes genuine graph-isomorphism-style matching against the admissible-mask tables, and turns out to be unnecessary once the two-mesh framing is taken literally: `refine()`'s point map is *always* the identity, so a green parent's exact connectivity and cell_data are already sitting, byte-for-byte, in `coarse` at the row `fine`'s `refine:parent_id` names (resolved against `coarse`'s own `refine:cell_id`, or its implicit global-block-major id when absent — `refine_attach_hierarchy`'s own "start fresh" fallback, reused here rather than re-derived). **Classification is per SIBLING GROUP** (cells sharing one `parent_id`, since a parent's mask — and so its red/green status — is uniform across every child, set once in `refine_once`'s phase 6): a singleton group (`cell_id == parent_id`) is untouched, kept verbatim; a group whose `level` is the coarse parent's own level **+1** is red (a genuine refinement, passed through unchanged); a group whose `level` **equals** the coarse parent's own level is green (substituted, the whole group collapsing to one row read from `coarse`); a group more than one level deeper is refused by name (**only a single-pass, `levels=1`, hierarchy is supported** — a multi-level call's different branches can reach different final depths, which this operation does not attempt to reassemble). **Hoisted, not duplicated**: `refine.cpp`'s private `RefineHierarchyState`/`refine_read_hierarchy` (the Absent/Valid/Invalid guard `refine_attach_hierarchy` already used) moved verbatim into `detail/refine_hierarchy.{hpp,cpp}` so `undo_green` can resolve `coarse`'s id space with the identical logic rather than a second, driftable transcription; `refine.cpp` now includes and calls the shared version, behaviour-preserving (its own hierarchy test suite is the regression guard). **Output keeps `fine`'s own block structure** — same types, same order, unlike `subdivide`/`agglomerate`'s restructuring into polyhedron blocks — since a green substitution only shrinks a block's row count (green children always share their parent's type). **Points are never pruned or renumbered**, which is what makes the substitution a zero-translation row copy: a coarse cell's node ids are already valid indices into `fine`'s own point array. The six reserved `refine:*` arrays (`parent_cell`/`level`/`cell_id`/`parent_id` in `cell_data`, `entity`/`hanging` in `point_data` — a first-attempt version of this operation dropped only the four `cell_data` ones and left `refine:entity` behind, caught by a test that checked `HasPointData` rather than the vacuously-passing `HasCellData`) are unconditionally dropped from the output; every other `cell_data` array is carried, a green row's value copied from the **same-named array on `coarse`** with a dtype/shape pre-check per substituted group (the whole array dropped with a warning, never partially guessed, on any mismatch). **Regions use `CellMapKind::Direct`** — the first genuinely **non-injective** use of `Direct` anywhere in the repo (every existing caller — crop/split/clean/partition/reorder — is injective by construction, confirmed by reading each one; a new `test_region_remap.cpp` case, `ANonInjectiveDirectMapCollapsesDuplicateRegionEntries`, pins the previously-untested shape) — needing **no change to `region_remap.cpp`/`region.cpp`**: `rremap_children`'s `Direct` branch is a plain per-entry lookup with no uniqueness assumption, and `Region::Canonicalize`'s existing sort+unique (run unconditionally by `RegionList::Add`) collapses the duplicates for free. Named **Side** regions do not survive at all (`mDropSideRegions=true`, the `subdivide`/`agglomerate` precedent — a removed green child's facet numbering has no correspondence to the substituted parent's own facets even though the cell type is unchanged); Point regions survive trivially (identity point map). **This operation needs no `RefineOptions` change and no ABI bump** — a correction to the original plan, which assumed a new `RefineOptions` flag (Tier A, would have moved `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_ABI_VERSION` 7→8); the two-mesh substitution design is pure array bookkeeping over already-shipped hierarchy arrays, Tier C additive only (one new header plus one hoisted-but-unchanged detail header). **This is also why, uniquely among this section's recent additions, it has a FULL numpy twin rather than being C++-core-only**: unlike `subdivide`/`agglomerate`, there is no `orient_rings` winding repair or other discrete sign branch anywhere in the algorithm, so a second, independently-written implementation cannot disagree with the first near a degenerate cell — `_undo_green_py` reuses `_refine.py`'s own `_read_hierarchy`/`CELL_ID_NAME`/`PARENT_ID_NAME`/`LEVEL_NAME` rather than re-deriving them, and is pinned byte-identical to the C++ path by `test_cpp_matches_python` (including region parity, via `meshes_equal` plus an explicit region-name/entry comparison, `Region` not being covered by `meshes_equal` itself). **Binding shape mirrors `interpolate`'s two-mesh precedent exactly**: module-level (not type-bound) in Fortran, an ordinary exported symbol in Julia/R (no shadowing risk), a plain two-path MCP tool (`coarse_path`/`fine_path`/`output_path`, no special-casing needed there, unlike the pipeline), and **excluded from the settings pipeline** via the existing `pipe_excluded_hint()` mechanism (`"UndoGreen"` joins `Merge`/`Interpolate`/`Split`/`Diff`, naming the `undo-green` CLI verb) — in WASM specifically this means `{op: 'undoGreen'}` never reaches `pipeline_op_table()` at all, so it surfaces the same generic "unknown operation" catchable `Error` the other three two-mesh-excluded ops already get there, not the C++ engine's more specific excluded-hint text. C API `mio_undo_green(coarse, fine, &num_groups_undone, &num_cells_removed)` → a plain `mio_mesh*` with nullable counters (`mio_smooth`'s shape, not an opaque result handle — there are no index maps exposed on the flat ABI, a documented gap like `mio_smooth`'s `mFrozen`). Fortran `mio_undo_green(coarse, fine [, num_groups_undone, num_cells_removed, stat, errmsg])`. Julia `undo_green(coarse, fine)` → `(; mesh, num_groups_undone, num_cells_removed)`. R `mio_undo_green(coarse, fine)` → a named list of the same three. WASM `undoGreen(coarse, fine)` → `{mesh, numGroupsUndone, numCellsRemoved}`. CLI verb `undo-green COARSE FINE OUT [--quiet]` in both CLIs, positioned right after `refine`'s own verb in each.
  - **Decimation** (`operations/decimate.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_decimate.py`, docs [`doc/decimate.md`](doc/decimate.md)): `decimate(mesh, DecimateOptions{mTargetRatio, mTargetFaces, mMaxError, mPlacement, mPreserveBoundary, mPreserveFeatures, mFeatureAngleDeg, mFrozen})` → `DecimateResult{mMesh, mPointMap, mCellMaps, mFacesRemoved, mPointsRemoved, mCollapsesRejected, mMaxErrorApplied}` — reduce a **surface** mesh's face count by greedy quadric-error-metric (Garland–Heckbert) edge collapse, the resolution-*reducing* inverse of `refine`. **Surface-only by name**: a 3D/polyhedron block throws pointing at `extract_surface` (QEM decimation of a *volume* mesh is a different, much harder problem — deliberately not silently skinned), higher-order 2D throws naming `linearize`, ragged and `line`/`vertex` blocks throw too; `quad`/rectangular-`polygon` blocks are triangulated first via `convert_cells(Simplexify, record_parent_ids)` (marching_prepare's hold-by-value pattern; `convert:parent_cell` is consumed for `mCellMaps` — input-block-indexed, input cell → first surviving triangle, −1 if none — and never forwarded), so **output is all-triangle with block structure 1:1** (possibly 0-row blocks). Exactly one stopping criterion must be set: `mTargetRatio` (fraction of triangulated faces to KEEP, target = `int64(ratio*F + 0.5)` truncation — never `llround`, whose tie rule the numpy twin couldn't match), `mTargetFaces` (a collapse removes 1–2 faces, so the result lands within one collapse), or `mMaxError` (pop-time `err > e` break — valid even on a stale entry since the heap min bounds all). Per-vertex 4×4 quadrics (10-double symmetric, **area-weighted** so the result is independent of how a planar quad was fanned) accumulate **in ascending incident-face index** (the FP-order pin; the numpy twin's `np.add.at` scatter reproduces it exactly); placement `Optimal` = cofactor 3×3 solve with a documented ill-conditioning fallback to midpoint (`|det| <= 1e-12·max|A_ij|³`, scale-invariant — note an exactly planar patch is singular *by construction*, so flat geometry runs on midpoints; expected, not a bug) / `Midpoint` / `Endpoint` (lower error, tie → lower id). **Pinning mirrors smooth's vocabulary**: boundary vertices (once-used-edge test) and feature vertices (pairwise incident-face-normal cos test over *all* faces, not just boundary facets — creases of a closed surface are interior; default 30°) never move and are never removed; pinned–pinned edges never enter the queue (which is what preserves an open patch's outline exactly), a collapse toward a pinned vertex keeps its position, and `mFrozen` unions in. **Guards reject (counted), never abort**: the link condition (`|ring(a)∩ring(b)| == |shared faces|`; >2 shared = non-manifold, never collapsible; two boundary vertices joined by an interior edge = pinch), and a normal-flip sweep (`n_before·n_after <= 0` on every surviving incident face — smooth's "do no harm": an already-degenerate face imposes no constraint; a zero-area survivor yields dot = 0 and is caught by the same test). **The greedy loop is SERIAL** (QEM is inherently sequential) on a `priority_queue` with the total order `(error, lower id, higher id, versions)` and lazy deletion via per-vertex version counters; setup (planes, quadrics, edge keys, seeding scores) is `parallel_for` into disjoint slots with serial dedup/pushes. Float `point_data` blends endpoints at the clamped edge-projection parameter `t` (exact for Midpoint/Endpoint, an approximation for Optimal; accumulated in Float64 working copies per commit, one cast back at the end); **integer `point_data` keeps the survivor's row** (a blended material id is meaningless); cell rows/`field_data` carry through; emission = surface.cpp's used/remap compaction; `mPointMap` maps every input point to its **survivor's** output index (−1 only if the survivor itself became unreferenced). Queue exhaustion before the target (heavy pinning) is a `log::warn` + normal return, never a throw. **Byte-identical across the three backends, thread counts, and the C++/numpy boundary** (`tests/python/test_decimate.py::test_cpp_matches_python`): `_decimate_py` is a full parity twin — vectorized numpy setup, pure-Python `heapq` greedy loop on the same tuples, every branch-feeding expression transcribed token for token (pinned comments on both sides; no FMA contraction across the pinned expressions, slice/interpolate's standing assumption). The shim resolves `frozen` as ids or a `point_sets` name, remaps `point_sets` through `mPointMap` (survivors dedup via `np.unique`) and `cell_sets` through `mCellMaps` (−1 drops), and supports `return_report` (smooth's shape). C API opaque `mio_decimate_result` (`_mesh`/`_take_mesh`/zero-copy `_point_map`/`_num_cell_maps`/`_cell_map`/counter getters `_faces_removed`/`_points_removed`/`_collapses_rejected`/`_max_error_applied`/`_free`, modelled on `mio_refine_result`; `frozen` is a documented flat-ABI gap like smooth's). Fortran `m%decimate(ratio|target_faces|max_error [, placement, preserve_boundary, preserve_features, feature_angle, counter out-args, point_map])`. WASM `decimate(mesh, ratio, targetFaces, maxError, placement, preserveBoundary, preserveFeatures, featureAngle)` → `{mesh, facesRemoved, pointsRemoved, collapsesRejected, maxErrorApplied}` **plus a `convertSurfaceOps` pipeline op** (`{op:"decimate", ...}`, defaulting to `ratio: 0.5` when no criterion is given, so the browser viewer's worker can chain it; the viewer UI chip itself is a follow-up). CLI verb `decimate IN OUT --ratio R|--target-faces N|--max-error E [--placement optimal|midpoint|endpoint --no-preserve-boundary --no-preserve-features --feature-angle A --quiet]` in both CLIs.
  - **Volume decimation** (`operations/decimate_volume.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_decimate_volume.py`, docs [`doc/decimate_volume.md`](doc/decimate_volume.md), v10.6.0, closing roadmap §1's last open item): `decimate_volume(mesh, DecimateVolumeOptions{mTargetRatio, mTargetCells, mMaxError, mPlacement, mPreserveBoundary, mPreserveFeatures, mFeatureAngleDeg, mFrozen})` → `DecimateVolumeResult{mMesh, mPointMap, mCellMaps, mTetsRemoved, mPointsRemoved, mCollapsesRejected, mMaxErrorApplied}` — the volume-mesh sibling of surface `decimate`, greedy QEM **tet**-edge collapse. **A separate operation, not a mode on `decimate`**: `DecimateOptions`/`mio_decimate` are completely untouched, and `decimate()` keeps throwing by name on any 3D block, pointing here — `mio_decimate` has no `reserved`-tail C-ABI growth path at all (unlike `mio_refine_ex`'s `mio_refine_opts`), which is what settled "new operation" as the only viable shape rather than merely the preferred one. **Tet-only**: any non-tetra 3D block (hexahedron/wedge/pyramid/polyhedron/ragged) throws naming `convert_cells(Simplexify)`, and any non-3D block mixed in with the tets throws naming `split` (their nodes would dangle). **Boundary vertices PARTICIPATE by default** (`mPreserveBoundary` defaults **`false`**, the opposite of `decimate`'s own `true`) — a deliberate, harder-than-necessary v1 scope chosen over pinning the boundary outright; setting it `true` reproduces `decimate`'s own behaviour exactly. **The objective is one priority key over two regimes.** Every vertex accumulates a boundary-triangle-only Garland-Heckbert quadric (from `detail::build_global_faces`'s faces with `mNeighbour == -1` — the mesh's own outer skin, in the volume mesh's own node ids, no remapping needed unlike `extract_surface`, which compacts points and returns no point map); a purely interior vertex therefore has an EXACT zero quadric, not merely small. For candidate edge `(a,b)`, the combined quadric `Q(a)+Q(b)` feeds the **identical, unmodified** placement solve `decimate` uses (`detail::decim_place`) whether or not the edge touches the boundary — a zero `Q_ab` is just a degenerate case of the *existing* `|det| <= 1e-12*scale^3` bound, which already falls back to the midpoint, so no interior-specific placement code exists at all. Scoring alone needs an explicit split (a zero-quadric error is identically zero for every interior-interior edge, which would tie the whole interior and fall through to id order): the heap key is `(regime, score, lo, hi, version_lo, version_hi)`, `regime=0` (`score` = quadric error) whenever `Q_ab` is non-degenerate, else `regime=1` (`score` = squared edge length) — every boundary-touching collapse is considered strictly ahead of purely-interior ones. `mMaxError` is only strictly meaningful for `regime==0` entries; compared directly against a `regime==1` entry's squared length it is a documented rough tool for mixed meshes, not a claimed exact criterion — `mTargetRatio`/`mTargetCells` stay exactly well-defined regardless (`target = int64(ratio*T + 0.5)` truncation, `decimate`'s own tie rule). **Shared machinery, hoisted rather than duplicated**: `decimate.cpp`'s placement solve, error form, normal-flip check, ring/shared-face link condition, and the generic per-face-plane/per-vertex-quadric/feature-marking machinery all moved verbatim into `detail/decimate_common.{hpp,cpp}` (`decimate.cpp` now `using`-imports them unchanged) — `decimate.cpp`'s own 16-test suite is the regression guard that this relocation is behaviour-preserving, run both before and after. **Validity guards, tet-only so simpler than the general 3D case**: `T(ab)` (the tets shared by both endpoints) via sorted-vector intersection of two hand-maintained `vtets[v]` CSR-seeded incidence lists (no shared builder exists for 4-corner cells — `detail::decim_vertex_faces_csr` is hardcoded to 3); the **vertex-link condition** is exact SET equality `Vlink(a) ∩ Vlink(b) == Vlink(ab)` (node adjacency via each vertex's own incident tets vs. the two "opposite" corners of every tet in `T(ab)`) — pure integer sets, no floating point, no cardinality-only shortcut (4-corner tets make a false-positive-by-count real and cheap to rule out exactly); the **duplicate-tet guard** rejects when a surviving tet incident to `a` alone and one incident to `b` alone share the same sorted "opposite triple" (the two would become identical after the substitution — a failure mode the vertex-link check alone does not catch, verified by direct construction rather than assumed); **tet-inversion rejection** (`smooth`'s "do no harm") via `detail::cell_volume_from_corners` on every surviving (non-`T(ab)`) incident tet, before/after the candidate placement, requiring a non-zero `before` to keep the SAME sign in `after` (an already-degenerate tet imposes no constraint). **Boundary-touching collapses additionally run `decimate`'s own 2D guards** — the ring/shared-face link condition and the normal-flip check, both hoisted unchanged — over a second, independently mutable `vboundary[v]`/`boundary` skin structure built once from `build_global_faces` and migrated in lockstep with every commit exactly as `decimate.cpp` migrates its own `vfaces`/`face_alive` (kill `bshared`, then move the removed vertex's remaining alive boundary faces onto the survivor) — so the outer surface cannot tear, pinch or fold independently of the interior guards. **C++-core only, no numpy fallback at all** — like `subdivide`/`agglomerate` and unlike `decimate` itself: the volume-specific guards depend on incrementally maintaining the mesh's own skin through hundreds of collapses, intricate bookkeeping a second, independently-written implementation could silently diverge from; `_decimate_volume.py` raises `NotImplementedError` by name when `_core` is unavailable, for any input. Regions carry via `detail::RegionRemap`/`CellMapKind::Direct` per input tet block (Point/Cell survive, Side dropped by name, the `undo_green`/`convert_cells` precedent) — natively in C++, so unlike the numpy-fallback-having `decimate` the Python shim needs no manual `point_sets`/`cell_sets` remapping at all. Emission mirrors `decimate`'s own phase 7 (used/remap point compaction, per-block `Direct` cell maps `-1` for removed tets, float `point_data` blended at the clamped edge-projection parameter, integer `point_data` keeps the survivor's row, `cell_data`/`field_data` copied verbatim). Block structure kept 1:1 with the input (possibly 0-row blocks), exactly `decimate`'s own rule. Determinism matches `decimate`'s pattern (parallel fixed-FP-order setup, serial greedy loop, lazy version-stamped heap deletion) — no ABI change anywhere in this addition (`MESHIOPLUSPLUS_ABI_VERSION` stays 7; `DecimateOptions`/`mio_decimate` untouched, `mio_decimate_volume` is its own C entry point with its own opaque `mio_decimate_volume_result`, modelled on `mio_decimate_result` but with `Tets`- rather than `Faces`-named counters). C API opaque `mio_decimate_volume_result` (`_mesh`/`_take_mesh`/zero-copy `_point_map`/`_num_cell_maps`/`_cell_map`/counter getters `_tets_removed`/`_points_removed`/`_collapses_rejected`/`_max_error_applied`/`_free`); `frozen` is a documented flat-ABI gap like `decimate`'s own. CLI verb `decimate-volume IN OUT --ratio R|--target-cells N|--max-error E [--placement optimal|midpoint|endpoint --preserve-boundary --no-preserve-features --feature-angle A --quiet]` in both CLIs — note `--preserve-boundary` is opt-**in** here, the mirror image of `decimate`'s opt-out `--no-preserve-boundary`, matching the flipped default. Reachable as a `DecimateVolume` pipeline step (`{Ratio, TargetCells, MaxError, Placement, PreserveBoundary, PreserveFeatures, FeatureAngle}`) in both engines' op tables, dispatched through the same generic mechanism `Subdivide`/`Agglomerate` already use; an MCP tool (`decimate_volume`, closing the parity guard automatically). Fortran/Julia/R/WASM bindings are a recorded follow-up (not yet shipped as of v10.6.0) — every other surface above is complete.
