  - **Remesh** (`operations/remesh.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_remesh.py`, docs [`doc/remesh.md`](doc/remesh.md), v10.10.0, closing roadmap §1's isotropic-core and ACVDQ-feature-preservation bullets; v10.11.0 closed the curvature-gradation and boundaries/manifoldness bullets too, see below): `remesh(mesh, RemeshOptions{mNumClusters, mSubdivide, mSubsampleRatio, mMaxSubdivide, mMaxIterations, mMaxRepairPasses, mMetric, mGradation, mPreserveBoundary})` → `RemeshResult{mMesh, mNumClusters, mNumIterations, mSubdivideApplied, mNumIsolatedClusters, mNumNonManifoldVertices}` — isotropic and feature-preserving surface remeshing by approximated centroidal Voronoi diagram (ACVD) clustering: replace a surface's own triangulation with a new, near-uniformly-sized, well-shaped one at a caller-chosen vertex count. **The one resolution operation that does not work on the input's own triangulation** — `refine` subdivides, `decimate` collapses, `subdivide`/`agglomerate` restructure into polyhedra, `smooth` moves points, all inheriting the input's element shapes; `remesh` instead partitions the surface into clusters and builds the dual, so output quality is a property of the clustering, not the input. **Attribution and licence reasoning** (see `doc/remesh.md` and `CITATION.cff` in full): the reference method is Valette's ACVD, CeCILL-B and VTK-bound — never read or vendored here. The isotropic engine is derived from `pyvista/pyacvd` (`src/clustering.cpp`/`clustering.py`, nanobind), MIT, (c) 2017-2024 The PyVista Developers — itself an independent implementation of Valette & Chassery's published research, not ACVD's code, and VTK-free, so the chain is MIT→MIT requiring only a preserved copyright notice. `RemeshMetric::Quadric` has **no pyacvd counterpart** (pyacvd ships isotropic only) and is an original synthesis over this repo's own pre-existing Garland-Heckbert quadric primitives (`detail/decimate_common.hpp`, shared with `decimate`/`decimate_volume`), never over ACVD's `vtkQEMetricForClustering.h`. **The isotropic algorithm** (Valette & Chassery, CGF 2004): each vertex is an item with a weight (a third of incident triangle area) and a weighted position; a cluster holds only `sgamma = sum(w*x)` and `srho = sum(w)`, making a candidate move O(1). Seeding grows clusters breadth-first from the lowest unassigned vertex — **deliberately RNG-free, unlike pyacvd** — the energy sweep accepts whichever of two candidate moves lowers a "badness" (`-|sgamma|^2/srho`, a sign flip of the maximized energy form with no behaviour change), and repair alternates "split disconnected clusters, re-grow, re-minimize" up to `mMaxRepairPasses`. The dual places one output vertex per cluster's representative point and one triangle per input triangle spanning three distinct clusters, deduplicated, wound against the mean input normal of its three clusters. **Quadric mode was measured to need a compactness stabilizer, not a pure swap**: an initial pure-quadric-error badness (no isotropic term at all) reproducibly left 17 of 150 clusters disconnected on a smooth closed icosahedron — a cluster confined to a common plane can grow arbitrarily thin and snake-like along a low-curvature direction without its quadric error rising at all, so nothing in a pure-quadric objective penalizes shape. The shipped objective **adds** the quadric term to the same isotropic badness (`iso_badness + quadric_error_at_optimal_point`, both already area-weighted squared-distance-like quantities, dimensionally consistent, no new tunable weight), which keeps the compactness pull while still letting the quadric term decide near an actual feature; the fix was sabotage-verified — reverting all three badness sites (`remesh_trial_badness`/`remesh_init_state`/`remesh_commit`) back to pure quadric reproduces the same watertightness failure on demand. **Quadric placement is unaffected by that fix**: the dual's representative point under `Quadric` is always the pure quadric minimizer (`detail::decim_quadric_optimal_point`, falling back to the isotropic centroid when ill-conditioned) — only the clustering *acceptance* test is blended, not the final output geometry, which is what actually pins a crease/corner onto the true surface instead of the isotropic centroid. **Shared-machinery hoist**: `decim_place`'s inline 3x3 cofactor solve + ill-conditioning fallback (previously private to its `Optimal` branch) was extracted into `detail::decim_quadric_optimal_point(q, fallback, out) -> bool`, with `decim_place` refactored to call it with the edge midpoint as fallback — a pure, behaviour-preserving refactor verified by re-running `decimate`'s and `decimate_volume`'s full existing gtest suites unmodified before trusting `remesh`'s own reuse of it with the cluster centroid as fallback instead. **Subdivision matters more than iteration count**: the clustering is a *discrete* CVD approximation whose resolution is items-per-cluster, so `mSubdivide` (`-1` = auto, picking the smallest `refine` pass count reaching `mSubsampleRatio` items/cluster capped at `mMaxSubdivide`; `0` disables) matters more than `mMaxIterations`. **Surface only, triangles out**, mirroring `decimate`'s exact scope: `quad`/rectangular `polygon` triangulated via `convert_cells(Simplexify)`; 3D volume throws naming `extract_surface`; higher-order throws naming `Linearize`; ragged/`line`/`vertex` throw outright. **What does NOT survive**: the output has new points and new connectivity with no correspondence to the input — unlike every other operation here, there is no point or cell map, and `point_data`/`cell_data`/regions are dropped (`warn_regions_dropped`, never silent); `field_data` carries through; compose with `interpolate`/`conservative_interpolate` to transfer a field onto the result. **v10.11.0 closed the curvature-gradation and boundaries/manifoldness roadmap bullets.** Curvature gradation (`mGradation`, the exponent `gamma` in the item weight `area * kappa^gamma`) reuses the same `detail::NodeAdjacency` graph the energy sweep already builds -- no new neighbourhood machinery -- via a fresh local osculating-paraboloid fit (`remesh_vertex_curvature`, new: `grep -rn "curvature" src/cpp/` returned zero matches before this): an area-weighted vertex normal (`remesh_vertex_normals`, the per-vertex twin of the existing `remesh_cluster_normals`) gives a local tangent frame, the 1-ring is least-squares fit to `h = a*u^2 + b*u*w + c*w^2` via an explicit 3x3 Cramer's-rule solve (`remesh_solve_sym3`) with the same `decim_quadric_optimal_point`-style ill-conditioning fallback (near-singular fit -> `kappa = 0`), and `kappa = max(|kappa1|, |kappa2|)` from the eigenvalues of `[[2a, b], [b, 2c]]`. `mGradation == 0.0` (default) means `kappa^0 == 1` identically, so curvature is never even computed and `remesh_item_weights` reproduces plain area weighting byte-for-byte -- every pre-existing test is unaffected; a nonzero value rescales each item's weight by `max(kappa, kappa_floor)^gamma` (`kappa_floor` an internal fraction of the mesh's own max curvature, guarding against an exact-zero weight), the **one** weight already threaded through `sgamma`/`srho`/badness/final placement under both metrics unchanged -- gradation is orthogonal to `Isotropic`/`Quadric`, not a third metric. Boundaries: `mPreserveBoundary` (default **true**, free on every closed-mesh test) detects the open boundary via a local edge-use-count pass (`remesh_boundary_info`: a triangle edge used by exactly one face is a boundary edge -- deliberately **not** hoisted from/into `decimate_common.hpp`'s `decim_build_edges`, whose own header doc argues against reuse outside its two current callers), seeds boundary vertices **before** the interior BFS (`remesh_seed_order`: boundary-vertices-first-then-interior, ascending id within each group) so a cluster's boundary segment stays anchored rather than absorbed piecemeal, and emits one `line` dual cell (`remesh_dual_boundary_edges`, deduplicated by unordered cluster-id pair, the triangle dual's own dedup idiom) for every boundary edge whose endpoints land in different clusters -- the output gaining a **second, optional `line` cell block** alongside `triangle`. Manifoldness now has two distinct, separately-reported causes: `mNumIsolatedClusters` (disconnected clusters, unchanged since v10.10.0) and the new `mNumNonManifoldVertices` (a "bowtie" vertex whose incident dual-triangle fan is not a single loop/chain, detected by `remesh_detect_nonmanifold_vertices` over the triangle-key dedup `remesh_dual_faces` already builds -- hoisted into a shared `remesh_dual_triangle_keys` so both the per-pass repair check and the final emission agree) -- both fold into the **same** repair loop (`unassign_bad_clusters` unions each pass's disconnected and non-manifold findings before regrowing/reminimising), rather than two separate loops. **A clean-room design, not a reproduction of ACVD's own boundary-fixing algorithm**, whose CeCILL-B source this project does not read (the same licence posture as the v10.10.0 core). Two sabotage-verified oracles in `test_remesh.cpp`: a Gaussian-bump fixture (spatially *varying* curvature -- the constant-curvature icosahedron/cube fixtures cannot discriminate a working `kappa^gamma` weighting from a no-op) asserts gradation concentrates more of a fixed cluster budget within a central disk than plain area weighting; an open flat-square-patch fixture asserts the output carries a non-empty `line` block whose total length approximates the true perimeter. **Determinism, and no numpy twin**: seeding RNG-free, the energy sweep serial and fixed-order (an accepted move mutates the accumulators the next comparison reads), setup passes parallel with fixed FP order — byte-identical across backends and thread counts, but **C++-core only**, the same reasoning as `subdivide`/`agglomerate`/`decimate_volume`/`conservative_interpolate`: a near-tie decided differently by an independent implementation diverges into a different clustering, not a last-ulp difference; `_remesh.py` raises `NotImplementedError` by name when `_core` is unavailable, for any input. **No ABI change in v10.10.0** (Tier C additive — wholly new header/`.cpp`, no existing installed header's layout changes; `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_ABI_VERSION` stayed 7). **v10.11.0 bumped `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_ABI_VERSION` 7→8**, by contrast: `RemeshOptions`/`RemeshResult` are the `RefineOptions` precedent exactly (an *existing* options/result struct, passed by const-ref/value through the exported `remesh()`, gaining data members — Tier A layout by `doc/abi.md`'s own criterion, not Tier C, since the header itself already existed) — `RemeshOptions` is now pinned in `tests/cpp/test_abi_layout.cpp` (`MIO_ABI_LAYOUT(meshioplusplus::RemeshOptions, 56, 8)`, from the release that first grew it rather than left unpinned the way `RefineOptions` was for over twenty releases); `RemeshResult` stays unpinned like every other `*Result` struct (it embeds a `Mesh`, whose size is per-backend). **Full binding surface, shipped in one release** (unlike `decimate_volume`'s deferred bindings), **extended in lockstep in v10.11.0** for `mGradation`/`mPreserveBoundary`/`mNumNonManifoldVertices`: C API `mio_remesh(mesh, num_clusters, subdivide, subsample_ratio, max_subdivide, max_iterations, max_repair_passes, metric, gradation, preserve_boundary, &num_clusters_out, &num_iterations, &subdivide_applied, &num_isolated_clusters, &num_non_manifold_vertices)` → a plain `mio_mesh*` with nullable counter out-params (`mio_smooth`'s shape, no opaque result handle, since there are no maps); Fortran `m%remesh(num_clusters, ..., gradation=, preserve_boundary=, num_non_manifold_vertices=)`; Julia `remesh(mesh, num_clusters; subdivide=-1, metric=:isotropic, gradation=0.0, preserve_boundary=true, ...)` → a NamedTuple; R `mio_remesh(mesh, num_clusters, ..., gradation = 0.0, preserve_boundary = TRUE)` → a named list (own dedicated roxygen block, the `mio_hessian` precedent, **with the `NAMESPACE` export entry not forgotten** — a documented past miss for exactly this shape of addition; this round needed no new NAMESPACE entry, since `mio_remesh` was already exported); WASM `remesh(mesh, numClusters, subdivide, subsampleRatio, maxSubdivide, maxIterations, maxRepairPasses, metric, gradation, preserveBoundary)`, reachable generically as a `{op: 'remesh', numClusters: ...}` `convertSurfaceOps`/pipeline step with no extra WASM dispatch code needed (the shared `apply_pipeline_step` mechanism both `apply_one_op` and `run_pipeline`/`_core.run_pipeline_file` already route through -- adding `"Gradation"`/`"PreserveBoundary"` to `pipeline_op_table()`'s `"Remesh"` row was the entire pipeline-side cost); a `remesh` verb in both CLIs (`--num-clusters` required, `--metric isotropic|quadric|anisotropic`, `--gradation`, `--no-preserve-boundary`, `--max-anisotropy`); a `Remesh` step (`{NumClusters, Subdivide, SubsampleRatio, MaxSubdivide, MaxIterations, MaxRepairPasses, Metric, Gradation, PreserveBoundary, MaxAnisotropy}`) in both pipeline engines' op tables, pinned equal by `test_op_table_matches_core`/`_core.pipeline_op_table`; a `remesh` MCP tool closing the parity guard automatically. **v10.12.0 closed roadmap §1's remaining anisotropic-metric bullet, leaving only volumetric CVD/ODT open in that section at the time** — closed in v10.13.0 by `remesh_volume` (below) plus `SmoothMethod::Odt`, a different algorithm than the one named, honestly reported as such; see `doc/roadmap.md`. `RemeshMetric::Anisotropic` cites the same Valette/Chassery/Prost TVCG 2008 paper the quadric metric already cites -- a metric-weighted discrete Voronoi diagram is that paper's whole subject, curvature-tensor metrics its headline anisotropic case, and no ACVD source is read for it any more than for the quadric metric. **The hazard found before writing any new code**: every metric branch in `remesh.cpp` was a binary `if(Metric == Isotropic) {...} else {...}` where `else` implicitly meant `Quadric` (`remesh_trial_badness`/`remesh_init_state`/`remesh_commit`/`remesh_final_positions`/`remesh_minimize`/`remesh_vertex_quadrics`) -- appending a third enumerator there would have made it silently execute quadric behaviour under the new name, plausible output, wrong metric, no diagnostic. Every site was converted to an explicit `switch(Metric)` with **no `default:`** first, as a self-contained, behaviour-preserving step (`RemeshMetric` gained a fixed `: std::uint8_t` underlying type + a width `static_assert`, the `CellType` precedent, so `-Wswitch` catches the next enumerator at compile time rather than a reader having to), verified against the full pre-existing 16-test `Remesh.*` suite unchanged before any new behaviour landed. **Construction reuses the gradation fit rather than duplicating it**: `remesh_vertex_curvature` already built a tangent frame and fit `h = a*u^2 + b*u*w + c*w^2` to get `kappa = max(|kappa1|,|kappa2|)` for `mGradation` and threw away the frame, the signed `kappa1`/`kappa2` and the eigenvectors at the loop brace; it now optionally also emits each vertex's principal-direction eigenvectors (closed-form 2x2 eigensolve of `[[2a,b],[b,2c]]`, sign/tie-convention-free since only the outer products `e⊗e` ever enter the metric and `(-e)⊗(-e) == e⊗e`) mapped into world coordinates. Per vertex: target lengths `~ 1/sqrt(|kappa|)` along each principal direction, their ratio clamped to `mMaxAnisotropy` **before** anything else is computed (the clamp is load-bearing for robustness, not just quality -- see below); the normal eigenvalue is pinned to the **tighter** of the two in-plane eigenvalues, which is what keeps the metric ellipsoid tangent to the true surface instead of letting a cluster drift off it; the whole SPD tensor is then rescaled so `det(M) = 1` -- a pure reshape, never a rescale, which is what keeps `mMaxAnisotropy` orthogonal to both `mNumClusters` (global count) and `mGradation` (density) rather than interacting with either. A perfectly flat vertex yields **exactly** the identity tensor, so `Anisotropic` on an exactly flat mesh is bit-for-bit identical to `Isotropic` (verified, not assumed). **Packs into the existing quadric accumulator with zero new storage**: the anisotropic energy `E = sum(w*(x-v)^T M (x-v))` expands algebraically to `v^T A v + 2b.v + c` with `A=sum(w*M)`, `b=-sum(w*M*x)`, `c=sum(w*x^T M x)` -- *exactly* the same 10-double Garland-Heckbert layout `Quadric` mode already accumulates and the same `detail::decim_quadric_optimal_point` solver already minimizes (confirmed against its `E = x^T A x + 2b.x + c`, `Ax = -b` sign convention before relying on it) -- so the new function `remesh_vertex_metrics` (sibling of `remesh_vertex_quadrics`) differs from it only in *which* 10 doubles a vertex contributes, never in how they are accumulated or read back. **The badness term is used PURE, the opposite of `Quadric`'s additive stabilizer**: `Quadric`'s badness *adds* the quadric error to isotropic compactness because a flat quadric is rank-1 and does not penalize a cluster snaking along its own null direction; a curvature tensor has no null direction (SPD by construction, via the normal-pinning step), so it already penalizes spread in every direction, just anisotropically up to the `mMaxAnisotropy` ratio -- adding the isotropic term on top would double-count that penalty. Placement under `Anisotropic` shares `Quadric`'s exact code path (`case Quadric: case Anisotropic:` in `remesh_final_positions`, the same `decim_quadric_optimal_point` call with the same ill-conditioning fallback to the isotropic centroid). `mMaxAnisotropy` (`kRemeshDefaultMaxAnisotropy`) is **measured, not guessed**: an initial round-number default of 10.0 produced a severe cluster-count shortfall specific to `Anisotropic` mode on a near-umbilic input (an icosahedron/sphere, which carries zero genuine anisotropy signal, only discretization noise) -- whole clusters absorbed by neighbours during energy minimization (confirmed distinct from disconnection: 0 isolated/0 non-manifold at every repair-pass count, ruling out "needs more repair"), and a direct sweep of the ratio (1/2/4/10) showed a monotonic 0/0/1/18-cluster shortfall; the shipped default is **4.0**, the measured sweet spot, with the full sweep recorded in the header doc comment; setting `mMaxAnisotropy` under a non-`Anisotropic` metric is a **named validation error**, the `write_options.hpp`/selective-`refine` mutually-exclusive-selector posture, never silently ignored. **C ABI growth path introduced for the first time on this operation**: `mio_remesh`'s flat signature had already grown once this section's history (`gradation`/`preserve_boundary` in v10.11.0), so a third parameter would have broken every C/Fortran/Julia/R caller a second time -- `mio_remesh_ex(mesh, const mio_remesh_opts*, mio_remesh_report*)` transcribes the `mio_refine_ex`/`mio_refine_opts` precedent exactly: an append-only `reserved`-tailed 120-byte options struct, `mio_remesh_opts_init` for defaults, a symmetric 72-byte `mio_remesh_report`, both sizes `static_assert`ed in `c_api.cpp`; plain `mio_remesh` is unchanged and delegates to `mio_remesh_ex` with `max_anisotropy` left at `kRemeshDefaultMaxAnisotropy`. Fortran/Julia mirror `mio_remesh_opts_t`/`_CRemeshOpts` field-for-field (the metric string riding the `target`-buffer/`GC.@preserve` NUL-terminated-`c_loc` idiom struct fields need, distinct from the simpler direct-string-argument idiom plain subroutine parameters get) with a runtime layout guard each (`check_..._layout()`/`_check_abi_layout()`), the same discipline `mio_refine_opts`' mirrors already established; R builds a real `mio_remesh_opts` directly from the installed C header, needing no manual mirror at all. **Tier A, `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_ABI_VERSION` 8→9**: `RemeshOptions` gained `mMaxAnisotropy` (a data member on an *existing* struct -- appending `RemeshMetric::Anisotropic` alone would have been Tier C, but the option field is not), `MIO_ABI_LAYOUT(meshioplusplus::RemeshOptions, 64, 8)` re-pinned at the new measured size. Six new `Remesh.*` gtests (cylinder-elongation, flat-surface-identity, watertight/deterministic/valid, `mMaxAnisotropy` validation), every new oracle sabotage-verified before being trusted -- including a weak-oracle self-correction: the flat-surface-identity test was found, on reflection, to also pass under several plausible bugs (dropping the entire normal-pinning term degenerates to a rank-deficient matrix whose ill-conditioning fallback coincidentally also gives z=0 on that symmetric fixture), so it was kept as a documented-weak regression guard alongside a genuinely discriminating replacement (a Gaussian-bump fixture measuring vertical deviation from the true surface, confirmed strong by sabotage: removing the normal-pinning term produces a ~30000x bound violation).
  - **Partitioning** (`operations/partition.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_partition.py`, docs [`doc/partition.md`](doc/partition.md)): `partition(mesh, PartitionOptions{nparts, method, imbalance, mode, seed, record_ids, ghost_layers, weights_key})` → `PartitionResult{vector<PartitionPiece{mPartId, mMesh, mPointMap, mCellMaps}>}` and `partition_labels(...)` → block-aligned Int64 arrays (`partition:part` contract) — decompose into **exactly nparts balanced pieces**, the count-driven complement to `split`. Methods (`PartitionMethod{SFC, KaHIP, Auto}`, `partition_method_from_name`): **SFC** (always available) = Hilbert keys of the cell centroids via `detail/space_filling.hpp` + reorder's quantization idiom, serial `stable_sort` (index tie-break), then a serial cut — unweighted `part = (rank*nparts)/n` (sizes ≤1 apart, the test pin), weighted (`mWeightsKey` = scalar numeric cell_data) `part = min(nparts-1, floor((S_r + w_r/2)*nparts/W))` on the prefix sum (monotone → contiguous; a part can be empty when one weight exceeds W/nparts) — **byte-identical across backends, thread counts, and the C++/numpy-fallback boundary** (`test_cpp_matches_python` pins identical labels; the numpy Hilbert is a lane-exact vectorized Skilling port, and its centroid/prefix sums replicate C++'s sequential accumulation order — never `np.mean`/`np.cumsum`, whose pairwise summation would drift a ulp and flip a quantization bin). **KaHIP** (optional, `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_HAS_KAHIP`; ported from the Kratos KaHIPApplication, KratosMultiphysics/Kratos#14453) = shared-facet dual graph (cells=vertices; facet-key idiom re-implemented locally with `partition_` prefixes — surface.cpp's anon-namespace machinery stays untouched; faces via `cell_faces.hpp` (3D) / edges via `cell_edges.hpp` (2D) at the mesh's max dimension, everything else — ragged/polyhedron/lower-dim — becomes isolated vertices) → CSR (each undirected edge twice, deduped, sorted) → `kaffpa` with `imbalance` (validated in (0,1)), `mode` (`Fast/Eco/Strong`, default **Eco** — the Kratos benchmarks showed eco/strong carry the edge-cut wins), fixed `seed`; the CSR is built in the **installed lib's** index width via runtime `kahip_sizeof_idx()` (int64 buffers `reinterpret_cast` for a `-D64BITMODE=On` lib; never trust the compile-time `kahip_idx` typedef), with named overflow errors; weights scale to int vwgt (sum ≈ 2^30, floor 1); n/nparts/part stay `int`. The compiled-out `partition_kahip_parts` **always exists and throws naming `-DMESHIOPLUSPLUS_WITH_KAHIP=ON`** (vtk_codec pattern — never a silent SFC downgrade); `Auto` = KaHIP if built else SFC (`partition_has_kahip()`, `_core.__has_kahip__`). Pieces via `detail::build_cell_subset(..., drop_empty_blocks=false)` — **deliberately unlike split**: every piece keeps the input block structure 1:1 so `mCellMaps` index by input block and concatenation reproduces the mesh (partition of unity, `ghost_layers=0`); `record_ids` attaches `partition:original_point_id`/`_cell_id`; **`mGhostLayers > 0`** (v9.0.0, was a throwing stub) grows each piece by that many **shared-node** BFS layers of other parts' cells — the halo an MPI decomposition needs — tagged Int64 `partition:ghost` (0 = owned, L = reached at layer L), emitted for *every* block so the array stays block-aligned. Shared-node rather than shared-facet adjacency is the conservative choice (it is what a node-based assembly needs, and the same neighbour definition the KaHIP dual graph falls back on). The pieces then **overlap**, so partition-of-unity holds only at 0; a `stamp[cell] == part` array keeps the BFS one-visit-per-cell-per-part instead of needing a per-part bitmap. **`partition_labels` REJECTS a nonzero value** rather than ignoring it — a flat per-cell label array is the *ownership* map and a cell can be a ghost of several parts at once — and the numpy fallback raises `NotImplementedError` rather than silently returning unghosted pieces. KaHIP tests assert balance/coverage/fixed-seed only, never exact labels (assignment varies by KaHIP version). The Python shim adds set remapping and a second quality route: with a KaHIP-less `_core` plus the MIT `kahip` PyPI wheel (`meshioplusplus[kahip]` extra), `method="kahip"`/`"auto"` bypasses `_core` and runs a numpy dual graph (reusing `_skin._CELL_FACES`/`_surface._CELL_EDGES`) through `kahip.kaffpa` — pip-only users get the quality path. C API opaque `mio_partition_result` (`_num_pieces`/`_part_id`/`_mesh`/`_take_mesh`/zero-copy per-piece `_point_map`/`_num_cell_maps`/`_cell_map`/`_free`, modelled on `mio_refine_result`) + `mio_partition_labels` (caller buffer, block-major flat, size validated). Fortran `m%partition(nparts [, method, imbalance, mode, seed, record_ids, ghost_layers, weights_key])` → allocatable mesh array + `m%partition_labels(...)` (flat Int64; values are part ids, **no** 1-based shift). WASM `partition`/`partitionLabels` (SFC only — KaHIP never built for wasm; `method:"kahip"` throws by name). CLI verb `partition IN 'OUT_{part}.vtu' --nparts N [--method auto|sfc|kahip --imbalance --mode fast|eco|strong --seed --weights NAME --ghost-layers --record-ids --labels-only]` in both CLIs (`{part}` expansion via a partition-local helper — split's `replace_key` untouched; `--labels-only` writes one file with `partition:part` attached).
  - **Smoothing** (`operations/smooth.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_smooth.py`, docs [`doc/smooth.md`](doc/smooth.md)): `smooth(mesh, SmoothOptions{method, iterations, lambda, mu, fix_boundary, preserve_features, feature_angle, guard_inversion, frozen, move_tolerance})` → `SmoothResult{mMesh, mNumNodesMoved, mMaxDisplacement, mNumSkippedInversion}` — relax point coordinates toward their edge-neighbour centroids, the quality-*improving* companion to `compute_quality`, which measures exactly what this changes. A **pure coordinate move**: connectivity, `cell_data`, `field_data` and `point_data` *values* pass through unchanged, point/cell counts are unchanged, and the points array keeps its input dtype (iteration runs in `double` with a single cast on write-back, so a Float32 mesh does not accumulate one rounding per pass). Both operators are driven by the same displacement `L(i) = mean(x[j] : j adjacent to i) - x[i]`: **Laplacian** (`x <- x + lambda*L(x)`) is strong per pass but unconditionally shrinking, **Taubin** (the default) follows each `+lambda` pass with a larger-magnitude `-mu` pass whose pass band leaves features alone (on a jittered 8x8 quad grid over 40 iterations Laplacian contracts the bbox 57%, Taubin 3.6%). `mLambda` uses a **negative sentinel meaning "this method's own default"** — 0.5 Laplacian, 0.33 Taubin — because the sensible value genuinely differs per method and two separate fields would let a caller set the one the chosen method ignores; Python spells the parameter `lambda_` (trailing underscore, `lambda` being a keyword). The neighbour graph is `node_adjacency.hpp`'s **Edge** kind, *not* reorder's Clique: under the clique a hexahedron corner is pulled by its three face diagonals and its body diagonal as hard as by its real edge neighbours, bevelling cubes toward spheres, whereas the edge graph leaves a structured hex block a fixed point. **Pinned by default**: boundary nodes (`mFixBoundary`, classic once-used-facet test — a face used by exactly one 3D cell, or in a pure surface mesh an edge used by exactly one 2D cell), feature nodes where two incident boundary facet **normals** differ by more than `mFeatureAngleDeg` (default 30, the `vtkFeatureEdges` convention, which is what keeps a cube's edges sharp), the caller's `mFrozen` mask, and the nodes of blocks whose edge topology is unknown (the higher-order family, VTK-Lagrange, `custom`) — an unknown neighbourhood is an undefined target, so the node holds still rather than being guessed at. The **inversion guard** (default on) commits a candidate position only if no incident cell's signed measure changes sign, counting each `(node, pass)` rejection; it is "do no harm", not "preserve the sign", so a cell that arrives already inverted imposes no constraint and a tangle can still be repaired. Measures: face-fan volume (tetra/hexahedron/wedge/pyramid), signed shoelace area (2D cells in a 2D mesh), and normal-flip (2D cells in a 3D mesh) — that last mode is smooth's own, with **no counterpart in `compute_quality`**, which correctly declines to call a triangle floating in 3D "inverted" for want of a global orientation convention; a cell with no signed measure at all (`line`, polyhedron, `custom`) is **skipped, not pinned**, since a missing safety check is not a missing target and pinning there would freeze an entire beam-element curve. Determinism: every pass is **Jacobi** (all new positions computed from the previous pass and committed together, so no node observes a half-updated neighbour), neighbour sums run in ascending node id over the sorted adjacency rows, the boundary set comes from a **serial** dedup over a `parallel_for`-filled disjoint-slot buffer (surface.cpp's phase-split idiom) and the counters fold serially out of per-node slots — byte-identical across the three mesh backends and across 1/2/8 threads on a 52k-node mesh. Throws `std::invalid_argument` on a mis-sized `mFrozen`, `mLambda` outside (0,1), or a Taubin `mMu` not satisfying `mMu < -mLambda < 0` (which would amplify rather than filter); `mMoveTolerance` is **relative to the bbox diagonal** so `mNumNodesMoved` is scale-invariant. The numpy fallback (`_smooth_py`) implements both operators but **deliberately not the guard** — it is a discrete branch on a sign, and near-degenerate cells could land the two implementations on opposite sides and then diverge macroscopically — so it raises `NotImplementedError` when one is requested, and the shim re-raises `ValueError`/`TypeError` from `_core` rather than letting a genuine user error fall through to numpy. The Python shim also resolves `frozen` given as a `point_sets` **name** and copies `point_sets`/`cell_sets` through untouched (nothing is renumbered, so unlike refine/crop/split there is nothing to remap). C API `mio_smooth(mesh, method, iterations, lambda, mu, fix_boundary, preserve_features, feature_angle, guard_inversion, &nodes_moved, &max_displacement, &skipped_inversion)` → a plain `mio_mesh*` with nullable counter out-params (mio_clean's shape, **not** an opaque result handle — there are no index maps to hand back); `frozen` is **not** exposed on the flat ABI, a documented gap like the sets gaps in diff/merge/split. Fortran `m%smooth(method, iterations [, lambda, mu, fix_boundary, preserve_features, feature_angle, guard_inversion, nodes_moved, max_displacement, skipped_inversion])`. WASM `smooth(mesh, method, iterations, lambda, mu, fixBoundary, preserveFeatures, featureAngle, guardInversion)` → `{mesh, numNodesMoved, maxDisplacement, numSkippedInversion}`. CLI verb `smooth IN OUT [--method taubin|laplacian|odt --iterations N --lambda L --mu=M --no-fix-boundary --no-preserve-features --feature-angle A --no-guard-inversion --quiet]` in both CLIs (a negative `--mu` needs the `--mu=` form). **`SmoothMethod::Odt` (v10.13.0, closing roadmap §1's "ODT" half honestly as ODT *smoothing* on FIXED connectivity, not ODT *remeshing* — see `remesh_volume` above for the operation that generates a fresh tet mesh)**: each free INTERIOR tet vertex moves to the closed-form volume-weighted average of its incident tets' circumcenters (`smooth_tet_circumcenter`, an independent Cramer's-rule solve via `detail::det3` — NOT transcribed from `quality.cpp`'s unrelated private `quality_solve3`), reusing the existing `SmoothCellTable`/`SmoothCsr` node→incident-tet lookup the guard already builds rather than a new structure. **Tet-only**: `smooth_check_odt_blocks` (the `dv_check_blocks` shape/messages from `decimate_volume.cpp`) raises by name on any non-`tetra` block — a new KIND of per-method scope restriction inside one enum, distinct from the mesh-wide scope every other `smooth` method has. **Step 0 was a separate, behaviour-preserving switch-safety refactor landed and verified FIRST**: `smooth.cpp` used to collapse the method to a bool at six sites (`p.mTaubin = rOptions.mMethod == SmoothMethod::Taubin`, `p.mLambda = p.mTaubin ? 0.33 : 0.5`, `p.mNumPasses = p.mTaubin ? iterations*2 : iterations`, ...) where "not Taubin" implicitly meant Laplacian — appending `Odt` without touching these would have made it silently run Laplacian, the exact hazard `RemeshMetric::Anisotropic` found and fixed last cycle; fixed the same way, an explicit `switch(rOptions.mMethod)` with NO `default:` (`SmoothMethod` gained a fixed `: std::uint8_t` underlying type + a width `static_assert`, the `CellType`/`RemeshMetric` precedent, mirrored in `test_abi_layout.cpp` for discoverability), landed and the full pre-existing `Smooth.*` suite re-run unchanged BEFORE any ODT behaviour was added. **`mLambda`'s existing negative-sentinel-means-"this method's own default" design is what makes ODT need NO new `SmoothOptions` field** (the header's own doc comment already justified the sentinel as total for exactly this reason) — ODT's own default is `0.9`, a near-full step toward the circumcenter average, damped rather than exactly `1.0` for the same reason the other two methods aren't. **`mMu` is silently ignored by ODT, matching Laplacian's own silent ignoring of it — deliberately NOT a named-error option the way `RemeshOptions::mMaxAnisotropy` is under the wrong metric**: making only the newest method strict while its sibling stays lenient would be a worse inconsistency than either rule applied uniformly, and changing Laplacian's existing behaviour would be breaking. Boundary pinning (`mFixBoundary`) and the inversion guard apply to ODT unchanged. **The numpy fallback (`_smooth_py`) raises `NotImplementedError` for `method="odt"` UNCONDITIONALLY, not merely under `guard_inversion=True`** — checked before the `_METHODS`/`guard_inversion` checks, inheriting the SAME grounds the existing inversion-guard refusal already uses (an unguarded ODT step can invert a tet near a concave boundary, so the method is only meaningful guarded, and duplicating that discrete branch risks the two implementations diverging macroscopically near a degenerate cell). **`MESHIOPLUSPLUS_ABI_VERSION` 9→10, but NOT because `SmoothOptions` gained a field (it didn't)**: giving `SmoothMethod` an explicit `: std::uint8_t` underlying type for the FIRST TIME is itself Tier A under `doc/abi.md`'s own rule ("changing an enum's underlying type") — a scoped enum with no explicit underlying type defaults to `int` per the standard, so this is a genuine 4-byte→1-byte narrowing of `SmoothMethod` alone, independent of whether `sizeof(SmoothOptions)` happens to be unaffected by padding (it is pinned at 80 bytes in `test_abi_layout.cpp` for the first time as a direct result — the `RefineOptions`/`RemeshOptions` "pin from the release that first touches it" lesson, applied to a change easy to mistake for the Tier C "appended enumerator" case when the ACTUAL Tier A act is the underlying-type introduction the appendage rode in on). `mio_smooth`'s `method` C string is passed straight through to `smooth_method_from_name`, so `"odt"` needed ZERO C API, Fortran, Julia, R or WASM code changes beyond the string itself; the ONE genuinely new binding-surface cost was documentation (this entry, `doc/smooth.md#odt-smoothing`, both CLIs' `--method` choices/help text) plus tests at every layer (C++ gtest, Python `_core`-gated, C API, Fortran, Julia, R, WASM smoke). Pipeline step `Smooth`'s existing `"Method"` key needed no change either, for the identical reason.
  - **Interpolation / field transfer** (`operations/interpolate.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_interpolate.py`, docs [`doc/interpolate.md`](doc/interpolate.md)): `interpolate(source, target, InterpolateOptions{mMethod, mArrays, mExtrapolate, mDefaultValue, mOnConflict})` → a plain new `Mesh` (no maps/counters — transform's shape, not an opaque handle) that is a **copy of the target** (geometry, connectivity, own data preserved exactly, via `detail::clone_mesh`) with the requested SOURCE arrays sampled onto it — the repo's first cross-mesh data-transfer op (diff compares, merge concatenates; neither resamples). Source point_data is sampled at target POINTS, source cell_data at target CELL CENTROIDS and **always by nearest source-cell centroid regardless of method** (no meaningful barycentric interpolant of a piecewise-constant field); cross-location transfer is out of scope — compose with `data to-cell`/`to-point`. `mArrays` empty = all source point_data (sorted names); cell_data only when named; a name in neither location throws listing what exists. Methods (`InterpolateMethod{Nearest, Barycentric}`, `interpolate_method_from_name`): **Nearest** = nearest source point via the shared `detail/spatial_hash.hpp` bucket grid with an expanding Chebyshev-shell search — stop once `((r-1)*cell)² > best_d2`, shells clamped to the occupied-key bbox so far-outside queries stay O(bbox surface) — tie-break smallest d² then lowest source index; values are **raw byte row copies** (dtype-preserving, int64 > 2^53 safe). **Barycentric** = `convert_cells(Simplexify)` on the source first (points untouched for linear sources; point_data rides through the higher-order linearization's row subset, so the op samples the **simplexified mesh's own arrays**), then only the max-topological-dimension simplices (tetra if any, else triangle — evaluated **in the xy-plane**, a documented planar assumption; curved 3D surfaces should use Nearest) are `InsertBox`ed into a grid by quantized bbox; containment = single-cell `Find` (floor quantization is monotone, so a containing simplex's bbox always covers the query's cell) then **first containing simplex in ascending block-then-cell index**, weights = sub-simplex volume ratios via `detail/geometry.hpp`'s `triple_product` with tolerance `>= -1e-12` (dimensionless ⇒ scale-invariant); output always Float64, accumulated corner-by-corner in connectivity order. Outside the domain → `mDefaultValue` (one `log::warn` with the count) unless `mExtrapolate` → nearest-point fallback (from the simplexified mesh). `mOnConflict` `Error` (default, throws) / `Overwrite` / `Suffix` (fixed `name + "_interp"`, throws if that is taken too). Grid cell size is derived **without cbrt** (smallest integer R with R³ ≥ n via an integer loop, then one division of the largest bbox extent) so the numpy fallback reproduces it bit-for-bit; centroids duplicate `partition_centroids`' exact accumulation as `interp_centroids` (anon-namespace helpers can't be shared — cross-referenced comments; the numpy side imports `_partition._centroids`, already the lane-exact twin). Output is **byte-identical across the three backends, thread counts, and the C++/numpy boundary** (`tests/python/test_interpolate.py::test_cpp_matches_python`, partition's pattern); the numpy fallback implements both methods fully and raises `NotImplementedError` only where `_partition._centroids` does (ragged/polyhedron cell_data centroids). Target `point_sets`/`cell_sets` pass through verbatim in the Python shim (nothing renumbered); source sets are NOT transferred; `mesh.info`/`gmsh_periodic` not carried. C API `mio_interpolate(source, target, method, arrays /*char** + count; NULL or <= 0 = all point_data*/, arrays_count, extrapolate, default_value, on_conflict)` → plain `mio_mesh*`. Fortran module-level `mio_interpolate(source, target [, method, arrays, extrapolate, default_value, on_conflict, stat, errmsg])` → `type(mio_mesh)` (two-mesh input like `mio_merge`). WASM `interpolate(source, target, method, arrays, extrapolate, defaultValue, onConflict)`. CLI verb `interpolate SOURCE TARGET OUT [--method nearest|barycentric --arrays a,b --extrapolate --default-value=V --on-conflict error|overwrite|suffix --quiet]` in both CLIs (a negative `--default-value` needs the `=` form).
  - **Conservative (mass-preserving) interpolation** (`operations/conservative_interpolate.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_conservative_interpolate.py`, docs [`doc/conservative_interpolate.md`](doc/conservative_interpolate.md), closing roadmap §1's largest item): `conservative_interpolate(source, target, ConservativeInterpolateOptions{mArrays, mDefaultValue, mOnConflict})` → a plain new `Mesh` (transform's shape, no maps/counters — deliberately, since verifying conservation is the separate, not-yet-built "field integration" roadmap item's job, not this operation's) that is a **copy of the target** with the requested SOURCE arrays remapped onto it so that `sum(target value * target measure)` equals `sum(source value * source measure)` over the shared region — the property `interpolate`'s `Barycentric` mode does not have, hence a **separate sibling operation** (the `decimate`/`decimate_volume` split precedent) rather than a third `InterpolateMethod`. A **fresh** `ConservativeInterpolateConflict` enum (not a reuse of `InterpolateConflict`, keeping the two operations' headers independent). Unlike `interpolate`, empty `mArrays` means every source `point_data` **AND** `cell_data` array (one algorithm regardless of location). **Algorithm**: both meshes are `convert_cells(Simplexify, mRecordParentIds=true)`d first — the same call `interpolate`'s Barycentric mode already makes — which is what lets this operation accept **ragged and polyhedron blocks for free** (Simplexify already fans them into simplices via a shipped, tested path); a pre-check on max topological dimension (`cb.IsPolyhedron() ? 3 : cell_type_dimension(cell_type_from_name(cb.Type()))`, `surface_effective_dim`'s exact idiom) throws when source and target disagree. Each simplex's ORIGINAL global parent-cell index is `detail::block_bases(original mesh)[block] + convert:parent_cell[block][local]`. Broad phase reuses `detail/spatial_hash.hpp`'s `SpatialGrid`/`InsertBox` (interpolate's own barycentric-candidate pattern) plus a new `ForEachInBox` (the read-side twin of `InsertBox`, same nested-loop shape) — since both the insert and query sides can span multiple grid cells, candidates are **sorted-and-deduplicated** before accumulation or overlap measure silently double-counts. Narrow phase is an **exact geometric clip**, computed once per call and reused across every requested array: 2D `cons_clip_triangle_triangle_area` is a Sutherland-Hodgman convex polygon clip (source triangle CCW-ensured, clip against its 3 edges, measure via `detail::polygon_area`, the xy-plane assumption `interpolate`'s own barycentric path already documents); 3D `cons_clip_tetra_tetra_volume` is a **bounded** (never general-polyhedron) convex-polytope clip since both operands are always tetrahedra — the source tet's 4 outward-wound faces (`cell_faces.hpp`'s table) clipped against the target tet's 4 half-spaces in turn via a shared `cons_clip_ring` (Sutherland-Hodgman generalized to 3D, tracking newly-created chord points per clip), each cut capped by dedup'ing the chord (tolerance scaled to the source tet's own edge length), angle-sorting it around the cutting plane's own right-handed 2D basis (explicit ascending-build-index tie-break on an exact angle tie, never insertion order) and fan-triangulating; **recentred on the source tet's own corner average before any arithmetic** (`poly_measure`'s numerical-stability lesson); volume via the closed-triangulated-surface divergence theorem `V = (1/6) sum(v0.(v1 x v2))`. Accumulation is the `data_average.cpp` two-phase idiom: `parallel_for` per target simplex builds a `(source simplex id, measure)` list once (array-independent, reused for every requested array), then a **serial**, ascending-target-simplex-index scatter folds it into per-ORIGINAL-target-cell `(numerator, denominator)` accumulators — floating-point addition is not associative, so this many-to-few fold cannot be parallelised without becoming thread-count-dependent. A target cell's `covered_fraction = denominator / own_measure < 1e-9` gets `mDefaultValue` (own_measure from the same per-simplex measure, no extra pass) and counts toward one aggregated `log::warn` per call. **`point_data` transfers by composition, not a separate algorithm**: `point_data_to_cell_data` (suffix `__cons_interp_proxy__`, `overwrite=true`, batched over every requested point-data name in one call) lumps the source array onto a cell proxy sharing `rSource`'s exact geometry (so the SAME overlap structure and global-parent resolution apply unchanged), the direct engine remaps that proxy, then `cell_data_to_point_data(weight=Measure)` on a scratch target clone distributes it back — conservation is **exact only for the middle step**, the two lumping steps sandwiching it are each already-documented approximations of their own, so the overall path is a layered approximation, not exact nodal/FEM conservation; no dual-cell/control-volume machinery exists. Deliberately **no `mExtrapolate`** (unlike `interpolate`): a silent nearest-cell fallback would break the exact conservation guarantee for precisely the cells a caller is most likely to reach for it. Output arrays are always Float64. **C++-core only, with no numpy fallback at all** — the `subdivide`/`agglomerate`/`decimate_volume` precedent: the 3D clip's discrete branches (half-space classification, chord dedup, angle-sort) could disagree with a second implementation near a degenerate/tangent overlap; `_conservative_interpolate.py` raises `NotImplementedError` naming the reason for any input. No ABI change (Tier C additive — a wholly new header/`.cpp`/C entry point touching no existing installed header). C API `mio_conservative_interpolate(source, target, arrays /*char** + count; NULL or <= 0 = all point_data AND cell_data*/, arrays_count, default_value, on_conflict)` → plain `mio_mesh*`. Fortran module-level `mio_conservative_interpolate(source, target [, arrays, default_value, on_conflict, stat, errmsg])` → `type(mio_mesh)`. Julia `conservative_interpolate(source, target; arrays, default_value, on_conflict)`. R `mio_conservative_interpolate(source, target, arrays, default_value, on_conflict)`. WASM `conservativeInterpolate(source, target, arrays, defaultValue, onConflict)`. Excluded from the settings pipeline like `Interpolate`/`UndoGreen` (`pipe_excluded_hint`'s `"ConservativeInterpolate"` entry, mirrored in `_pipeline.py`'s `_EXCLUDED_OPS`), since it needs a second (source) mesh. An MCP tool (`conservative_interpolate`, closing the parity guard automatically). CLI verb `conservative-interpolate SOURCE TARGET OUT [--arrays a,b --default-value=V --on-conflict error|overwrite|suffix --quiet]` in both CLIs.
  - **Slicing / cross-sections** (`operations/slice.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_slice.py`, docs [`doc/slice.md`](doc/slice.md)): `slice(mesh, SliceOptions{mOrigin[3], mNormal[3], mRecordParentIds})` → a plain new `Mesh` (transform's shape, no maps/handle) that is the planar cross-section — the actual **intersection** of the mesh with a plane, one topological dimension below the cut cells (3D volume → `triangle`/`quad` surface; 2D surface → `line` mesh). **Distinct from `crop` (plane mode)**, which keeps whole cells on one side; slice computes the intersection and lowers the dimension. **Robust construction (marching tetrahedra):** the input is `convert_cells(Simplexify)`ed first (recordParentIds on) so every 3D cell is a tetra / 2D cell a triangle — each cell's cross-section is then a convex, unambiguously-ordered primitive (no angular sort, no non-convex-hex ambiguity); a hex/wedge section is thus the union of its simplices' sections (correctly located, just triangulated). Signed distance `d_i = dot(x_i − origin, normal)`; an edge crosses iff its endpoints straddle zero, crossing param `t = d_lo/(d_lo − d_hi)` from the **sorted** endpoint pair, so both simplices agree bit-for-bit; `point_data` interpolated at the same `t` (Float64 out). **Degeneracy rule (uniform, documented, tested):** `d_i >= 0` is the positive side (a node on the plane is "above"), making the 4-bit (tetra) / 3-bit (triangle) sign mask **total**, so a plane grazing a shared face is emitted by exactly one incident simplex — **no double-emit** (`test_slice.py::test_grazing…`); any primitive whose crossings collapse (area/length < a bbox-relative tol) is dropped. **Winding:** a fixed marching case table (keyed by the sign mask) gives each section polygon's crossing-edge ring, then a runtime Newell-normal flip orients every face toward the `+normal` side (globally consistent, pinned by `test_slice.cpp`'s Newell invariant). **Watertight:** cut points are shared *by topology* — keyed by the sorted simplexified-node-id edge pair and deduped in a **serial** first-seen sweep over a fixed (block, cell, ring-edge) traversal (surface.cpp/refine.cpp's phase-split idiom; the signed-distance and coordinate/point_data passes are `parallel_for`/`parallel_for_bw`), so shared edges yield one node (grazing configs may leave coincident-but-distinct nodes — the documented exception). Section points are all new: each section cell inherits its parent's `cell_data` (replicated like simplexify, `convert:parent_cell` excluded); `mRecordParentIds` attaches Int64 `slice:parent_cell` (global block-major input cell, composed through `convert:parent_cell` + original block bases). `point_sets`/`cell_sets`, `mesh.info`, `gmsh_periodic` **not** carried. Output is **byte-identical across the three backends, thread counts, and the C++/numpy boundary** (`tests/python/test_slice.py::test_cpp_matches_python`; the numpy `_slice_py` reuses `_convert_cells._simplexify_py` and replicates the traversal/lerp/Newell/tol arithmetic). C API `mio_slice(mesh, origin[3], normal[3], record_parent_ids)` → `mio_mesh*`. Fortran type-bound `m%slice(origin, normal [, record_parent_ids])`. WASM `slice(mesh, origin, normal, recordParentIds)`. CLI verb `slice IN OUT --origin x,y,z --normal x,y,z [--record-parent-ids]` in both CLIs (negatives need the `--normal=`/`--origin=` form). The **browser viewer's** section feature routes through this (see the viewer entry). `slice` shadows the Python builtin **as a module attribute only** (`meshioplusplus.slice`); the builtin is never rebound inside `_slice.py`.
  - **Volumetric remeshing (isosurface stuffing)** (`operations/remesh_volume.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_remesh_volume.py`, docs [`doc/remesh_volume.md`](doc/remesh_volume.md), v10.13.0, closing roadmap §1's last bullet — by a different algorithm than the one named, see below): `remesh_volume(mesh, RemeshVolumeOptions{mResolution, mCellSize, mBounds, mPadding, mPaddingRelative, mMaxCells, mMaxTets, mWarpFraction, mDistance})` → `RemeshVolumeResult{mMesh, mQuality, mNumTets, mNumVerticesWarped, mNumTetsRejected, mNumNonManifoldEdges}` — retetrahedralize a volume mesh (or a closed surface) at a caller-chosen resolution by isosurface stuffing: a body-centered cubic (BCC) lattice, warped onto the surface, then cut. `remesh`'s volumetric sibling — the same relationship `decimate_volume` has to `decimate` — generating an entirely fresh mesh rather than working on the input's own tetrahedra, since nothing else in this repo can *raise* a tet mesh's quality at a chosen resolution (`refine` subdivides the input's own possibly-bad cells, `decimate_volume` can only remove elements). **Why a lattice, not literal Delaunay/CVD**: the roadmap bullet names optimal Delaunay triangulation / CVD in 3D, which needs robust orientation/in-sphere predicates — exactly where this project's dependency-free posture stops paying (Geogram is a large new dependency; TetGen is AGPL-3.0, CGAL's `Mesh_3` is GPL). Isosurface stuffing needs no predicate at all, only the signed-distance field this repo already had (`sample_distance`, `operations/sdf.hpp`), and gives every uncut lattice tet a dihedral angle from a small FIXED set rather than an unbounded one — closing the bullet's *capability* honestly, not its literal method. **Attribution**: implemented from the PUBLISHED DESCRIPTION only of Labelle & Shewchuk, "Isosurface Stuffing" (SIGGRAPH 2007) — neither the paper's own reference implementation (Stellar, non-commercial) nor TetGen (AGPL-3.0, also by Shewchuk) is read or vendored; see `CITATION.cff`. **The algorithm**: (1) a BCC lattice — two interleaved simple cubic lattices, the second offset half a cell on every axis — sized with `SdfOptions`' own `mResolution`/`mCellSize`/`mBounds`/`mPadding`/`mPaddingRelative` vocabulary verbatim (this operation is a lattice generator exactly as `compute_sdf` is); its natural 12-tet-per-cell tetrahedralization (`kBccLocalTets`, each cell's body-center connected across one fixed diagonal of each of the cell's 6 faces) gives every uncut tet a dihedral angle from **{45°, 60°, 90°, 120°}** — this project's OWN derivation, algebraically pinned (tiling with no gap/overlap, orientation, volume-sum) rather than transcribed from the paper's own {60°,90°,120°} construction, and honestly reported as a deliberately simpler alternative, not a reproduction. (2) every lattice vertex is classified inside/outside by `sample_distance`'s sign. (3) a vertex within `mWarpFraction * h` of the surface (`h` the cell size) is warped onto its nearest surface point — load-bearing, not an optimization: `mWarpFraction = 0` still yields a watertight, correctly-oriented cut, but with arbitrarily bad boundary dihedral angles, plain marching tetrahedra's usual failure mode. (4) a small sign-mask case table (`rvol_cut_tet`, `detail/marching.hpp`'s sibling for volume rather than surface output) cuts straddling tets, reusing a warped endpoint's position for a crossing edge when available. **Determinism**: phase-split, `refine.cpp`/`surface.cpp`'s idiom — parallel classify/warp into disjoint per-vertex slots, then a SERIAL ascending-root-tet cut loop with per-root-tet scratch validation (`rvol_tet_ok`, a signed-volume sign check) so degenerate sub-tets are rejected (counted in `mNumTetsRejected`) without ever validating a not-yet-created point. **The cut/dedup topology (case counts 1/2/3, the "hub"/distinguished-vertex choice in each) is derived and verified independently in Python before being written into `remesh_volume.cpp`** — three real bugs were caught this way during development (an invalid 2-inside decomposition whose sub-tets shared one face across all three, passing a volume-sum check by structural coincidence; a local-array-index-based hub choice for the 2-inside case that is ambiguous across neighbouring tets sharing the same global vertices, since each root tet's `kBccLocalTets` ordering is independent; and the identical bug in the 3-inside case, both fixed by sorting the relevant vertices by GLOBAL id ascending before applying the canonical "staircase fan" prism-split formula `T1=(P0,P1,P2,Q2), T2=(P0,P1,Q2,Q1), T3=(P0,Q1,Q2,Q0)`) — `push_oriented` (compute the sign via `rvol_tet_ok`, swap the tet's OWN last two corners if negative) then fixes winding without risking cross-tet consistency, since it never changes which vertices/faces a sub-tet uses. **A measured, honestly-reported limitation, not a hidden defect**: reusing a warped vertex's position for every cut edge it touches — the mechanism that carries warping's quality benefit into the cut region — can leave a SMALL number of the output's own boundary edges non-manifold, on some inputs, at `mWarpFraction > 0` only; reported as `RemeshVolumeResult::mNumNonManifoldEdges` (computed internally via `extract_surface` + `surface_watertight_check` on the OUTPUT mesh) rather than hidden, always exactly 0 at `mWarpFraction = 0` (proven, not merely observed — a plain unwarped cut has no such reuse), and small relative to the output's own boundary edge count on every fixture tried (the measured sweep is in `doc/remesh_volume.md`); orientation (`compute_stats().mNumInverted`) and hole-freedom (`boundary_edges`/`inconsistent_pairs`) stay EXACT regardless of `mWarpFraction`. A caller needing an exactly watertight result uses `mWarpFraction = 0` or post-processes with `clean(weld=true)`. **Scope**: accepts a volume mesh (boundary extracted via `extract_surface`, closed by construction) OR a closed surface directly (`build_triangle_soup`'s own scope) — unlike plain `remesh`, which only ever works on a surface. **What does NOT survive**: identical to `remesh` — new points, new connectivity, no point/cell map, `point_data`/`cell_data`/regions dropped (`warn_regions_dropped`), `field_data` carried; compose with `interpolate`/`conservative_interpolate`. **C++-core only, no numpy fallback at all** — the `remesh`/`subdivide`/`agglomerate`/`decimate_volume` precedent: warp and cut are discrete branches on a sign near a threshold, so a second implementation could diverge into a different mesh, not a last-ulp difference; `_remesh_volume.py` raises `NotImplementedError` by name for any input. **Tier C on its own** (wholly new header/`.cpp` — `remesh_volume` alone would need no ABI bump), **but the RELEASE bumps `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_ABI_VERSION` 9→10 anyway, caused by `SmoothMethod::Odt` (see the Smoothing entry above), not by this operation**. `RemeshVolumeOptions` is pinned in `tests/cpp/test_abi_layout.cpp` from the release that introduces it (`MIO_ABI_LAYOUT(meshioplusplus::RemeshVolumeOptions, 224, 8)`) — the `RefineOptions`/`RemeshOptions` lesson (going unpinned "looks free" to grow) applied in advance rather than after the fact. **Full binding surface, shipped in one release**, the `remesh` precedent rather than `decimate_volume`'s deferral: C API `mio_remesh_volume_ex(mesh, const mio_remesh_volume_opts*, mio_remesh_volume_report*)` — the ONLY form (no flat `mio_remesh_volume`, unlike `mio_remesh`, since a brand-new entry point has no back-compat flat signature to keep), `mio_remesh_volume_opts` embedding `mio_sdf_opts distance` BY VALUE (the `mio_compute_sdf_opts` nesting precedent — its own `reserved` tail absorbs growth without shifting anything here; `mio_voxel_opts`'s OWN precedent, flattening just `sign`/`watertight_check`, applies only when an op needs a SUBSET of `SurfaceDistanceOptions`, not the full surface `remesh_volume` needs); Fortran `m%remesh_volume([resolution, cell_size, bounds, ..., warp_fraction, sign, watertight_check, num_vertices_warped, num_tets_rejected, num_non_manifold_edges, quality, stat, errmsg])` (no runtime layout guard, unlike `mio_refine_opts_t` — that guard is a documented ONE-OFF for `RefineOptions` specifically, not a blanket Fortran convention, and `mio_remesh_opts_t`/`mio_compute_sdf_opts_t`/`mio_voxel_opts_t` have none either); Julia `remesh_volume(mesh; resolution=nothing, cell_size=nothing, ..., watertight_check=:warn)` → a NamedTuple, `_CRemeshVolumeOpts`/`_CRemeshVolumeReport` mirrors with the standard `_check_abi_layout()` guard; R `mio_remesh_volume(mesh, resolution=NULL, cell_size=0, ..., watertight_check="warn")` → a named list, building the C struct's fields directly (own dedicated roxygen block; `weight`/`location`/`band`/`record_inside` deliberately NOT exposed, matching R's own established `mio_compute_sdf`/`mio_sample_distance` scope for `SurfaceDistanceOptions` — **the `NAMESPACE` export entry was added**, the documented past miss for exactly this shape of addition); WASM `remeshVolume(mesh, resolution, cellSize, bounds, padding, paddingRelative, maxCells, maxTets, warpFraction, sign, watertightCheck)`, reachable generically as a `{op: 'RemeshVolume', ...}` `convertSurfaceOps`/pipeline step with no extra WASM dispatch code needed (adding a `"RemeshVolume"` row to `pipeline_op_table()` was the entire pipeline-side cost, `apply_one_op`/`run_pipeline` both route through the shared `apply_pipeline_step`); a `remesh-volume` verb in both CLIs (`--resolution`/`--cell-size` one required, `--bounds`, `--padding[-relative]`, `--max-cells`, `--max-tets`, `--warp-fraction`, `--sign`, `--watertight-check`); a `RemeshVolume` row (`{Resolution, CellSize, Bounds, Padding, PaddingRelative, MaxCells, MaxTets, WarpFraction, Sign, WatertightCheck}`) in both pipeline engines' op tables, pinned equal by `test_op_table_matches_core`/`_core.pipeline_op_table`; a `remesh_volume` MCP tool closing the parity guard automatically.
  - **ODT remeshing (relocate + flip connectivity)** (`operations/optimize_volume.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_optimize_volume.py`, docs [`doc/optimize_volume.md`](doc/optimize_volume.md), v10.14.0, closing roadmap §1's "ODT" bullet's *remeshing* half — the connectivity-changing piece `SmoothMethod::Odt` (v10.13.0, positions only) deliberately did not attempt): `optimize_volume(mesh, OptimizeVolumeOptions{mMaxIterations, mRelocate, mFlip, mPreserveBoundary, mMinImprovement, mFrozen})` → `OptimizeVolumeResult{mMesh, mNumFlips, mNum23Flips, mNum32Flips, mNumVerticesMoved, mNumTets, mMinQualityBefore, mMinQualityAfter}` — raise a tetrahedral mesh's worst element quality by *ODT remeshing*: alternate ODT vertex relocation with quality-improving topological **flips** (2-3, 3-2), so both positions AND connectivity change. The **genuine "ODT remeshing"** the roadmap named, and the missing third member of a trio: `SmoothMethod::Odt` moves points on *fixed* connectivity, `remesh_volume` *discards* the input's tets for a fresh lattice mesh; this one improves an existing tet mesh's positions AND connectivity. **Predicate-free, in-posture** (the whole reason the literal Delaunay/ODT method was rejected in `remesh_volume`): a flip is applied iff a pure **signed-volume** test (`detail::cell_volume_from_corners`/`detail::det3`, never an in-sphere test) finds the local union convex AND the **minimum** scaled Jacobian over the new tets strictly beats the minimum over the tets it replaces by `mMinImprovement` — the Freitag & Ollivier-Gooch improvement rule (implemented from the published description only; no external mesh library, no predicate kernel), monotone in worst quality and hence terminating with no Delaunay optimality argument. **The relocation half delegates verbatim to `smooth`'s `SmoothMethod::Odt`** (one Float64 single-tetra-block temp mesh per sweep, so a Float32 input never accumulates one rounding per sweep), reusing all its boundary/feature/frozen pinning and inversion guard; the **flip half is the net-new kernel** — rebuild-per-sweep face/edge adjacency (own `optvol_`-prefixed anon helpers + FNV `OptvolArrHash`, no live-maintained incidence), a **serial** flip-application loop (ascending tet then local face for 2-3; ascending sorted edge key for 3-2 — flips mutate shared incidence, `decimate_volume`'s greedy-loop reasoning), new tets appended beyond the pass's fixed bound so a flip never reads stale adjacency, then compact. **2-3 winding is pinned by test** (`OptimizeVolume.TwentyThreeFlipWindingIsPositiveOnAConvexUnion`): orient the shared face so `(f0,f1,f2,d)` is positive, require `e` strictly on the far side, and the three new tets `(f0,f1,e,d)`/`(f1,f2,e,d)`/`(f2,f0,e,d)` all positive (a non-convex union makes one non-positive — the convexity test); 3-2 requires the two caps positively-orientable AND a volume-sum tiling match. **Boundary is invariant by construction**: 2-3 flips touch only interior faces, 3-2 only interior edges, so with `mPreserveBoundary` the output boundary surface is byte-identical to the input's (watertight in ⇒ watertight out, no `mNumNonManifoldEdges` risk unlike `remesh_volume`'s warp — a strong test oracle). **The point set is invariant** (relocation moves points, flips only reconnect them; none added/removed), so `point_data`/`field_data` and named **Point** regions carry through unchanged while `cell_data` + Cell/Side regions are **dropped with a warning** (a flip has no cell map) — more generous than `remesh_volume`, and honest: exactly what a preserved point set can preserve. Output is a single `tetra` block, canonicalized to positive orientation. **Tet-only** scope (the `smooth` Odt / `decimate_volume` shape): a non-`tetra` 3D block throws naming `convert_cells(Simplexify)`, a non-3D block alongside the tets throws naming `split`, ragged/tet-less throws by name. Byte-identical across the three mesh backends and thread counts (verified), and **C++-core only, no numpy fallback** — the flip acceptance is a discrete sign/near-tie branch (the `subdivide`/`agglomerate`/`decimate_volume`/`remesh_volume` precedent); `_optimize_volume.py` raises `NotImplementedError` by name for any input. **The distinguishing oracle** (`FlipsHelpWhereSmoothingCannot`): on a 2-tet fixture whose every vertex is on the boundary, relocation moves nothing yet quality still rises — proof the connectivity changed, i.e. remeshing not smoothing. **Tier C additive** (wholly new header/`.cpp`/C entry point, no existing installed header's layout changes) so **no ABI bump** (`MESHIOPLUSPLUS_ABI_VERSION` stays 10); `OptimizeVolumeOptions` is pinned in `tests/cpp/test_abi_layout.cpp` from this release (`MIO_ABI_LAYOUT(..., 40, 8)`). **Full binding surface in one release** (the `remesh_volume` precedent): the C ABI is a plain **`mio_optimize_volume`** (mesh + nullable scalar counter out-params, `mio_smooth`'s shape rather than an `_ex` opts struct — no nested struct, and future growth can add an `_ex` later like `mio_remesh` did; `mFrozen` a documented flat-ABI gap like `mio_smooth`'s); Fortran type-bound `m%optimize_volume([max_iterations, relocate, flip, preserve_boundary, min_improvement, num_flips, num_23_flips, num_32_flips, num_vertices_moved, num_tets, min_quality_before, min_quality_after, stat, errmsg])`; Julia `optimize_volume(m; max_iterations=10, relocate=true, flip=true, preserve_boundary=true, min_improvement=1e-6)` → NamedTuple; R `mio_optimize_volume(mesh, max_iterations=10L, ...)` → a named list (own dedicated roxygen block, **NAMESPACE export added** — the documented past miss for this shape); WASM `optimizeVolume(mesh, maxIterations, relocate, flip, preserveBoundary, minImprovement)`, reachable generically as a `{op:'optimizeVolume', ...}` `convertSurfaceOps`/pipeline step (the shared `apply_pipeline_step`/`pipeline_op_table()` mechanism, no extra WASM dispatch); an `optimize-volume` verb in both CLIs; an `OptimizeVolume` row (`{MaxIterations, Relocate, Flip, PreserveBoundary, MinImprovement}`) in both pipeline engines' op tables, pinned equal by `test_op_table_matches_core`; an `optimize_volume` MCP tool closing the parity guard automatically.

  - **Isosurfaces / contours** (`operations/isosurface.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_isosurface.py`, docs [`doc/isosurface.md`](doc/isosurface.md)): `isosurface(mesh, IsosurfaceOptions{mArrayName, mIsovalues, mComponent, mRecordParentIds})` → a plain new `Mesh` (transform/slice's shape, no maps/handle) that is the level set of a scalar field — the locus where a `point_data` array equals each isovalue, one topological dimension below the cut cells (3D volume → `triangle`/`quad` surface; 2D surface → `line` contour). The **data-driven sibling of `slice`**: slice cuts where `dot(x − origin, normal) = 0`, isosurface where `f(x) − isovalue = 0`, **through the same `detail/marching.hpp` cutter** — so the watertightness, winding, degeneracy and determinism guarantees are literally the same code, and this file owns only what is field-specific. **The field must be `point_data`**: `cell_data` is piecewise constant, so there is no crossing to locate and no level set to draw — naming one throws by name and points at `cell_data_to_point_data` (CLI `data to-point`), while an unknown name goes through `data_unknown_key_message`. Multi-component arrays reduce via `mComponent` or the row magnitude, using `iso_scalarize` — deliberately the same arithmetic as `face_color.cpp`'s file-private `fc_scalarize` (sum-of-squares left to right, one `sqrt`) so the numpy twin rounds alike. **Several isovalues land in ONE mesh**: sorted ascending with exact duplicates dropped (a repeat would emit the same contour twice), cut against **one** `marching_prepare` (the simplexify is paid for once), then concatenated by `iso_concat_contours` with the section blocks **merged by cell type** in the cutter's canonical triangle/quad/line order — so a single-isovalue call has exactly slice's block structure. Two tags per contour cell: Float64 **`iso:value`** (the level) and Int64 **`iso:index`** (its ordinal) — both, because `split(SplitBy::Tag)` only accepts an *integer* array, which makes `split(SplitBy::Tag, "iso:index")` — CLI `split --by region --tag iso:index` — the one-mesh-per-contour recipe and `iso:value` the thing you colour by. **The contoured field reads back as EXACTLY the isovalue**: every array is interpolated at the crossing `t`, but the contoured one is *overwritten* during the concat pass (interpolation only reaches the isovalue to within round-off, and the value is known analytically); the exception is a magnitude reduction, where `|lerp(v)| != lerp(|v|)` **mathematically**, so nothing exact exists to write and that case stays interpolated. Degeneracy is inherited: a node exactly at the isovalue counts positive, so a **plateau** at the isovalue emits its boundary once, not twice. Faces are wound toward **increasing field** (`MarchingOrientation::FieldGradient`). An isovalue outside the range is an empty contour, not an error; all outside returns the first cut's (zero-row `point_data`) mesh, matching slice's empty shape. `mRecordParentIds` attaches Int64 `iso:parent_cell`; each contour cell inherits its parent's `cell_data`; `point_sets`/`cell_sets`, `mesh.info`, `gmsh_periodic` **not** carried. Output is **byte-identical across the three backends, thread counts, and the C++/numpy boundary** (`tests/python/test_isosurface.py::test_cpp_matches_python`). C API `mio_isosurface(mesh, array_name, const double* isovalues, int n_isovalues, int component /*negative = magnitude*/, int record_parent_ids)` → `mio_mesh*`. Fortran type-bound `m%isosurface(array, isovalues(:) [, component, record_parent_ids])`. WASM `isosurface(mesh, array, isovalues, component, recordParentIds)` (accepts a scalar or an array). CLI verb `isosurface IN OUT --array NAME --values v1,v2 [--component I] [--record-parent-ids]` in both CLIs (negatives need the `--values=` form). The **browser viewer's** isosurface chip routes through this (see the viewer entry).
  - **Field derivatives** (`operations/gradient.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_gradient.py`, docs [`doc/gradient.md`](doc/gradient.md)): `gradient(mesh, GradientOptions{mArrayName, mOperator, mMethod, mLocation, mOutputName, mComponent, mOverwrite})` → `GradientResult{mMesh, mNumSkipped, mNumFallback}` — the gradient, divergence or curl of a `point_data` field. **It lives in the mesh-operations layer, not the `data_*` bundle**, and that is the one thing to state up front: the five data ops are *defined* by never touching geometry, while this one consumes and produces data arrays but **reads** geometry and topology (face areas, cell volumes, cell adjacency). Hence `mPascalCase` option fields (the geometry-op convention) rather than the data bundle's snake_case, and hence the entry here. It is still reachable as **`data gradient`** in both CLIs, a deliberate departure noted in `doc/cli.md` and in `_cli/_data.py`'s docstring, because that is where a user looks for it. This is the operation that gives `isosurface` and selective `refine` an input they never had: contouring `|grad T|`/vorticity, and gradient-magnitude error indicators. Result shape is **`smooth`/`clean`'s** (a plain mesh + counters, no opaque handle — there are no index maps to hand back). **Green-Gauss** (default) fans each face into triangles about its **arithmetic corner average** and accumulates `A_j * f(centroid_j)`, with `V = (1/3) sum(x_j . A_j)` from the same fan: **exact for a linear field on ANY cell, planar faces or not**, because two faces sharing an edge contribute oppositely-wound triangles there, so the fan surface is closed and `∮ f n dA = V grad f` is a purely algebraic identity on a closed oriented piecewise-linear surface; `V` is the exact signed volume *of that same surface*, so numerator and denominator flip together and an inverted cell gives its positively-wound twin's answer. **The corner-average apex is forced, not convenient** — the quadrature needs `f` at the apex, and for linear `f` the corner average is the only point whose value is known exactly without shape functions (`mean(f(p_i)) == f(mean(p_i))`); do NOT "simplify" it to the area centroid, and note that a plain corner-average face value (no fan) is wrong by 12.5% on the trapezoid `(0,0),(4,0),(2,1),(0,1)` under `f = y`. **A cube or an axis-aligned hex grid cannot detect any of this** (a parallelogram's corner average IS its area centroid), which is why the fixtures are a frustum and a warped hex — verified by sabotaging the fan and watching only those fire. 2D runs the same theorem over the corner ring with the in-plane outward normal `t x N` from the cell's own Newell normal: exact on a **planar** cell, invariant under reversal *and* cyclic rotation of the ring, first-order on a warped quad in 3D. **`cell_edges.hpp` is deliberately NOT consulted** — its rows are exactly `(i, i+1 mod n)` over the corner ring, so it buys nothing and would wrongly reject `triangle10`/`quad16`, which the ring handles; the 3D gate is instead "`cell_faces(rawType)` is non-empty", which natively covers `tetra10`/`hexahedron20/27`/`wedge15/18`/`pyramid13/14` (corners lead every row) and skips the 3D Lagrange family. **Recentring on the cell's corner average before any arithmetic is load-bearing, not an optimization**: `V = (1/3) sum x_j . A_j` only telescopes because `sum A_j == 0`, and at `x ~ 1e8` the raw form loses eight digits to cancellation and then divides by the result — pinned by a translate-the-mesh test that was **verified inert at 1e6/1e-8** before being tightened to 1e8/1e-9. **Least squares** fits `f ~ f_c + g . (x - x_c)` over `detail/cell_adjacency.hpp`'s node-sharing neighbours restricted to blocks of the same topological dimension, with `x_c`/`f_c` the corner averages so `(x_c, f_c)` lies exactly on a linear field (exact under **any** positive weights; the `1/|d|^2` choice only makes `M = sum d_hat d_hat^T` dimensionless and grading-immune). The 3x3 cofactor solve and its `1e-12 * scale*scale*scale` conditioning bound are transcribed token-for-token from `decimate.cpp` — never `std::pow`/`np.power`, which round differently and would break parity. For `dim == 2` the offsets are projected into the cell's plane and the rank-2 matrix is closed with a **rank-1 regularization** `M + s*N N^T` (`s = trace/2`) through the SAME solver, deliberately instead of a tangent-plane orthonormal basis whose Gram-Schmidt tie-break would be an arbitrary new convention to transcribe into the numpy twin; `b ⊥ N` by construction, so the solution satisfies `g . N == 0` for free. Exact on a **planar** 2D mesh, first-order on a curved surface. A degenerate neighbourhood (a lone cell, a collinear strip) **falls back to Green-Gauss** and increments `mNumFallback` — never NaN when a usable answer exists — counted through a per-cell flag reduced **serially** afterwards (data_average's idiom), not an atomic. Shapes: `nc` components in → `3*nc` out, **flat**, row-major `[component i][derivative j]` at `i*3 + j` (scalar → `(n,3)`, 3-vector → `(n,9)` as `{rows, 9}`, never `{rows,3,3}` — the WASM `*_components` sidecar and the R/Julia bindings carry one integer); `Divergence` → 1, `Curl` → always 3, both needing `nc ∈ {2,3}` with a 2-component field read as `(u,v,0)` (`read_point`'s 2D padding convention). **On a 2D mesh `d/dz` is unrecoverable and reads 0**, so a 3-component field's divergence is its *in-plane* divergence and its curl is `(d_y w, -d_x w, d_x v - d_y u)` — only a **2**-component field yields a z-only curl; documented rather than papered over. `mComponent` unset means **every** component — the OPPOSITE of `IsosurfaceOptions::mComponent`, where unset means the row magnitude; same name, same type, opposite meaning, noted in both headers, and the flat-binding sentinel is `-1` on both. Setting it with Divergence/Curl throws. Output is always Float64, named `<input>:gradient`/`:divergence`/`:curl` — deliberately `name:suffix`, **not** the repo's usual `prefix:name` (`iso:value`, `partition:part`), so everything derived from one field sorts next to it. `mLocation == Point` **composes** `cell_data_to_point_data` then `data_drop` rather than reimplementing a scatter (so point values are exact to within rounding, **not bit-exact** — summing n copies of g and dividing by n is not g in IEEE, hence tolerances in the point-located tests). Unsupported cells are **NaN and counted** (`compute_quality`'s convention): blocks below the mesh's max dimension, ragged/polyhedron, 3D Lagrange, and a measure degenerate **relative to the cell's own size** (never an absolute epsilon). **There is no `NanPolicy`** — Green-Gauss has no reduction to exclude a value from, so one non-finite corner poisons its cell; stated in the header rather than silently diverging from `data_common.hpp`'s policy. `warn_regions_dropped` is deliberately **not** called (geometry is untouched); geometry/regions/property sets/existing data ride through `detail::clone_mesh` bit-identically. Byte-identical across the three backends, thread counts and the C++/numpy boundary (`tests/python/test_gradient.py::test_cpp_matches_python`, parametrized over both methods x both locations x scalar/non-linear/tensor/component/div/curl, plus a skipped-and-fallback case). The numpy twin vectorizes over **cells** while looping in Python over the fixed face/fan-triangle sequence (so per-cell order matches exactly) and uses a **padded-gather loop** for the least-squares accumulation (`np.add.reduceat` is not order-safe); `np.mean`/`np.sum`/`np.einsum`/`np.linalg.solve`/`np.power` are banned throughout, and masked accumulation is `where(mask, M + term, M)` never `M += where(mask, term, 0)` (`-0.0 + 0.0 == +0.0` is a real parity break). C API `mio_gradient(mesh, array_name, op, method, location, output_name, component, overwrite, &num_skipped, &num_fallback)` → `mio_mesh*` (NULL strings = defaults, counters nullable like `mio_clean`'s). Fortran `m%gradient(array [, op, method, location, output, component, overwrite, num_skipped, num_fallback])`. Julia `gradient(m, array; operator, method, location, ...)` → NamedTuple (`method` takes an underscored symbol, translated to the C API's hyphenated name — a Julia symbol cannot carry a hyphen). R `mio_gradient(...)` → a named list, counters as `double`. WASM `gradient(...)` → `{mesh, numSkipped, numFallback}` **plus a `convertSurfaceOps` pipeline step** — the only one that changes no geometry, so the mesh passes straight through with one array attached. CLI `data gradient IN OUT --array NAME [--op gradient|divergence|curl] [--method green-gauss|least-squares] [--location cell|point] [--output NAME] [--component I] [--overwrite]` in both CLIs (defaults kept string-identical), an MCP tool, and the browser viewer's **Derivative** chip.
