  - **Second derivatives (Hessian)** (`operations/hessian.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_hessian.py`, docs [`doc/hessian.md`](doc/hessian.md), v10.9.0, closing roadmap §1 in full — the section's last open item): `hessian(mesh, HessianOptions{mArrayName, mMethod, mLocation, mOutputName, mOverwrite})` → `HessianResult{mMesh, mNumSkipped, mNumFallback}` — the Hessian (second derivative) of a **scalar** `point_data` field, `gradient`'s companion one order further (`gradient` differentiates a field once, this differentiates it twice). **Deliberately a COMPOSITION of TWO `gradient` calls, not a new numerical kernel** — the `estimate_error` precedent applied one differentiation order further: (1) `gradient(mArrayName, location=Point, method=mMethod)` gives the field's gradient as a genuine `point_data` array, `(n,3)`; (2) `gradient()` again on THAT array with the default `Gradient` operator — generic over its input's own component count, no special-casing — differentiates the 3-component field and produces `(n,9)`, the flattened row-major 3x3 Hessian, `H[i][j]` at index `i*3+j`. **The first pass MUST use `Point` location regardless of `mLocation`** — the second pass's own `point_data`-only validation would otherwise reject a `cell_data` intermediate; the second pass always computes at `Cell` first, mirroring `gradient.cpp`'s own unconditional cell-first step, then composes `cell_data_to_point_data(weight=Uniform)` + `data_drop` if `mLocation == Point`, byte-for-byte `gradient.cpp`'s own Point-location handling. `mMethod` simply forwards to both internal passes — no new differentiation math exists for Hessian at all, so `LeastSquares` is available for free. Private working names (`__hessian_raw_gradient__`, `__hessian_raw__`) never reach the returned mesh; the actual result is built from a fresh `detail::clone_mesh(rMesh)` plus the final `(n,9)` array copied out of the second pass's intermediate mesh, `error.cpp`'s exact isolation discipline (a same-named collision in the caller's own data cannot corrupt anything). **Scalar-only, v1**: `field_comp != 1` throws by name ("call it once per component of a vector field") — a vector field's Hessian is `(n, 3*9)`, a separate need deliberately out of scope. **Exactness is stated honestly, not oversold, and the honest claim was TIGHTENED by direct measurement while writing the tests**: the one mesh-shape-independent guarantee is that a field at most LINEAR has an exactly zero Hessian everywhere (its gradient is a constant, and Green-Gauss of a constant field is trivially exact — the same class of guarantee `test_gradient.cpp`'s own `CurlOfAGradientAndDivergenceOfACurlVanish` already relies on, for the identical reason). For a genuinely QUADRATIC field, each Green-Gauss pass alone would be exact, but the mandatory intermediate `Uniform`-weighted point-averaging step is only exact for a field constant over the averaged neighbourhood — for a genuinely-varying linear intermediate (the gradient of a true quadratic) on an IRREGULAR mesh, averaging several cells' individually-exact-but-different values does not in general reproduce the true nodal value. **Measured, not merely argued**: on a regular axis-aligned hex grid, every INTERIOR node's 8-cell neighbourhood is exactly symmetric enough, and the composed Hessian comes back exact to MACHINE PRECISION there (originally planned as a loose `< 0.25` empirical tolerance test; probing the actual interior error found it to be exactly 0.0, so the test was rewritten as a genuine exactness assertion — `test_hessian.cpp`'s `QuadraticFieldOnAStructuredGridIsExactAwayFromTheBoundary`); the SAME mesh's own boundary cells (one-sided neighbourhood) show real, bounded, nonzero error (`...HasBoundedBoundaryError`, asserting both `> 1e-6` and `< 3.0` so the oracle cannot be vacuously satisfied by a since-fixed bug). **No curvature-driven marking subsystem was built** — unlike `estimate_error`'s ZZ-specific marking, `data_calc`'s existing `norm(...)` (confirmed: plain sum-of-squares-then-sqrt over however many components its argument has, `DATA_CALC_MAX_COMPONENTS=16` comfortably covers 9) on the 9-component Hessian output is exactly its Frobenius norm with zero new code, so the worked composition in `doc/hessian.md` is `hessian` → `data_calc norm` → `refine --where`, mirroring `gradient.md`'s own worked-compositions section. Result shape is **`gradient`/`estimate_error`'s** (plain mesh + counters, no opaque handle). C API `mio_hessian(mesh, array_name, method, location, output_name, overwrite, &num_skipped, &num_fallback)` → `mio_mesh*` (NULL strings = defaults, counters nullable like `mio_clean`'s). Fortran `m%hessian(array [, method, location, output, overwrite, num_skipped, num_fallback])`. Julia `hessian(m, array; method, location, ...)` → NamedTuple, reusing `gradient`'s own `_method_name` hyphen-translation helper. R `mio_hessian(...)` → a named list, counters as `double` (own dedicated roxygen block rather than joining the `mio_extract_surface` `@rdname` group, the `mio_data_integrate` precedent; **the `NAMESPACE` export entry was added**, the lesson from the `data_integrate` miss earlier this repo's history). WASM `hessian(...)` → `{mesh, numSkipped, numFallback}` **plus a `Hessian` `convertSurfaceOps`/`pipe_op_table()` pipeline step** (`{Array, Method, Location, Output}`) — reachable automatically through the shared `run_pipeline_steps` dispatch both `apply_one_op` (WASM) and `run_pipeline`/`_core.run_pipeline_file` (Python/C++) already route through; unlike `data_integrate` (report-only, deliberately excluded from the pipeline entirely), Hessian DOES attach a new array so it belongs in the pipeline the same way `Gradient`/`EstimateError` do. The Python pipeline engine's `Hessian` branch calls the public `hessian()` function (which itself prefers `_core.hessian`), so with `_core` installed both pipeline engines call the identical underlying `gradient()` twice — `test_pipeline.py::test_hessian_pipeline_step` therefore asserts bit-for-bit cross-engine equality via `meshes_equal`, unlike `EstimateError`'s own tolerance-only pipeline test. Python's own numpy "fallback" (`_hessian_py`) is likewise not a separate kernel: it composes the already-parity-tested public `gradient()` Python function twice (verified byte-identical to `_core.hessian`'s own output, not merely close, so `test_hessian.py::test_cpp_matches_python` uses exact equality rather than `pytest.approx`). CLI `data hessian IN OUT --array NAME [--method green-gauss|least-squares] [--location cell|point] [--output NAME] [--overwrite]` in both CLIs (defaults kept string-identical), an MCP tool (`wraps=("hessian",)`, closing the parity guard automatically).
  - **Error estimation** (`operations/error.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_error.py`, docs [`doc/error.md`](doc/error.md), v10.2.0, closing roadmap §1's error-estimator-helpers bullet): `estimate_error(mesh, ErrorOptions{mArrayName, mMethod, mMarking, mMarkingValue, mOutputName, mMarkedName, mOverwrite})` → `ErrorResult{mMesh, mGlobalError, mNumSkipped, mNumMarked}` — the Zienkiewicz-Zhu (ZZ) recovery-based error indicator of a `point_data` field, plus optional marking, the piece that closes the adaptive loop: `gradient` already differentiates and selective `refine`'s `--where` already consumes any scalar `cell_data` predicate; this produces one. **Deliberately a COMPOSITION, not a new numerical kernel**: `gradient(mLocation=Cell)` (raw per-cell gradient) → `cell_data_to_point_data(weight=Measure)` (recovery onto points) → `point_data_to_cell_data` (back onto cells) gives the recovered gradient `G_K`; the indicator is `eta_K = sqrt(|measure_K| * sum((G_K - g_K)^2))`, `measure_K` reusing the exact same `detail::cell_measure` primitive the recovery step's `Measure` weighting already called, so the two agree by construction. `mGlobalError = sqrt(sum eta_K^2)` over evaluable cells. Lives in the mesh-operations layer for the same reason `gradient` does (reads geometry though it produces data) and is reachable as **`data estimate-error`** in both CLIs alongside it. Composed internally via fixed private working names (`"__estimate_error_raw__"` + `"_recovered"` suffix) that never reach the returned mesh — the actual result is built as `detail::clone_mesh(rMesh)` plus the two final arrays, so even a same-named collision in the caller's own data can only corrupt a discarded intermediate copy, never the real output. **Marking** turns the continuous indicator into `error:marked` (Int64 0/1, one array per block, attached only when `mMarking != None`) so `refine --where "error:marked > 0.5"` needs no change to `refine` at all: `Absolute` (threshold), `Fraction` (top `k = size_t(ratio * N + 0.5)` by descending indicator — `decimate`'s own rounding convention, never `llround`), `Dorfler` (smallest descending-indicator prefix whose cumulative `eta_K^2` covers a bulk fraction theta — the AMR-standard bulk-chunk criterion). Ties break on ascending global cell index (`detail::block_bases`), a strict weak order independent of scheduling. **NaN, never a guess**: a cell `gradient` could not differentiate, whose recovery neighbourhood had no finite contribution, or whose `measure_K` is unmeasurable reads NaN in `error:zz` and **0 (never NaN)** in `error:marked`, counted in `mNumSkipped` and excluded from `mGlobalError` and every marking policy's count. Output names are fixed `prefix:name` constants (`error:zz`, `error:marked`) — unlike `gradient`'s deliberately unusual `name:suffix` — since the estimator has exactly one continuous and one boolean output regardless of which field produced them. **A real, independently-discovered bug surfaced and was fixed while building this**: `_data_average.py`'s `_FACES` table (the numpy twin's tabulated-cell fan-volume formula, reused by `Measure`-weighted `cell_data_to_point_data`) had **inconsistent face winding** for all four linear 3D types (tetra/hexahedron/wedge/pyramid) — one inward-wound face per type — which the origin-tetrahedra volume decomposition needs to be globally consistent to give a translation-invariant answer; a unit cube reported volume 1.0 at the origin but 2.33 when translated, silently corrupting `Measure`-weighted recovery (and hence THIS estimator) for any mesh not centred near the origin. Fixed by transcribing `detail/cell_faces.cpp`'s Newell-normal-gtested windings verbatim (corner-only columns); pinned by `test_data_location.py::test_cell_measures_are_translation_invariant`, parametrized over all four types with a `(37, -19, 53)` translation, verified to fire against the pre-fix table. **Twin policy is case-by-case, not blanket-exempt**: the raw (pre-recovery) gradient stays bit-exact; the recovery step, and so the final indicator, matches only to the SAME already-accepted tolerance `data_average`'s own `Measure` weighting has (`test_data_location.py::test_cpp_matches_python` uses `np.allclose`, not exact equality) — `test_error.py::test_cpp_matches_python` inherits it rather than re-deriving a new one. The numpy twin raises `NotImplementedError` for a ragged/polygon/polyhedron block (the `_convert_cells.py:142` precedent: `detail::cell_measure` runs `orient_rings`' winding repair for ANY closed 3D cell, tabulated or not — not just polyhedra, a finding that corrected the original plan) and, separately, for a KNOWN quadratic 3D type (`tetra10`, `hexahedron20`/`27`, `wedge15`) since `_cell_measures`' face table only covers the four linear types while `detail::cell_measure` reduces quadratic cells to corners exactly as `gradient` does — a gap in the *reference* only, scoped no wider than that (a genuinely unknown/exotic type is left alone, since both sides already skip it gracefully with no disagreement to guard against). Result shape is **`gradient`/`smooth`'s** (plain mesh + counters, no opaque handle). C API `mio_estimate_error(mesh, array_name, method, marking, marking_value, output_name, marked_name, overwrite, &global_error, &num_skipped, &num_marked)` → `mio_mesh*` (NULL strings = defaults, all three counters nullable). Fortran `m%estimate_error(array [, method, marking, marking_value, output, marked, overwrite, global_error, num_skipped, num_marked])`. Julia `estimate_error(m, array; method, marking, marking_value, ...)` → NamedTuple. R `mio_estimate_error(...)` → a named list, counters as `double`. WASM `estimateError(...)` → `{mesh, globalError, numSkipped, numMarked}` **plus an `EstimateError` `convertSurfaceOps`/`pipe_op_table()` pipeline step** (`{Array, Method, Marking, MarkingValue, Output, Marked}`) — reachable automatically through the shared `run_pipeline_steps` dispatch both `apply_one_op` (WASM) and `run_pipeline`/`_core.run_pipeline_file` (Python/C++) already route through, so no per-surface pipeline code was needed beyond the `pipe_op_table()`/`_OP_TABLE` entry and the dispatch branch. Not bit-exact across engines (per the tolerance above), so `test_pipeline.py`'s dedicated `test_estimate_error_pipeline_step` checks both engines attach the right arrays rather than joining the existing `meshes_equal`-based `test_cpp_matches_python` chain. CLI `data estimate-error IN OUT --array NAME [--method zz] [--marking none|absolute|fraction|dorfler] [--marking-value V] [--output NAME] [--marked NAME] [--overwrite]` in both CLIs (defaults kept string-identical), an MCP tool (`wraps=("estimate_error",)`, closing the parity guard automatically).
  - **Field integration** (`operations/data_integrate.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_data_integrate.py`, docs [`doc/field_integration.md`](doc/field_integration.md), v10.8.0, closing roadmap §1's field-integration bullet): `data_integrate(mesh, DataIntegrateOptions{mArrayNames})` → `DataIntegrateReport{mArrays: [FieldIntegralArray{mName, mNumComponents, mDomain, mRegions}]}` where `FieldIntegralRegion{mName, mNumCells, mNumSkipped, mDomainMeasurePerComponent, mTotalPerComponent, mMeanPerComponent, mNumNanPerComponent}` — a cell-measure-weighted total/mean of one or more `cell_data` arrays, `gradient`'s integration counterpart (`gradient` differentiates a field, this integrates one: total mass from a density field, total heat flux, an occupied volume). **Lives in the mesh-operations layer for the same reason `gradient`/`estimate_error` do** (reads `detail::cell_measure` — length/area/volume by topological dimension — though it produces only data) and is reachable as **`data integrate`** in both CLIs alongside them. **Report-only, like `data_info`/`compute_stats`**: the mesh is never modified, no opaque handle, no mesh-with-a-new-array shape. **Weighting**: `sum(value * |measure|)` / `sum(|measure|)` per component; a cell whose measure is not computable (ragged, unsupported type, degenerate) is excluded from **both** numerator and denominator — never a fallback weight of 1, unlike `cell_data_to_point_data`'s own `Measure` weighting, since a silent unit-weight substitution would corrupt a physical total in a way it only softens an average (counted in `mNumSkipped`). A **non-finite value** in one component excludes that cell from that component's numerator **and** denominator too (not just zeroed into the numerator), counted per-component in `mNumNanPerComponent` — the geometric exclusion rule applied one level down. **No whole-array (cross-component) total** — only per-component, since summing a vector field's components has no general physical meaning, unlike `data_info`'s flattened mean. **No `NanPolicy`** — the `gradient`/`data_info` precedent: nothing to exclude a value *from*. **`cell_data` only**: a `point_data` name throws naming `point_data_to_cell_data` (CLI `data to-cell`) — the mirror of `gradient`'s own `point_data`-only contract. **Regions are not filtered and not a partition**: every named `RegionKind::Cell` region gets its own independent entry (`split`'s "split by region" contract) — a cell in two regions contributes fully to both, one in none contributes to neither; `Point`/`Side` regions are skipped (no cell-measure meaning for a point/facet group). Per-cell measures are computed **once** (`parallel_for`, shared across every array and every region, mirroring `data_average.cpp`'s weight-precompute idiom) via `detail::block_bases`; region cells resolve via `detail::global_to_block_row`, exactly as `split_by_region` does. The new shared accumulator **`detail::WeightedSum`/`detail::accumulate_weighted`** (`detail/data_ops.hpp`, beside `FiniteStats`/`accumulate_stats`/`cell_measure`) is a chunked-`parallel_for`-then-serially-merged `sum(value*weight)`/`sum(weight)`/non-finite-count reducer — the same determinism idiom `accumulate_stats` already uses for `data_info`, so output is byte-identical across the three mesh backends, thread counts, and the C++/numpy boundary (`tests/python/test_data_integrate.py::test_cpp_matches_python`, `pytest.approx` rather than exact equality since the numpy twin's vectorized `.sum()` is not guaranteed bit-identical to the chunked-parallel-merge order on larger inputs — a smaller mixed-block/NaN-cell case is pinned exact instead). The Python numpy twin (`_data_integrate_py`, full fallback — a plain arithmetic reduction with no discrete sign/winding branch, unlike `subdivide`/`agglomerate`) reuses `_data_average.py`'s `_cell_measures(mesh, block)` rather than re-deriving per-type formulas, and resolves same-named region candidates by sorting `(dim, tag)` before picking the first, matching `Mesh::FindRegion`'s canonical `(kind,name,dim,tag)`-sorted storage order exactly (verified byte-identical). C API opaque `mio_data_integrate` (mirrors `mio_data_info`'s 6-function shape extended with a region axis): `mio_data_integrate_create(mesh, names, count)` (`names=NULL` = every array) / `_count` / `_name` / `_entry(index, out mio_field_integral_info{num_components, num_cells, num_skipped})` (whole-mesh) / `_component(index, comp, total*, mean*, domain_measure*, num_nan*)` / `_region_count(index)` / `_region_name(index, region, ...)` / `_region_entry` / `_region_component` / `_free`. No ABI bump — wholly new entry points, no existing installed header's layout changed (Tier C additive). Fortran **splits domain and region access into two calls** rather than nesting three axes (array x region x component) into one call's optional out-arguments — `m%data_integrate(arrays=, keys=, totals=, means=, domain_measures=, num_nans=)` (flat, array-major, per-component buffers, the `mesh_regions`/`mesh_data_info` flat-buffer idiom) for the whole mesh, `m%data_integrate_region(array_name, keys=, totals=, ...)` for one named array's per-region breakdown — the C interfaces `mio_field_integral_info` bind(c) type placed near `mio_data_array_info`. Julia `data_integrate(mesh, names=String[])` → `Vector{NamedTuple}` (`name`, `num_components`, `domain::NamedTuple{...,components}`, `regions::Vector{NamedTuple}`) — the C-struct-per-index style Fortran needs is unnecessary here since Julia nests NamedTuples natively; exported in `MeshioPlusPlus.jl` alongside `data_info`. R `mio_data_integrate(mesh, names=NULL)` → a nested named-list mirroring the C++ report shape 1:1, `components` as an `(nc x 4)` matrix with `total`/`mean`/`domain_measure`/`num_nan` dimnames columns — **remember the `NAMESPACE` export entry**, missed once before for exactly this shape of addition (see the R lesson recorded elsewhere in this file). WASM `dataIntegrate(mesh, arrays)` → a plain JS array of objects (no opaque handle, `data_info_js`'s own choice), per-component data as parallel `totalPerComponent`/`meanPerComponent`/`domainMeasurePerComponent`/`numNanPerComponent` arrays (the `data_info_js` `*PerComponent` convention) on both `domain` and each region object — **not** an array-of-component-objects, for consistency with `dataInfo`'s existing shape; forwarded in `index.mjs`, typed in `index.d.ts` (`FieldIntegralArray`/`FieldIntegralRegion`), exercised through the wrapper in `smoke.mjs` (whose exhaustive forward-reachability guard gained the name) — **not** wired into the settings pipeline / `convertSurfaceOps`, since it is report-only like `data_info`/`stats`/`diff`, which are absent from `pipeline_op_table()`/`_OP_TABLE` entirely rather than excluded-with-a-message (that message class is reserved for genuinely mesh-transforming multi-mesh ops a user might plausibly expect to chain — `Merge`/`Interpolate`/`Split`/`Diff` — not for report-only ops, which were never plausible pipeline steps to begin with). CLI `data integrate IN --array NAME [--array NAME2 ...] [--json]` in both CLIs — report-only like `data info`, no OUTFILE since the mesh is never modified; an MCP tool (`wraps=("data_integrate",)`, closing the parity guard automatically) in the read-only-report grouping alongside `data_info`/`stats`/`regions`, not the mesh-ops grouping.
  - **Regular grids and signed distance** (`operations/voxelize.{hpp,cpp}` + `operations/sdf.{hpp,cpp}`, `detail/{grid_lattice,point_triangle,tri_box,surface_distance}.*`, Python `_grid.py`/`_voxelize.py`/`_sdf.py`, docs [`doc/voxelize.md`](doc/voxelize.md) + [`doc/sdf.md`](doc/sdf.md), v9.24.0): `grid(dims, origin, spacing)` builds a regular hexahedron lattice **from nothing** -- the library's first mesh *generator*, every other operation transforming a mesh you already have -- and `voxelize(mesh, VoxelOptions{...})` builds one around a mesh keeping the whole bounding box (`VoxelFill::All`), only the cells a surface triangle passes through (`Surface`) or only those inside it (`Inside`). `sample_distance`/`distance_to_surface`/`surface_watertight_check` are the distance half. **The output is an ordinary `hexahedron` `CellBlock` and that IS the design**: every writer, `view`/`screenshot`, `crop`/`split`/`data calc`, `--color-by` and `isosurface` already work on it with no new code and no new format. `custom` was rejected because `cell_type_num_nodes(Custom) == -1` and `cell_type_dimension(Custom) == -1` make a block invisible to `stats`/`quality`/`surface`/`gradient`/`refine` and unwritable by most formats -- forfeiting exactly the property that motivates the choice; a new `voxel` cell type was rejected too (VTK type 11 is deliberately unmapped in `_vtk_common.py`, and adding it means a row in all 42 format tables to buy an implicit ordering nothing needs). **The lattice numbering is INHERITED, not chosen**: points x-fastest, `vid = (k*(ny+1)+j)*(nx+1)+i`, VTK hex winding -- what `test_refine.cpp`'s `hex_grid` and its Python twin already agree on, and VTK ImageData's own layout (so `cell_data` reshapes to `arr[z,y,x]`, and a future `.vti` writer is a copy). A minority of older fixtures (`test_smooth.cpp`, `test_openfoam.cpp`, `mio_notebook.hpp`, `benchmark/inputs.py`) use a transposed linearization and are deliberately left alone. Coordinates are `origin + index*spacing`, **never accumulated** (accumulation makes the last plane depend on the cell count in the low bits and makes the twin's `arange`-vs-loop choice observable; `np.arange` is banned in the twin for the same reason). Every cell is a right parallelepiped, so `attach_quality` reports scaled Jacobian **exactly 1.0** -- a free structural oracle that a transposed axis trips as an *inverted cell*, and the tests use it as one (verified to fire by mirroring the rings; note a weaker sabotage -- transposing two corners of one ring -- twists the cell without negating its volume and does NOT fire). **The sign is the hard part.** The standard mistake is taking it from the nearest *triangle's* normal instead of the nearest *feature's* angle-weighted pseudonormal (Baerentzen & Aanaes: face -> its own normal, edge -> the sum of the two incident unit normals, vertex -> the angle-weighted sum). **A reentrant corner does NOT expose that bug** -- at a concave edge both incident faces give the correct sign and the naive method passes, which is what an earlier fixture proved nothing with; the failure needs two nearly *opposite* normals, i.e. a sharp spike, and `test_surface_distance.cpp` uses a sliver prism whose tip subtends ~1.7 degrees and *demonstrates* the naive method failing rather than asserting the right answer. `SdfSign::WindingNumber` is the honest answer for a non-watertight STL and is **O(triangles) per query**, guarded by `mMaxWindingWork` rather than silently running for an hour. **The accelerator is provably unobservable and that is load-bearing**: every candidate comparison is totally ordered on `(distance squared, triangle id)`, so the `SpatialGrid` bucket size cannot change the answer -- which is why `mGridCellSize` is a *public* option (a test proves the invariance), why the bucket heuristic could be retuned after measurement with **byte-identical** output (sizing by mean triangle alone made a 64^3 inside-fill of the 112k-triangle bunny take 19.1s; adding the domain extent cut it to 2.9s), and why the **numpy twin needs no accelerator at all** -- a brute-force scan in ascending triangle order is the same computation, hence bit-identical. A BVH was rejected precisely because its build order and split heuristic WOULD be observable. Byte-parity stops in exactly one place: `WindingNumber` sums one `atan2` per triangle and thresholds the total, so the twin **raises `NotImplementedError`** (`_smooth.py`'s inversion-guard call) rather than hiding a genuine disagreement behind a tolerance. `mMaxCells` (default 20M, just above 256^3) refuses by name: 128^3 is 0.19 GB, 256^3 is 1.5 GB and 512^3 is 11.8 GB. The C ABI carries `mio_voxel_opts`/`mio_sdf_opts`/`mio_surface_quality` with the `mio_refine_opts` reserved-tail discipline, and **deliberately does NOT nest** them the way the C++ options do -- `SdfOptions`/`VoxelOptions` embed `SurfaceDistanceOptions` by value, which is a Tier A trap the flat ABI need not inherit; sizes are `static_assert`ed and mirrored by the Fortran and Julia layout guards. Point compaction after a selective fill numbers survivors in **ascending original order** (surface.cpp's used/remap), not on first encounter -- a bug the numpy twin caught, since first-encounter order makes the point ids depend on the hexahedron's node order. **v9.25.0 completed the item** with `compute_sdf`, the octree, the `.vti` format and `crop_predicate`. `compute_sdf` is a *composition* -- resolve the lattice, build it, `distance_to_surface` -- plus two things it owns: the `sdf:*` `field_data` header and, for `SdfStructure::Octree`, a ~50-line driver that iterates `refine(RefineClosure::Balanced)` over the cells within `mBandCells` of their **own** diagonal. `mResolution`/`mCellSize` size a Voxel grid and are an **error** with Octree (whose finest cell is root/2^depth and is therefore already determined) -- accepting either would silently ignore one of the two. **The justification for the octree is also the test**: against a uniform grid of the same finest resolution the contour is *the same contour* -- identical facet count and point set -- from far fewer cells. **Both octree traps produce a valid, plausible mesh, so both have a sabotage-verified oracle, and getting either oracle to fire took two attempts.** (1) The field is computed ONCE on the final mesh, because `refine` interpolates `point_data`; the sabotage is computing it one pass early and refining, and it does **not** fire against a *cube* -- a cube's SDF is piecewise linear near its faces, so interpolation reproduces it exactly and the contour is unchanged at 18636 facets either way. The fixture had to become a **sphere**. (2) The band selection is rebuilt from each pass's own output, since `RefineOptions::mCells` are global block-major indices into whatever `refine` last returned; the sabotage is caching the first pass's selection, and it does **not** fire against a cube either (symmetry leaves the stale low indices near the surface anyway) nor against a `2.0 * coarsest` tolerance -- the real maximum was **measured** at 0.59, the bound tightened to 0.75, and `TheBandOracleIsTight` now pins that margin so loosening it silently is a failing test. `detail/grid_lattice.hpp` gained `lattice_resolve` (the six-field resolution hoisted verbatim out of `vox_resolve_lattice`, taking the caller's error prefix so voxelize's messages are unchanged) and `lattice_from_mesh` (the *inverse*: recover origin/spacing/dims from a mesh's geometry). That inverse is what `write_vti` runs on, and it exists because **no format persists arbitrary `field_data`**, so the `sdf:*` header is in-memory-only. Its recovery is **exact rather than a fit** -- `lattice_build_mesh` writes `origin + index*spacing` independently per point, so every point on a plane carries a bit-identical double and a plain sort-and-unique recovers the planes with no tolerance; only the *spacing* needs one (1e-9 relative, since `origin + i*h` gaps differ in the last bits). It checks the **point order** and the **connectivity** too, not just the plane sets: a permuted lattice has identical planes and is a different mesh. A **partial** lattice (`voxelize`'s surface/inside fills, an octree) correctly reports false -- ImageData cannot express a hole, and inventing the missing cells would be a different mesh. `crop_predicate(mesh, array, RefineCompare, value)` is deliberately **general rather than crop-by-surface**: inside/outside composes as `distance_to_surface(..., Center)` then `< 0`, and the same one mode also crops by `quality:*`, a material id or anything `data_calc` produces. `CropMode` is **absent, not ignored** -- bbox/half-space test *points* and need an all/any reduction, a `cell_data` predicate is already one value per cell -- and passing it is an error on every surface. `refine_compare_value` moved out of `refine.cpp`'s anonymous namespace into `refine.hpp` so the two operations share one evaluator; a second transcription would drift exactly on the boundary cases (`==` on a tie, and NaN, where `NaN != 1.0` is *true* in IEEE and a naive `!=` implementation keeps the NaN cell). **Offsetting and inside/outside needed no code at all** and the roadmap item closed by composition: `isosurface` at a non-zero level is the offset (measured on a unit cube: 5.771 / 11.471 / 18.547 at r = 0/0.25/0.5), and `crop(where=)` is the predicate.
  - **Settings pipeline** (`operations/pipeline.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_pipeline.py`, docs [`doc/pipeline.md`](doc/pipeline.md), v9.11.0): a declarative read -> operation chain -> write described by a `settings.json` (`{"Version", "Input", "Operations": [{"Op": ...}], "Output"}`), run by the `pipeline` verb in BOTH CLIs, Python `run_pipeline`, C `mio_pipeline_run_file`/`_json` (+ `mio_pipeline_has_json`), Fortran/Julia/R wrappers over those, WASM `runPipeline`, and the MCP `pipeline` tool. **Two-layer split, and the split is load-bearing**: the typed layer (`PipelineStep{mOp, mParams}` over a `PipelineValue` variant, `validate_pipeline_step`, `apply_pipeline_step`, `run_pipeline_steps`, `run_pipeline`, `pipeline_op_table`, and the `pipeline_encoding/codec/mmap_from_name` option-spelling owners) ALWAYS compiles and is the **single owner of the step dispatch** — `bindings/wasm/js_bindings.cpp`'s `apply_one_op` collapsed into a val->PipelineStep converter over it, so the browser viewer's `convertSurfaceOps` pipeline and a settings.json cannot drift; the JSON front-end (`parse_pipeline_json/_file`, `run_pipeline_json/_file`) uses **nlohmann/json v3.12.0, vendored as a git submodule at `src/cpp/third_party/json` exactly like Eigen** (`MESHIOPLUSPLUS_WITH_JSON`, default ON + `EXISTS` probe → `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_HAS_JSON`; PRIVATE include of `json/single_include`; angle-bracket include behind `#ifdef` in `pipeline.cpp` ONLY, so no installed header names an nlohmann type and the amalgamation + its staleness gate stay submodule-independent) and, when compiled out, throws naming the flag (the `partition_kahip_parts` contract — the STL `cpp-tests` CI leg builds `WITH_JSON=OFF` so the throws-by-name gtest actually fires; conan/vcpkg keep it off like Eigen, wheels/release CLI binaries carry it). **Vocabulary is PascalCase** for ops and keys (`"Op": "ConvertCells"`, `"RemoveOrphans"`; user decision), derived from the wasm camelCase names by capitalizing the first character — the frozen `convertSurfaceOps` contract stays camelCase externally (report counter keys re-cased mechanically back, and its unknown-op error still echoes the CALLER'S spelling — a smoke step pins that); enum *values* keep the lowercase `*_from_name` spellings, and refine's comparator is `Compare`, never `Op`. **Strict parsing**: unknown op/key/top-level key errors by name (excluded multi-mesh ops `Merge`/`Interpolate`/`Split`/`Diff` error pointing at the CLI verb — v1 is a single-mesh chain; `Partition` as a step attaches `partition:part` labels, exactly the viewer's chip); steps are validated BEFORE the input is read. `Input.Options` → `ReadOptions`, `Output` → `WriteOptions` via `registry_write_ex` (unsupported option = error). **Python `run_pipeline` is deliberately a pure-Python twin** dispatching over the public Python API (stdlib json), which is what gives it the per-format Python fallbacks and sdist (no-submodule) support; `_core.run_pipeline_file/_json` bind the C++ engine additionally, `_core.pipeline_op_table` pins the transcribed `_OP_TABLE` (the `refine_mask_table` pattern), and `tests/python/test_pipeline.py::test_cpp_matches_python` pins the two engines' meshes + reports against each other. The report is `{steps: [{op, ...counters}], warnings}` with PascalCase counter keys (`PointsWelded`, `SectionFaces`, ...), doubles throughout. The C ABI carries **JSON text only** (no step struct); a structured report accessor, multi-mesh steps (`Inputs: [paths]`, `Output.Pattern`) and conan/vcpkg shipping the parser via registry `nlohmann_json` are the recorded follow-ups in doc/pipeline.md.
  - **Sequences / transient datasets** (`operations/sequence.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_sequence.py`, docs [`doc/sequences.md`](doc/sequences.md), v9.12.0): treat a *set* of files -- or the steps inside one multi-step file -- as one ordered logical dataset, which is how transient solver output arrives (`out_0000.vtu … out_0500.vtu`) and how most of the 42 formats have to express time. Three shapes: **fan-out** (one multi-step file -> `out_{step}.vtu`), **fan-in** (N files -> one multi-step file, XDMF only), **sequence** (N -> N with the chain applied per step). **It is a DRIVER, not an operation, and that is the load-bearing rule**: it reads/writes through the existing registry and runs chains through the existing typed pipeline layer, so `run_pipeline_steps` stays the single owner of the step dispatch and a sequence document still cannot drift from `convertSurfaceOps`; there is deliberately no second `if (op == ...)` chain. **Ordering is natural-numeric and documented** (`out_9` < `out_10`): digit runs compare numerically **on the digits** (never `stoull`/`int()` -- a 40-digit name must not overflow), non-digit runs as **unsigned char** (plain `char` signedness would reorder UTF-8 paths differently on ARM), a digit run sorts before a non-digit run, and a final tie-break on the **unstripped** strings is what makes the comparator a **strict weak ordering** -- without it `out_1`/`out_01` are mutually not-less yet not equivalent and `std::sort` is UB on a directory mixing padded and unpadded names (brute-forced over all four axioms in `test_sequence.cpp`). An explicit path *list* is a stated order and is not re-sorted; a pattern always is. **Glob lives in the CORE, not Python**, so both CLIs and the C/Fortran/Julia/R surfaces accept the same language -- deliberately just `*` and `?` (no `**`, no `[set]`, directory component literal), narrower than `glob(3)` *and* `fnmatch`, and the Python twin re-implements it rather than delegating precisely so the two cannot accept different things. **Time-value precedence**: explicit list -> the file (a series step, or `field_data["meshio:time"]`, generalizing the length-1 `exodus:time` the Exodus reader/writer already round-trips) -> the **last** digit run of the stem -> the integer index; each entry **reports which source applied**, since "the file said 0.25" and "nothing said anything" are different facts. A single-step file's `meshio:time` needs a real read, so expansion marks it provisionally and the drivers upgrade it from the mesh they were going to read anyway -- no file is read twice. Note `meshio:time` **only survives where the format carries `field_data`** (exodus/gmsh/mdpa; VTU/VTK carry none), so a fan-out to `.vtu` then back recovers the *index*, not the time -- documented, and `Times` on the way back in is the recipe. **"Carries time" is two questions**: the read side is registry-derived (`read_metadata(...).mTimeValues`, XDMF+Exodus today) but **gated first** on a small `seq_format_may_have_steps` set, because `read_metadata` on a format with no native metadata reader is a *full read* and a plain convert must not pay for a probe whose answer is known; the write side has nothing to probe so it is an owned predicate (`xdmf` only) kept honest by a gtest iterating `registry_writers()` and comparing it to whether a real two-step fan-in succeeds. **MED is deliberately in neither list** -- it honours `mTimeStep` but has no metadata reader, so probing costs a full read and still reports one step; it closes for free when `read_med_metadata` fills `mTimeValues`. **The streaming invariant is a contract, not an optimization**: at most one `Mesh` alive in a fan-in/fan-out/per-step run, `read_sequence` is a generator, and `sequence_expand` returns the *plan* (paths/steps/times) never meshes -- pinned by a gtest measuring the peak through the `BufferAllocator` hook and requiring it **O(1) in the step count** (20 vs 40 files, verified to fire by sabotage), and by weak references on the Python side. **A multi-step input aimed at a single-step output fails by name** (naming `{step}` and `--time-step`) rather than writing step 0, which is what `convert series.xdmf out.vtu` silently did before -- the CLI and `run_pipeline` both route such an input to the driver so the refusal happens. `{step}`/`{index}` expansion is a **third, separate** expander: `replace_key`/`partition_replace_part` stay byte-identical (their tests are the guard), and since Python uses `str.format` while C++ uses substring replace a "shared" helper would be two helpers anyway -- the asymmetry is inherited and commented, not resolved. Settings keys (`Mode`, `Input.Pattern`/`Paths`/`Times`/`TimeFrom`, `Parallel`, `Workers`) live in the **JSON front-end only**; one parser builds a `SequencePipeline` and `parse_pipeline_json` projects it down, **refusing a sequence key by name** rather than ignoring it (ignoring would run a transient document as its first step). `Mode` **asserts** the inference rather than selecting it. `Parallel` is a Python-driver process pool, an **error** for a fan-in (an ordered writer cannot take buffered steps without breaking the invariant) and warned-and-serial in C++ (every op already parallelizes internally). **ABI**: the sequence types went into a **new header** rather than growing `Pipeline`, which embeds `PipelineInput` **by value** -- one added member would shift `mSteps`/`mOutput` under a v9.11.0-compiled consumer, a silent Tier A break; those four types were *unpinned* in `test_abi_layout.cpp` until v9.12.0, which is what made growing them look free, and they are pinned now. **WASM has it too**, over MEMFS paths -- the same virtual filesystem `convert`/`runPipeline` already work on, so a page that stages N buffers there gets fan-in/fan-out for free: `sequenceEntries`/`sequenceToTimeseries`/`timeseriesToSequence` bound in `js_bindings.cpp`, forwarded in `index.mjs`, declared in `index.d.ts` and exercised through the wrapper in `smoke.mjs` (whose exhaustive guard grew to 48 names), plus `runPipeline` routing a transient document itself. Its settings converter went through the **same** one-parser-two-projections change as the JSON front-end (`val_to_settings` -> `SettingsDocument`), and `Parallel` is accepted and **ignored with a warning** there since a wasm module has no processes to pool. **The WASM smoke test is what found the routing gap**: a document naming a multi-step input with a plain output and no sequence key took the single-file path and silently wrote step 0 -- in the C++ engine too, which the Python-only refusal test had not covered. The fix is `sequence_input_needs_driver`, one predicate every front-end shares (gated on `seq_format_may_have_steps`, so the probe stays free), pinned by `SequenceJson.APlainDocumentNamingAMultiStepInputStillRefusesToTruncate`. Surfaces: Python `read_sequence`/`write_sequence`/`sequence_entries`/`run_sequence_pipeline` (and `run_pipeline`, which routes); both CLIs' `convert` (quoted glob, `{step}` output, repeated `--input` for a shell-expanded argv, `--sequence`/`--no-sequence`); C `mio_sequence_*` whose `_read` hands back an **owned** mesh (deliberately not `mio_split_result_mesh`'s borrow -- a borrow would force the handle to cache everything it produced, so the C ABI expresses the streaming rule); Fortran/Julia/R handles; and an MCP `sequence` tool with `_resolve_pattern`, which containment-checks a pattern's *directory* before expanding (`_resolve`'s `os.path.isfile` rejects a glob outright, and the directory is the obvious escape). **`TimeSeries` (Python, v9.12.0 follow-up)** closes the one item the roadmap had left open -- "hold a series as one value" with random access -- which the C/Fortran/Julia/R handles already had by construction (`mio_sequence_read(seq, i)`, `read_step(seq, i)`, ...) but Python's `read_sequence` generator could not (exhausted after one pass, no `len()`, no indexing). `TimeSeries(source, **read_kwargs)` holds only the entry *plan* (never a mesh, preserving the streaming invariant), supports `len()`, `series[i]`/`series[-1]`/slicing, repeated iteration, and `.times`/`.paths`/`.entries()` answered from the plan alone; each index access performs exactly one independent read with nothing cached between accesses. A class, so it goes in MCP's `_NOT_TOOLS` like `Mesh`/`Region` -- the `sequence` tool already covers the path-in/path-out cases.
- **Selective reads / metadata** (`src/cpp/include/meshioplusplus/read_options.hpp` + `src/cpp/src/read_options.cpp`, Python `_helpers.read`/`read_metadata`, docs [`doc/selective_read.md`](doc/selective_read.md)): `ReadOptions{mPointsOnly, mMetadataOnly, mDataArrays, mMmap, mTimeStep, mLenient}` is a **defaulted trailing parameter** on the five options-aware readers (`read_vtu`/`read_vtp`/`read_xdmf`/`read_gmsh`/`read_exodus`) — default-constructed reproduces the historical behaviour exactly, which is what let it land without touching a single existing caller. `mDataArrays` is `std::optional<vector<string>>` because `nullopt` (every array) and an empty vector (none) are distinct requests, and the ambiguity would be unfixable at the C ABI. **The default argument lives in the header only** — repeating it on the `.cpp` definition is a hard error that surfaces *only* in the `amalgamation` CI job. `MeshMetadata` + the single shared `metadata_from_mesh(const Mesh&)` back `read_metadata`; it must never copy the mesh (KRATOS `Mesh` is not copy-constructible). The registry gains **sparse** `registry_readers_ex()`/`registry_metadata_readers()` (`unordered_map`, pure keyed lookup) plus `registry_read`/`registry_read_metadata`, which own the fallback so no binding reimplements it — `ReadFn`/`registry_readers()`/`registry_writers()` are deliberately untouched. A native metadata reader may **decline** a construct it cannot describe cheaply (XDMF `Mixed` topology) by throwing `ReadError`; `registry_read_metadata` catches that and falls through to a full read with `mFellBackToFullRead` set, so declining costs a slower answer and never a failed one. Python's `_apply_read_filter` runs after *every* read, which is what makes the options mean the same thing for all ~40 formats. `read_metadata`'s Python fallback lives in `_helpers`, not per-format, since it has no per-format twin. Native metadata paths: VTU/VTP, XDMF, and **Gmsh 4.1** (`read_gmsh_metadata` walks `$Elements` block headers, skipping payloads by exact byte arithmetic for binary and line counts for ascii — ~4x ascii / ~8x binary; **format 2.2 declines and falls back**, since a per-element type means there is no cheap summary to give; since v9.7.0 it also reads `$Entities`/`$PhysicalNames` so a 4.1 summary reports `gmsh:physical` and the named regions). **`mTimeStep`** (v8.6.0) is deliberately *not* a narrowing option and must not be treated like one: `mPointsOnly`/`mDataArrays` can be emulated by `_apply_read_filter` after any read, but no post-filter can recover a step that was never decoded — so `_call_reader` **raises** when a non-default step reaches a reader that does not take the kwarg, rather than silently returning step 0, and `ResolveTimeStep` (0 = first, negative counts from the end) throws naming the available count instead of clamping. Its companion `MeshMetadata::mTimeValues` (always present, empty where there is no time concept) is what makes a step request checkable before it is issued. **Exodus is currently the only reader honouring it** — `read_exodus_metadata` still falls back to a full read (block *types* live in per-variable `elem_type` attributes) and exists purely to report `time_whole`; XDMF temporal collections, CGNS and MED all have a time concept and still take the first step. Note that being in `registry_readers_ex()` means "options-aware", **not** "narrows arrays natively" — exodus is there for `mTimeStep` alone. On the C ABI `time_step` consumed one of `mio_read_opts`'s six `reserved` `int64_t` slots, so `sizeof(mio_read_opts)` and every preceding offset are unchanged (a gtest pins the tail width; the Julia `_check_abi_layout` 80-byte guard also covers it). CLI: `info --fast` (which now also prints `Time steps: N [...]` when a file records more than one), `convert --points-only|--arrays|--time-step=N`, in both CLIs. **`MeshMetadata::mRegions`** (v8.7.0, `RegionSummary{mName, mKind, mDim, mTag, mNumEntries}` — no entries, unlike a real `Region`) is populated by `metadata_from_mesh` from whatever's already on the in-memory mesh, so it costs nothing extra on any fallback path (every format lacking a native metadata reader, plus Exodus, which always falls back) — a native metadata path used to report none, which was never a wrong answer while none of those formats mapped regions — **Gmsh 4.1 broke that assumption in v9.7.0** and `read_gmsh_metadata` therefore parses `$Entities` + `$PhysicalNames` (both small, both ahead of `$Elements`) and counts entries from the block headers, so its summary still agrees with a real read; VTU/VTP/XDMF still report none and still map no regions. Exposed identically on every surface `mTimeValues` reaches (Python `read_metadata(...)["regions"]`, C API `mio_read_metadata_num_regions`/`_region_name`/`_region_info` reusing `mio_region_info`'s shape — hoisted to the top-level typedef block so both the metadata section and the pre-existing `mio_regions` opaque-handle section can see it — Fortran `mio_metadata%regions(:)` as `mio_region_summary`, Julia/R the same shape, WASM `readMetadata(...).regions`, and `info`/`regions` in both CLIs), closing the "enumerating regions costs a full read" gap the v8.1.0 regions work left open.
- **Memory-mapped reading** (`src/cpp/include/meshioplusplus/detail/file_source.hpp`, docs [`doc/mmap.md`](doc/mmap.md)): `FileSource` is RAII whole-file access — POSIX `mmap`+`MADV_SEQUENTIAL`, Windows `MapViewOfFile`, always buffered under Emscripten. `Auto` maps regular files ≥ `mmap_auto_threshold_bytes` (16 MiB; `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_MMAP_THRESHOLD` overrides). Mapping is **advisory** — anything unmappable falls back silently rather than erroring. It is a **memory-footprint** win (one fewer full-file copy, halved peak RSS), not a throughput one; say so rather than overselling. **Lifetime is trivial by construction**: every reader copies into owning storage, so nothing in a returned `Mesh` points into the buffer and `FileSource` is just a function-local — the rule "nothing derived from `View()` may be stored in the mesh" is pinned by a read-then-unlink-then-revalidate test. **A mapped buffer is only NUL-terminated because the kernel zero-fills the final partial page**, which `strtod` (gmsh's `next_double`) relies on; files whose size is an exact page multiple are therefore never mapped, even on an explicit request. Windows needs `WIN32_LEAN_AND_MEAN`+`NOMINMAX` before `<windows.h>` since this header reaches user TUs via the amalgamation. All five whole-file readers are migrated: `gmsh` (which also honours an explicit `ReadOptions::mMmap`), `vtk_read`, `ensight`, `ugrid`'s ASCII branch and `openfoam` — the last gained most, since its `ostringstream`+`.str()` paid for two extra full-file copies. Cursors/parsers that held `const std::string&` now hold `std::string_view`; the ascii OpenFOAM parsers deliberately still take an owned string, because they consume via `istringstream`, which cannot take a view — converting them would only relocate the copy. `ugrid`'s binary branch keeps streaming per-section from an `ifstream` (no whole-file intermediate to map).
- **Compression codecs** (`detail/vtu_binary.hpp`'s `VtkCodec`, docs [`doc/codecs.md`](doc/codecs.md)): zlib (default, always) plus optional **zstd**/**lz4** behind `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_WITH_ZSTD`/`_LZ4` → `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_HAS_*` and `_core.__has_zstd__`/`__has_lz4__`/`__has_zlib__`. Both default **OFF** (unlike HDF5/netCDF/zlib) so a build without them reads and writes exactly what it always did; never probed under Emscripten (no port exists). All codecs share VTU's block framing verbatim (32 KiB blocks) — only the per-block compressor differs, dispatched through `vtk_codec_compress_block`/`_decompress_block`, parallelized with `parallel_for` `grain=1` (**not** `parallel_for_bw`). **`lz4` = real VTK interop** (`vtkLZ4DataCompressor`, LZ4 *raw block* format — the size comes from VTU's header since raw blocks carry none); **`zstd` = documented meshio++ extension** (`vtkZSTDDataCompressor`; VTK ships no ZSTD compressor, so ParaView reports an unknown compressor rather than misreading). The per-codec helpers **always exist and throw at runtime** when compiled out — a link error would break the Python-fallback contract — with messages naming the CMake option, `registry_compiled_out()`-style; availability is checked once at header parse. The pure-Python reference supports both via the `codecs` extra, so the fallback is real; a file needing a codec present in neither is genuinely unreadable (a new failure class, failing by name). `--codec zlib|lz4|zstd` on both CLIs' `compress`, **rejected** for formats with no block codec rather than silently ignored. Conan `with_zstd`/`with_lz4` + vcpkg `zstd`/`lz4`, both off by default.
- **Data operations** (`src/cpp/src/operations/data_*.cpp` + headers, Python `_data_*.py`, docs `doc/data_operations.md` + one page per op): act on `point_data`/`cell_data`/`field_data`, **never** on geometry (points/connectivity/block order come through bit-identical — `tests/cpp/mesh_fixtures.hpp`'s `mt::expect_same_geometry` and `tests/python/helpers_data.py`'s `assert_same_geometry` assert this per op). Shared vocabulary in `operations/data_common.hpp`: `DataLocation{Point,Cell,Field}`, `NanPolicy{Ignore,Replace,Fail}`, the `*_from_name` string parsers (both CLIs and WASM pass enums as strings), and `data_unknown_key_message` (the standard "no X array named 'y' (available: …)" diagnostic). **Cross-cutting rules:** any op writing `cell_data` emits exactly `NumCellBlocks()` arrays (KRATOS materializes per-entity variables from that list) and any op reading one validates `CellDataNumBlocks(name) == NumCellBlocks()`; components = product of trailing dims, 1-D scalars stay 1-D; **non-finite values are always excluded from every reduction**, with `NanPolicy` deciding only what reaches the output (`data_info` never throws — it counts). `point_sets`/`cell_sets` pass through unchanged in the Python shim and never reach the core. The five operations:
  - **Array management** (`data_manage.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_data_manage.py`): `data_manage(mesh, DataManageOptions{keep,drop,rename,ignore_missing})` → `DataManageResult{mMesh, mDropped, mRenamed}`, plus the `data_drop`/`data_keep`/`data_rename` convenience wrappers the flat bindings and CLIs use. Phases apply **keep → drop → rename**; `keep` only affects the locations it mentions (so `data_keep(m, Point, {})` drops all point_data but leaves cell/field alone — implemented as the *complement* via `drop`, not a sentinel key). All validation runs against the input before any cloning. Renaming onto an existing name throws unless that target is itself renamed away or dropped in the same pass, which makes a two-name swap legal. Values/dtypes/shapes are byte-identical passthrough. C API exposes the three primitives only (`mio_data_drop`/`_keep`/`_rename`) — the combined `data_manage` is a **documented flat-ABI gap**.
  - **Location averaging** (`data_average.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_data_average.py`): `point_data_to_cell_data` (each cell = mean over its own nodes; ragged rows use `Row`, polyhedra the *distinct* nodes across faces) and `cell_data_to_point_data` (each point = mean over incident cells, `CellPointWeight::Uniform|Measure`; unavailable measures fall back to weight 1 with one warn per call). Component-wise; output is **always Float64** (a mean is not an integer). A point/cell with no finite contribution yields NaN. **The cell→point accumulation pass is deliberately SERIAL** — it is a scatter and FP addition is not associative, so parallelizing it would make results thread-count-dependent and flake the 4-leg backend matrix; only the final divide is `parallel_for_bw`. Same phase-split philosophy as `surface.cpp`.
  - **Expressions** (`data_calc.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_data_calc.py`): `data_calc(mesh, expr, DataCalcOptions{location,output,overwrite,dtype})`. Hand-written tokenizer + recursive-descent parser — **no external parser lib, no arbitrary-code path**. Grammar: `+ - * /`, unary minus, parens, numeric literals, array names, and `abs`/`sqrt`/`min`/`max`/`norm`. Identifiers admit `:`/`.` after the first char (load-bearing: `gmsh:physical`), backticks quote names with spaces. Parse builds a **flat `vector<CalcNode>` pool** (shareable read-only across `parallel_for`, no per-node allocation); three passes: width inference + name resolution, operand widening to double once per array (`parallel_for_bw`), then the element loop (`parallel_for`) using stack buffers — `DATA_CALC_MAX_COMPONENTS=16` is what makes the loop allocation-free, `DATA_CALC_MAX_DEPTH=64` guards the parser against stack overflow from hostile input reaching it via the C ABI. Scalars broadcast against wider operands; `norm` is always scalar. Literals go through `std::strtod`, **not** `std::from_chars` (whose float overload is a real Emscripten/libc++ hazard) — matching `dex.cpp`/`wkt.cpp`/`nastran.cpp`. Every error is `std::invalid_argument` prefixed `meshio++: data_calc: ` and carries the character position. Division by zero is IEEE inf/NaN, not an error. Cell location evaluates **once per block**.
  - **Value conditioning** (`data_condition.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_data_condition.py`): `data_condition(mesh, DataConditionOptions{location,names,mode,scope,lo,hi,nan_policy,nan_replacement,suffix,preserve_dtype})` with `ConditionMode{Clamp,Normalize,Standardize}` and `ConditionScope{Component,Magnitude}` (magnitude rescales whole rows, preserving direction). For `cell_data` the statistics are computed **jointly across all blocks** before one transform is applied to each. Degenerate reductions are handled, not NaN'd: constant array under Normalize → fill `lo` + warn; zero stddev under Standardize → fill 0 + warn. `Clamp` + `preserve_dtype` keeps the input dtype; Normalize/Standardize force Float64. `suffix` writes to a new name instead of in place.
  - **Data summary** (`data_info.{hpp,cpp}`, Python `_data_info.py`): `data_info(mesh)` → `DataInfoReport{mArrays, mNumPointData/CellData/FieldData}` of `DataArrayInfo` (location, name, dtype, shape, blocks, entries, components, values, min/max/mean whole-array *and* per component, NaN/inf/finite counts, `mInconsistentBlocks`), plus `data_array_info(mesh, loc, name)`. Read-only; the data-side complement to the topological `info` and the geometric `compute_stats`. **Never throws on non-finite values** and reports (rather than throws on) a cell_data array whose blocks disagree in component count. C/Fortran use an **opaque `mio_data_info` handle** (`_create`/`_count`/`_name`/`_entry`/`_component`/`_free`) since the report is variable-length — modelled on `mio_split_result`.
- **SVG/TikZ 3D rendering** (`src/cpp/include/meshioplusplus/detail/projection.hpp`, Python twin `src/python/meshioplusplus/_projection.py` — arithmetic kept expression-for-expression identical for TikZ byte-parity): non-flat 3D input (any |z| > 1e-14) takes a projected path — skin-extract volume cells (linearized), orthographic camera from `azimuth`/`elevation`/`roll` degrees (defaults 45/atan(1/√2)/0 = classic CAD isometric, `w ∝ (1,1,1)`), painter's-algorithm stable sort on face-centroid depth, then the existing path/`\draw` emission. Flat input keeps the legacy 2D code path physically untouched (byte-identical output). Camera args are appended **after** the existing writer params in the C++ signatures, pybind bindings, and shims — the shims pass positionally, order is load-bearing. `tests/python/test_tikz.py` asserts C++/Python byte-identity on the 3D path too; the FP-parity rules: cast coordinates to double before arithmetic, left-to-right multiply-adds, `std::stable_sort`/`np.argsort(kind="stable")`. `ProjectedFace` also carries `mSourceCell` (twin: `_projection.py`'s faces are 3-tuples `(nodes, is_line, source_cell)`) — a **field on the sorted element, not a parallel array**, since `stable_sort` permutes `mFaces` and a side array would have to be permuted in lockstep; it counts block-major over **every** block including skipped ones, matching `surface:parent_cell` so the two indices compose. Three consumers unpack that tuple: `_svg.py`, `_tikz.py` and **`doc/logo/gen_logo_tikz.py`** (which bypasses the writers — re-run it and `git diff --exit-code doc/logo/` after touching projection).
- **SVG/TikZ data-driven coloring** (`detail/face_color.{hpp,cpp}` + Python twin `src/python/meshioplusplus/_facecolor.py`, over `detail/colormap.{hpp,cpp}` + `_colormap.py`; docs [`doc/formats/svg.md`](doc/formats/svg.md#data-driven-colouring)): `color_by`/`component`/`cmap`/`vmin`/`vmax`/`nan_color`/`colorbar` appended **after the camera args** in `write_svg`/`write_tikz`, the pybind bindings and the shims (which pass **positionally** — order is load-bearing). `color_by` unset ⇒ output byte-identical to before, flat 2D path physically untouched. Name resolves in `point_data` first then `cell_data`; point data = mean of a face's corner values (summed left-to-right then divided once — **never `np.mean`**, whose pairwise summation rounds differently), cell data = its owning cell's value, reached through `surface:parent_cell` when a skin was extracted (the writers request provenance **only** when `color_by` actually names a cell_data array). Multi-component → `component` or magnitude (sequential sum-of-squares + one `sqrt`, never `np.linalg.norm`). Auto range is over the **drawn faces**, not the whole array (differs from ParaView, deliberately: the visible figure then spans the whole colorbar, and the reduction stays serial so there is no parallel-chunk FP-parity question); `FiniteStats` seed-on-first-finite semantics, non-finite excluded and drawn in `nan_color`; `vmin == vmax` ⇒ 0.5, `vmin > vmax` throws. **Colormaps are 256-entry uint8 LUTs, not interpolated control points** — that is what makes byte parity structural rather than argued: the entire FP surface of the color path is `static_cast<int>(t * 255.0 + 0.5)` / `int(t * 255.0 + 0.5)`, provably identical because both truncate and no tie-breaking is involved. **Never "improve" that to `std::lround`/`round()`** — they disagree on the exact ties `t = k/510` produces. The C++ side is **declaration/definition-split like `projection.hpp`**: `colormap.hpp` declares `Rgb`/`kColormapSize`/the three functions only, while the table data (file-private, anonymous-namespaced) and function bodies live in `colormap.cpp` — so including the header doesn't pull three 768-byte array literals into every TU that colours a face. All three files (`colormap.hpp`, `colormap.cpp`, `_colormap.py`) are emitted by `tools/gen_colormaps.py` (matplotlib needed only to regenerate) and pinned against each other by `tests/python/test_colormap.py` via the `_core.colormap_table` export. The two writers share `color_faces_flat` (the flat path's line/triangle/quad enumeration, one rule for both) and `color_faces_from_projection`; only the format vocabulary (`svg_color_hex` → `#rrggbb`, `tikz_color_rgb` → `{rgb,255:red,R;green,G;blue,B}` — braces mandatory or the commas split the option list) stays per-writer. The SVG colorbar widens **only the viewBox** (scaling factor, stroke width and every mesh coordinate come from the unmodified width) and emits `<rect>`, not `<path>`, so the document `path {…}` rule does not stroke it; TikZ's is a pure append. **Coloring is a documented gap on the flat bindings** — `registry.cpp`'s `(path, mesh)` writer lambdas cannot carry params, so C/Fortran/WASM keep the fixed default styling (do NOT thread it through the registry). CLI: `convert --color-by NAME [--component --cmap --vmin --vmax --nan-color --colorbar]` in **both** CLIs, rejected for non-svg/tikz targets; the native one uses a `write_colored_variant` registry bypass modelled on `write_binary_variant`. `extract_skin`+`extract_surface` gained no public parameter for this — the shared impl was lifted to `detail::surface_extract(mesh, forceFaceMode, linearize, recordParentIds, opName)` (declared in `operations/surface.hpp`), because the two public functions are flat-bound in three places and no binding consumer needs linearize+provenance together; `_skin.py::_extract_skin_py` gained the matching `record_parent_ids`.
- **Doc figures** (`tools/gen_doc_images.py`): regenerates the committed static doc/README figures from `example/example.msh` — coloured SVGs through the writer above (`doc/public/images/color_by_*.svg`) and `screenshot()` PNGs through the Polyscope extra. Canonical static figures may come from this script; it **complements, does not replace**, the PyVista notebook convention above (which still owns the executable, re-executed-with-outputs demos). It crops the bracket to ~1.1k skin facets deliberately: the full 298k-cell mesh yields a 4.5 MB SVG whose elements are sub-pixel. Smoke-tested by `tests/python/test_gen_doc_images.py`, which redirects the output dirs and never writes into `doc/`.
- **Container convention**: use `std::unordered_map`/`std::unordered_set` for name/id lookup and dedup tables (O(1), order-irrelevant) — the default. Reach for `std::map`/`std::set` **only** when sorted iteration order is itself relied on (see `su2` `tag_counts`, `openfoam` `by_n`, gmsh's `node_data`/`elem_data` staging locals). The mesh data maps' name order **is** observable — it drives Python dict key order and the on-disk field/variable order of writers (VTU, VTK, XDMF, Exodus, Tecplot, HMF, PLY, AVS-UCD, tetgen, h5m) plus "first int field" selectors (medit, ugrid, su2, netgen, avsucd). The uniform API bakes that guarantee in: `PointDataNames()`/`CellDataNames()`/`FieldDataNames()` **always return sorted names** on every backend — memoized since v9.0.0 rather than re-sorted per call, which carries one **discipline**: the MESHIO backend's three data maps are public struct members and `bindings/python/np_conversions.hpp` inserts into them directly (the one sanctioned uniform-API exception), so anything bypassing `AddPointData`/`AddCellData`/`AppendCellData`/`AddFieldData` **must** call `Mesh::InvalidateNameCaches()` or the cache goes stale and silently reorders on-disk fields; `detail::NamedItems` (NATIVE/KRATOS) is sound by construction because all mutation is encapsulated (the former `detail::sorted_keys` idiom, now behind the accessors; `map_order.hpp` remains for non-Mesh maps and the pybind layer), so output stays byte-identical across backends.
- **C++ naming convention**: Kratos Multiphysics style — classes/structs/enums and methods (member functions) are `PascalCase` (e.g. `Mesh::NumPoints()`, `CellBlock::IsRagged()`); member variables are `m`-prefixed `PascalCase` (e.g. `Mesh::mPoints`, `CellBlock::mData`); reference parameters are `r`-prefixed (`const std::string& rPath`) and pointer parameters `p`-prefixed (`const int* pPerm`) — forwarding references (`F&&`) and rvalue-reference/move parameters are exempt; free functions, local variables, and lambda parameters stay `snake_case` (e.g. `read_vtu`, `parallel_for`, `detail::sorted_keys`). File names stay `snake_case`. Brace style is K&R (opening brace on the same line) at 4-space indentation — codified in the repo-root `.clang-format`, run via `clang-format -i` over `src/cpp/`, `bindings/`, `bindings/wasm/` (never `src/cpp/third_party`). None of this renames the pybind11/embind **string literals** that define the Python/JS-facing API (`.def("...")`, `.attr("...")`, `emscripten::function("...", ...)`) — those stay exactly as they are; only the C++ symbols on the other side of each binding call follow this convention.
- **Optional deps** (`CMakeLists.txt`): `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_WITH_HDF5`/`_NETCDF`/`_ZLIB`/`_ZSTD`/`_LZ4`/`_KAHIP`/`_EIGEN` options → `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_HAS_*` compile definitions and `_core.__has_hdf5__`/`__has_netcdf__`/`__has_kahip__` flags. **`MESHIOPLUSPLUS_ZLIB_STATIC`** (v10.20.0, OFF by default, orthogonal to `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_STATIC_RUNTIME`) swaps `find_package(ZLIB)` for a `FetchContent`-vendored, SHA256-pinned static zlib 1.3.1 build, so a portable artifact gets zlib (and so `gid`) without a dynamic libz dependency — see the `gid` note below and `cli.yml`/`wheels.yml`, the only two consumers. **KaHIP** (partition's quality backend; MIT like meshio++, so no licence implication; OFF by default) is bring-your-own: located by the repo's first Find module, `cmake/FindKaHIP.cmake` (ported from the Kratos KaHIPApplication, ParHIP section dropped — only the serial `kaffpa` interface is linked, never ParHIP, hence **no MPI**), honouring `KAHIP_ROOT`/`KAHIP_DIR` + pkg-config; `CMAKE_MODULE_PATH` is appended only inside the `WITH_KAHIP AND NOT EMSCRIPTEN` branch. Never vendored, never auto-downloaded, no KaHIP LICENSE copy in-tree (the Kratos reviewer's requirement); CI source-builds v3.25 into a cached prefix (the dedicated `kahip` job). `#ifdef`-guarded sources become empty TUs (or fall back to a plain loop) when off. Shared HDF5 helpers in `src/cpp/include/meshioplusplus/detail/hdf5_util.hpp`. **Eigen** is vendored as a git submodule at `src/cpp/third_party/eigen` (header-only; run `git submodule update --init` for source builds) and used for the MED Fortran↔C transpose (`med.cpp`, guarded by `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_HAS_EIGEN`); when the submodule is absent the code uses a hand-written transpose, so sdists without submodules still build. **nlohmann/json** (v9.11.0) is vendored the same way at `src/cpp/third_party/json`, pinned to v3.12.0 (`MESHIOPLUSPLUS_WITH_JSON`, default ON + `EXISTS` probe → `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_HAS_JSON`, PRIVATE `single_include`), used ONLY by `operations/pipeline.cpp`'s JSON front-end; absent, the typed pipeline layer still compiles and the parse entry points throw naming the flag.
- **C++ standard: C++20** (pinned in `CMakeLists.txt` twice: `CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD` + `target_compile_features`). macOS needs deployment target ≥ 13.3 for `std::format` (set in `ci.yml`/`wheels.yml`).
- **Logging** (`src/cpp/include/meshioplusplus/log.hpp`): `meshioplusplus::log::debug/info/warn/error("fmt {}", …)` — `std::format` (compile-time-checked) + `std::source_location`, thread-safe via `std::osyncstream`, filtered at runtime by the `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_LOG_LEVEL` env var (`debug|info|warn|error|off`, default `warn`). No printf/cerr elsewhere; errors remain exceptions.
- **Parallelism** (`src/cpp/include/meshioplusplus/parallel.hpp`): `meshioplusplus::parallel_for(n, f, grain=2048, max_threads=0)` with a compile-time backend selected by `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_PARALLEL_BACKEND` = `AUTO|SEQ|STL|OPENMP|TBB|KOKKOS`. **Default is `AUTO`**, which prefers **OpenMP** (portable: libgomp on manylinux, MSVC built-in, libomp on macOS; needs no TBB), else the STL(+TBB) probe, else SEQ. `_core.__parallel_backend__` reports the active backend from Python (STL-without-TBB is effectively sequential — that's why AUTO avoids it). **KOKKOS** (v8.5.0) is bring-your-own like KaHIP — never picked by AUTO, located via `find_package(Kokkos CONFIG)` (`Kokkos_DIR`), not under Emscripten, CI-covered by the dedicated `kokkos` job (cached source build of the pinned 4.5.01 **with `-DCMAKE_POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE=ON`** — required because the job also builds `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_BUILD_C_API=ON`, and linking Kokkos's non-PIC static archives into the shared `libmeshioplusplus.so` fails with `relocation R_X86_64_TPOFF32 ... can not be used when making a shared object`, bit the job once, fixed by rebuilding with PIC and bumping the `actions/cache` key — full ctest + a 2-thread-pool rerun) — and runs on `Kokkos::DefaultHostExecutionSpace` **deliberately**: every loop body captures host pointers and some call host-only libraries (zlib), so device dispatch is not meaningful; GPU data movement is the DLPack/CuPy handoff's job. It lazy-initializes Kokkos only if the embedding app hasn't (then finalizes via `std::atexit`), honours `parallel_for_bw`'s 4-thread cap by range-partitioning into ≤ max_threads chunks (Kokkos has no per-call cap), and needs plain `[&]` lambdas only (host space ⇒ no `KOKKOS_LAMBDA`); `Kokkos::is_finalized()` is version-guarded (`KOKKOS_VERSION >= 30700`). Adding a backend (HPX, …) = one CMake branch + one `#elif` in `parallel.hpp` (+ the guarded include block + `parallel_backend_name()`). Iterations must be independent; the first exception is captured and rethrown after the join; `n <= grain` runs sequentially. **Two flavors:** `parallel_for` (all cores — for compute-bound loops: zlib/base64 in `detail/vtu_binary.hpp`, ASCII formatting) and **`parallel_for_bw`** (thread-capped, `parallel_bandwidth_threads=4` — for memory-bandwidth-bound loops: byte-swap/transpose/gather, which *regress* past ~4 threads because they saturate memory bandwidth). Use `parallel_for_bw` for byte I/O and index gathers; `parallel_for` only when the per-element work is real compute. The OpenMP backend uses `schedule(dynamic, grain/4)` — on hybrid CPUs (P+E cores) a static split makes E-cores stragglers; the chunk honours explicitly coarse grains (the VTU zlib loop passes `grain=1` so each block dispatches individually — do NOT floor the chunk above the grain). Hoist per-element dtype switches with `detail::dispatch_dtype(dt, []<class T>(){…})` (`value_io.hpp`) before parallelizing. **Binary I/O rule:** never write per-element to a `std::ostream` (byte-at-a-time `os.put`/tiny `os.write` was the pre-optimization VTK/Gmsh bottleneck) — build one pre-sized buffer (fused gather+byteswap where possible) and emit it with a single `os.write`; read binary sections straight into the destination array (or bulk seek+read, never `istreambuf_iterator`). Endianness conversion goes through `detail/byteswap.hpp` (`bswap_copy`/`bswap_inplace`, single-instruction intrinsics) — never a per-byte reversal loop.

**Format registration** (`src/python/meshioplusplus/_helpers.py`): each format's `__init__.py` calls `register_format(name, extensions, reader, {name: writer})`. `read`/`write` auto-detect from extension. Modules are imported (and thus registered) in `src/python/meshioplusplus/__init__.py`.

**Format module layout** (the shim pattern, e.g. `src/python/meshioplusplus/su2/`):
- `__init__.py` — imports `_core`, defines `read`/`write` that **try the C++ function and fall back** to the Python reference on any exception, then `register_format(...)`.
- `_<format>.py` — the pure-Python reference reader/writer.
- Multi-version formats (Gmsh, VTK) dispatch to version submodules.

**Cell type naming**: meshio++ uses its own names (`triangle`, `tetra10`, `hexahedron20`, …). Each format maps between its native names and meshio++'s (e.g. `gmsh/common.py`).

**Tests** (`tests/python/`): `helpers.py` has `Mesh` fixtures + `write_read()` (the round-trip pattern). Per-format `test_<format>.py` parametrize over meshes. `tests/python/meshes/` and `tests/python/input/` hold read-only reference files (Git-LFS). HDF5/netCDF suites use `pytest.importorskip`.

**CLI** (`src/python/meshioplusplus/_cli/`): `convert` (now also `--points-only`/`--arrays a,b`, plus the v9.12.0 sequence forms -- a quoted glob or repeated `--input` fans a set of files in, an `out_{step}.vtu` output fans one multi-step file out), `info` (now also `--fast`), `ascii`, `binary`, `compress` (now also `--codec zlib|lz4|zstd`, rejected where no block codec exists), `decompress`, plus the operation verbs `quality`, `extract-surface`, `reorder`, `diff`, `merge`, `transform`, `clean`, `crop`, `slice`, `isosurface`, `split`, `convert-cells`, `refine`, `partition`, `smooth`, `interpolate`, `stats`, `view`, `screenshot`, registered in `_main.py`. **`data` is the repo's first *nested* subcommand** (`_cli/_data.py`, fourteen sub-verbs: `info`/`rename`/`drop`/`keep`/`to-cell`/`to-point`/`calc`/`clamp`/`normalize` over the five data operations, plus `gradient`, `estimate-error` and `integrate` — **mesh** operations grouped here because that is where a user looks for them, the deliberate exceptions to this group's "never touches geometry" rule (`estimate-error` composes `gradient` itself, closing the adaptive-refinement loop; `integrate` reads the same cell measures `gradient`'s Green-Gauss method does, to weight its totals) — plus `export`/`export-dataset` → Parquet/zarr/hdf5 via `_interop`, Python-only and whose pyarrow/zarr imports stay inside the handler) — it needs no change to `_main.py`'s dispatch because `set_defaults(func=...)` on the *inner* parser overrides the outer one, so the existing `args.func(args)` still reaches the right handler. **Colon rule** (implemented identically in `_cli/_data.py`, `src/cpp/cli/main.cpp` and `doc/cli.md`, because data names routinely contain colons): `data rename --point OLD:NEW` splits on the **last** colon, `data calc --point "NAME = EXPR"` on the **first** `=`; `drop`/`keep` take comma-separated lists with no location prefix. **`dataset` is the second nested group** (`_cli/_dataset.py`, v9.28.0: `add`/`list`/`split`/`tag`/`annotate` over the dataset manifests — same wiring, Python CLI only; see the dataset-manifests entry above).

**CI** (`.github/workflows/`): `ci.yml` (3-OS Python test matrix — Linux/macOS build native paths, Windows builds them off and uses Python fallbacks — plus a `cpp-tests` matrix running the gtest suite per mesh backend: MESHIO×STL, MESHIO×OPENMP, NATIVE×OPENMP, KRATOS×OPENMP, the non-MESHIO legs with `BUILD_PYTHON=OFF`; every leg also builds with `BUILD_C_API=ON`/`BUILD_FORTRAN=ON` so the C-API gtests and the `fortran_api` ctest run per backend; the NATIVE leg additionally does a `cmake --install` + external-consumer smoke test — gcc via pkg-config, gfortran against the installed `.mod`, and a `find_package` mini-project, compiling `doc/examples/*`; the KRATOS leg also compile-checks `kratos_bridge.hpp` against real CoSimIO headers cloned at CI time; plus a dedicated `kahip` job — KaHIP v3.25 source-built from the pinned tag with `PARHIP=OFF -DNOMPI=ON` into an `actions/cache`'d prefix (no Ubuntu package exists; meshio++'s CMake never downloads anything; `NOMPI=ON` is required on a runner with no MPI installed — KaHIP's own CMakeLists.txt does an unconditional `find_package(MPI REQUIRED)` unless it's set, since `kaffpaE` and not only ParHIP depends on MPI, even though meshio++ only ever links the serial `kaffpa` interface), then the gtest partition/C-API suites and `tests/python/test_partition.py` run with the C++ KaHIP backend, and once more against a KaHIP-less `_core` + the `kahip` PyPI wheel to cover the pure-Python quality path; the default legs stay KaHIP-off and exercise SFC + the named not-built error; plus an `interop` job installing `.[interop]` — PyVista/trimesh/pyarrow, all pip-friendly — and running `tests/python/test_interop.py`, while the default `test` matrix keeps exercising that file's pure-payload half with none of them installed; plus an `mcp` job installing `.[mcp]` and running `tests/python/test_mcp.py` — the default `test` matrix likewise keeps exercising that file's pure half (payload layer, sandbox, parity guard) with the `mcp` SDK absent, including on Pythons below the SDK's own 3.10 floor), `wheels.yml` (cibuildwheel with native paths off + PyPI trusted publishing on `v*` tags; the Linux matrix legs are `ubuntu-latest` (x86_64) and `ubuntu-24.04-arm` (arm64) — GitHub's free-for-public-repos hosted arm64 runner, native build with no QEMU/binfmt setup, since cibuildwheel's `CIBW_ARCHS` defaults to the runner's own arch and `CIBW_MANYLINUX_AARCH64_IMAGE` was already pinned), `packages.yml` (Conan recipe + vcpkg overlay validation on PRs; full vcpkg port build on `v*` tags; the Conan job's matrix crosses `{fallback, full}` option sets with `{x86_64 (ubuntu-latest), arm64 (ubuntu-24.04-arm)}` via `include` — see the note below), `cli.yml` (builds the standalone native CLI binary on PRs touching `src/cpp/`/`CMakeLists.txt`; on `v*` tags builds and attaches one statically-linked binary per supported OS/arch — Linux x86_64 and Linux arm64 (`ubuntu-24.04-arm`, native, no emulation) both in the matching `manylinux_2_34` container with gcc-toolset-13 + `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_STATIC_RUNTIME=ON`, verified to have no `libstdc++`/`libgcc_s` dependency; macOS universal (x86_64+arm64 via `CMAKE_OSX_ARCHITECTURES`); Windows x86_64 with the MSVC static CRT (`/MT`) — to the tag's GitHub Release, mirroring `packages.yml`'s release job. All legs build with `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_WITH_HDF5/NETCDF=OFF` and `PARALLEL_BACKEND=SEQ` for a dependency-free single-file binary — same portability trade-off as the manylinux wheel leg; users needing those need `build/configure.sh --cli` from source. zlib is the one exception since v10.20.0: `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_WITH_ZLIB=ON` + `MESHIOPLUSPLUS_ZLIB_STATIC=ON` vendors a pinned static zlib (see the `gid` note below) so the GiD postprocess writer ships here too with no new runtime dependency — verified alongside the existing libstdc++/libgcc_s check), `docs.yml` (VitePress → GitHub Pages).

## Adding a new format

1. Create `src/python/meshioplusplus/<format>/_<format>.py` (Python reference `read`/`write`) and `__init__.py` (shim + `register_format`). `register_format` may also live directly in `_<format>.py` for a Python-only format with no shim to wrap (e.g. `neuroglancer`, `ansysInp`, `openfoam`); move it into `__init__.py` if a shim is added later (`mdpa` did in v8.8.0).
2. Add the C++ implementation: `src/cpp/src/formats/<format>.cpp` + `src/cpp/include/meshioplusplus/formats/<format>.hpp`, bind it in `bindings/python/_core.cpp`, register it in the shared dispatch registry (`src/cpp/src/registry.cpp`: reader/writer entry + extension default — that's what makes it reachable from WASM and the C/Fortran API), and switch `__init__.py` to the try-C++/Python-fallback shim. New `.cpp` files are auto-globbed by CMake.
